## 2026-08-03 — Delivery Model: turnstile carousel replaced with a static full-width step rail

**Task:** replace the auto-rotating turnstile carousel (one of six steps visible at a time, prev/center/next peek, "STEP X OF 6" indicator) with a rail that shows all six steps at once — six columns on desktop, a vertical timeline on mobile.

**Built:** `front-page.php`'s Delivery Model section is now a single `<ol class="delivery-rail">` looping the same `delivery_model_steps` ACF repeater (unchanged options page/source) into six `<li class="delivery-step">` rows — no desktop/mobile markup duplication, a `@media (min-width: 768px)` block in `src/tailwind.css` flips the same list from a vertical timeline (number + connector line on the left, copy on the right) to six flex columns in a row with a horizontal `::before` connector line behind the numbered circles. Only the first step (`.delivery-step--active`) gets the glass treatment (`rgba(255,255,255,.08)` + `blur(8px)`, with an `@supports not (backdrop-filter)` solid fallback matching the existing wave-4 glass pattern); the other five are plain `rgba(255,255,255,.15)`-outlined cards, per spec ("glass as a highlight on the active step only," not the whole component).

**Removed:** `initDeliveryTurnstile()` and its call (`assets/js/main.js`) — nothing here auto-advances, so no timer/IntersectionObserver/swipe-gesture logic is needed. All `.turnstile-card`/`.turnstile-viewport`/`.turnstile-arrow`/`.delivery-progress-dot` CSS and markup (prev/next/hidden peek states, the wave-4 glass rule that targeted `.turnstile-card.is-center .turnstile-card-full`) is gone. `.products-compact-*` (the separate "Meet our products" carousel, same file) was untouched — only its stale comments referencing the deleted turnstile as a point of comparison were reworded.

**Content:** step 04/05/06 names updated from the placeholder set (Handover/Manage/SLA) to the client-provided set (Integrate/Support/Optimize) per the brief's table; step 02/03/04/05/06 descriptions updated to match. Updated both `acf-json/group_13112f7e3503.json`'s `default_value` and the live `options_delivery_model_steps_*` DB rows (ACF repeater sub-fields are stored as individual options on an options page, not inline in the parent value) so the JSON default and the actual rendered content stay in sync. Step 01 (Supply) and its description were already correct — untouched.

**Verification:** `php -l` clean, `npm run build` (Tailwind) clean, confirmed via `grep` that no `turnstile-card` selectors survive in the compiled `assets/css/style.css`. Screenshots via `scripts/screenshot.js` at 1440px and 375px confirmed: desktop shows all six columns with the horizontal connector line and step 01's glass highlight; mobile collapses into the vertical timeline with the vertical connector line. Cleared `temporary-screenshots/` after review per the standing rule.

## 2026-07-23 — Homepage "Partners, not vendors" teaser (links to the About page's full section)

**Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md in full first, per instruction.** Task: add a short homepage teaser for the About page's "Partners, not vendors" trust section (4-flip-card grid, `page-about.php`) — explicitly NOT a duplicate of that section, just a lightweight headline + one line + link pointing back to it.

**Placement: a new, separate, compact section directly ABOVE "Why Choose ITOI"** (`front-page.php`, between the traffic-demo widget and the Why-Choose teal-900 section), not integrated as an intro line inside Why-Choose itself. Reasoning: both sections are trust/differentiation framing ("we're partners, not a vendor" → "here's why teams choose us"), so placing the teaser immediately before reads as a natural one-line lead-in rather than a non-sequitur. Kept it as its own bounded section (plain white background, ~36px vertical padding vs. the surrounding sections' 70–90px) rather than folding it inside the teal-900 Why-Choose block, specifically so it can't visually compete with that section's own centered white H2 for weight — a separate, distinctly smaller block reads unambiguously as secondary; merging it into the same colored block would have made it read as part of the same statement instead of a quick pointer to somewhere else.

**ACF: reused the existing field, added exactly one new field to the same group — no new options page, no duplicate group.** The "Partners, Not Vendors" options-page group (`acf-json/group_7f2a4c9e01d3.json`, built in an earlier session for the About page section) already had `partners_headline` ("Partners, not vendors.") and `partners_intro` (the full multi-sentence paragraph). The homepage teaser's headline reuses `partners_headline` directly and verbatim — same field, same value, can never drift out of sync by construction. The one-sentence supporting line could *not* reuse `partners_intro` as-is: the task explicitly wants a condensed one-liner, not the full paragraph, and reusing the long paragraph on the homepage would have made this "a second full section" rather than a teaser (the thing the task explicitly said not to build). Added one new field, `partners_teaser_line` (plain text, default value = the exact condensed sentence given in the task), to this *same* group/options page rather than creating a second "Partners Teaser" group — so all of this section's copy (About page and homepage alike) is still edited from the one options page in wp-admin.

**ACF options-page gotcha hit again, same as previous sessions' notes — fixed the same way.** A brand-new field with only a `default_value` set doesn't resolve via `get_field()`-by-name on an options page until a value is actually saved once (confirmed via `wp eval`: returned `NULL`, not the default). Seeded it directly: `update_field('partners_teaser_line', <default>, 'option')` via WP-CLI `eval`. Confirmed resolves correctly afterward.

**Anchor target added to the About page, since the task asked for a direct section link where possible.** `page-about.php`'s Partners section had no `id` before this. Added `id="partners-not-vendors"` plus `scroll-mt-[88px]` (72px sticky header height + a small gap, same 72px figure the Delivery Model's earlier pinned-scroll mechanic used for the same reason) so `/about/#partners-not-vendors` lands the section cleanly below the sticky header rather than half-hidden under it. Confirmed via Puppeteer: after following the teaser's link, the target section's bounding rect sits right at ~89px from the viewport top — i.e., just clear of the header, not underneath it.

**Design specifics:** plain text block, no card/border/background box — headline (`text-[18px] font-extrabold text-ink`) and supporting line (`text-[14.5px] text-text-muted`) inline on desktop, stacked on mobile; link styled as plain underlined text (`text-ink underline underline-offset-4`, matching the existing "Case study →"/homepage-grid link convention already used elsewhere in this file, e.g. line ~365), not a pill button — deliberately quieter than the hero/CTA pill buttons so it doesn't read as a second call-to-action competing with "Get demo." No signature-navy anywhere in it (checked against the hard rule — this isn't one of the four permitted signature-layer spots).

**Verification:** `php -l` clean on both templates; `npm run build` clean. Puppeteer (`scripts/_partners-teaser-verify-temp.js`, kept per convention): confirmed the teaser renders with the correct reused headline + condensed line + link at both 1440px and 375px, confirmed the link's actual `href` is `/about/#partners-not-vendors`, and confirmed following it lands on the About page with the target section positioned correctly relative to the sticky header. `axe-core` via Puppeteer: 0 violations on both `/` and `/about/` after this change (ran both since this task touched both templates). Screenshots (`partners-teaser-1440`, `partners-teaser-375`, `partners-teaser-link-destination`) confirmed the teaser sits correctly next to Why Choose ITOI at both breakpoints and that the link destination is correct — cleared from `temporary-screenshots/` at session end per the standing rule, content already described here.

## 2026-07-23 — Delivery Model: pinned scroll-stepper replaced with a turnstile auto-rotating carousel

**Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md in full first, per instruction.** Task: replace the Delivery Model section's interaction entirely — whatever scroll-linked mechanic was currently live (this session found the PINNED, one-step-at-a-time scroll-driven slideshow, itself a same-day rebuild of an earlier progressive-reveal list) — with a genuinely different, non-scroll-linked mechanic: a turnstile-style carousel showing prev/center/next cards, auto-advancing on a timer.

**Built:** `front-page.php`'s Delivery Model section now renders all 6 `.turnstile-card` elements from the `delivery_model_steps` repeater (unchanged ACF source/options page) inside a `.turnstile-viewport`. `initDeliveryTurnstile()` (`assets/js/main.js`) gives each card a signed "shortest path" offset from the current index (wrapping at 6) every time the index changes, and CSS state classes (`.is-center`/`.is-prev`/`.is-next`/`.is-hidden`) do the rest — position, opacity, filter, box-shadow are pure functions of that one offset. This is what lets step 6 loop back to step 1 with no jump-cut and lets the 6 progress dots jump to any step directly (recomputes all 6 offsets from scratch, same as a +1/-1 step).

- Auto-advance: `setInterval` every 4.5s, purely time-based — never reads scroll position. An `IntersectionObserver` on the section (threshold 0.3) only starts/stops that timer as the section enters/leaves the viewport, so it doesn't run while off-screen; it does not drive the rotation itself.
- Pause/resume: hover and touchstart both stop the timer immediately; mouseleave/touchend schedule a resume 4s later (`RESUME_MS`), not instantly, per spec.
- Manual nav: clicking a side card, an arrow button (`#deliveryPrevBtn`/`#deliveryNextBtn`, same circular-pill convention as the Industries carousel's arrows), a dot, or swiping (mobile, since side cards are hidden there — see below) all call the same `goTo()`, which resets the auto-advance timer.
- `prefers-reduced-motion`: the timer is simply never started (checked once via the file's existing shared `reduceMotion` flag); step 1 renders as a static center card; every manual control stays fully wired regardless, per the explicit "manual clicks are fine, only automatic motion stops" instruction.

**Color choice for the active/center card: `--ink` (near-black), not `--teal-900`.** Both were offered as options. Chose `--ink` because (a) `--teal-900` is the "Why choose ITOI" section's own identity a few sections below on the same page — reusing it here would blur that section's distinctiveness rather than the Delivery Model having its own visual identity, and (b) `--ink` is already the site's general-purpose "serious/emphasis" dark, consistent with the primary-CTA black elsewhere, without colliding with any of the two reserved signature-navy (`--signature`) roles. Confirmed zero `--signature` usage anywhere in the new component — the eyebrow dot above the headline was already using it before this task and is unchanged, unrelated to the carousel itself.

**Mobile fallback — used, not just proposed.** At the peek-carousel's percentage-based widths, side cards became illegibly thin slivers below ~768px, so under that breakpoint the side cards are hidden entirely (`display:none`) and the center card goes full-width with `height:auto` (content-sized, not the desktop fixed clamp height) — dots + arrow buttons (kept everywhere, per the explicit "include arrows regardless" instruction) + a swipe gesture (`touchstart`/`touchend` delta-X threshold, wired in the same `initDeliveryTurnstile()`) are the mobile interaction surface. Confirmed via a 375px Puppeteer screenshot that the previous version of this fix (fixed absolute-positioned height carried over from desktop) was clipping the description text and the step-indicator — fixed by moving the fixed-height/absolute-positioning rules entirely inside the `@media (min-width:768px)` block, leaving mobile as plain static-flow, auto-height markup.

**Two real bugs caught and fixed via actual interaction testing, not just visual screenshots:**
1. **Prev-card content rendered blank.** The prev card's box is positioned so its LEFT portion hangs off the viewport's clipped edge and only its RIGHT portion is the visible sliver — but the compact badge+name content defaulted to flex-start (left-aligned within the card's own box), which put it squarely in the invisible clipped-off half. Next cards were fine by coincidence (their visible sliver is their own left edge). Fixed with `justify-content: flex-end` on `.is-prev .turnstile-card-compact` specifically (`.is-next` keeps flex-start).
2. **A Puppeteer `elementHandle.click()` at the "next" card's bounding-box center silently landed on the site's own "Find Your Fit" auto-popup** (unrelated pre-existing feature, `main.js`, opens once per session ~3.5s after load) instead of the carousel, because that overlay was covering the click point at the moment the test fired. Not a carousel bug — confirmed by suppressing the popup via `sessionStorage.setItem('finderAutoShown','1')` before navigation and re-running; side-card and dot clicks both correctly move the card into view. Worth remembering for future Puppeteer tests against this homepage: the Finder auto-popup is a real click-blocker at the ~3.5s+ mark regardless of what's being tested.

**Accessibility fix — the spec's own "~40–50% opacity" side-card dimming is mathematically incompatible with WCAG AA at that band, not just a coincidental miss.** Worked the sRGB alpha-compositing math by hand and confirmed against `axe-core`: blending even pure-black (`--ink`) text over the compact card's near-white background at 0.45 opacity caps the effective contrast at roughly 3:1 — below the 4.5:1 minimum for normal-weight 13–15px text, regardless of how dark the un-dimmed source color is (opacity is the bottleneck, not color choice). Solved for the lightest opacity that still clears 4.5:1 with `--ink` text: **0.6**, confirmed via `axe-core` (0 violations at 0.6; same 4 nodes flagged at 0.45). Also switched the compact badge/name color from `--text-muted`/`--text` to `--ink` outright, since starting from an already-muted gray made the deficit worse. Documented the reasoning inline in `src/tailwind.css` so a future session doesn't "fix" the opacity back toward the spec's literal number without re-deriving why it can't.

**Concurrent-session note, resolved (not a real conflict):** another same-day NOTES.md entry (Industries section / flip-cards work, timestamped ~13:04) flagged `.turnstile-card-compact-name` color-contrast as a pre-existing violation belonging to "a different, concurrently-in-progress feature." That was this exact component, caught by that other session's own axe run while my fix was still in flight (this is a shared, concurrently-edited sandbox — see that entry's own concurrent-editing caveat). Confirmed via file mtimes and my own build/test history that no separate/duplicate turnstile implementation exists — it was this session's work, observed mid-edit. Now fixed (0.6 opacity, see above); a re-run of axe against the homepage should no longer surface it.

**Removed, not left dormant:** the entire pinned-scroll mechanic — `.delivery-pin-outer`/`.delivery-pin-inner`/`.delivery-slide*`/`.delivery-progress-dot`'s old rules (`src/tailwind.css`), `initDeliveryModel()` (`assets/js/main.js`), and its two now-pointless verification scripts (`scripts/_delivery-pin-verify-temp.js`, `scripts/_delivery-pin-mobile-temp.js`, both deleted outright — they only ever exercised `.delivery-slide`/`.delivery-pin-inner`, which no longer exist). `.delivery-progress`/`.delivery-progress-dot` classnames were kept (same 26×4px pill convention as the "Find Your Fit" `.finder-dot`, per the explicit "match the site's existing progress-dot convention" instruction) — only their trigger (now `initDeliveryTurnstile()`) and markup (now `<button>`s, for real keyboard/click semantics) changed.

**Verification:** `php -l` clean; `npm run build` clean. Puppeteer (`scripts/_turnstile-verify-temp.js`, kept per the repo's `_*-verify-temp.js` convention): confirmed rotation actually advances the center card after ~5s wait (Supply → Stage), confirmed a side-card click jumps that step to center immediately (Stage → Install), confirmed a dot click jumps directly to an arbitrary step (→ Manage), confirmed `prefers-reduced-motion` keeps the center card static (Supply, unchanged) across a 5s wait with manual nav still wired. `axe-core` via Puppeteer (`scripts/_axe-turnstile-temp.js`, kept, since the CLI wrapper can't find a Chrome binary in this sandbox — same class of environment quirk as other sessions' tooling notes): 0 violations on `#deliveryModel` after the opacity fix. Screenshots in `temporary-screenshots/` (`turnstile-1` through `turnstile-7`) confirm at-rest layout, post-rotation state, post-side-click state, post-dot-click state, reduced-motion static state (t0 and t+5s, identical), and the mobile (375px) fallback layout — all cleared at session end per the standing rule, since their content is described here and in-session already.

## 2026-07-23 — Industries section: standardized tile sizing, flip-card mechanic reused, and the recurring bracket-artifact bug actually root-caused (4th occurrence)

**Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md in full first, per instruction.** Three-part task on the homepage Industries section ("Modern protection for any space"): (1) fix inconsistent tile sizing, (2) apply the shared flip-card mechanic, (3) do a real root-cause fix on the recurring stray-bracket/confidence-tag bug rather than another one-off patch.

**Concurrent editing caveat, worth recording since it directly affected this session's own investigation.** Mid-session, a first full-codebase grep for `flip`/`backface`/`rotateY` came back completely empty — no flip-card component existed anywhere, no "Partners, not vendors" section, nothing in git history. Surfaced this to the user as a broken premise before building anything. The user asked to check again; a second grep (run without having touched anything in between) found both the flip-card component and the About page's "Partners, not vendors" section fully built, documented, and screenshot-verified by what was evidently a separate concurrent session working on the same theme directory in parallel (its own scratch files, e.g. `scripts/_flip-cards-verify-temp.js`, and its own NOTES.md entry immediately below this one, confirm this). Re-audited everything against the actually-current file state from that point on rather than trusting the first (now-stale) pass. Noting this because it's a real trap: in a shared/concurrently-edited environment, "I searched and found nothing" can go stale between one tool call and the next.

**Part 1 — tile sizing, root cause.** All 7 tiles already shared `aspect-[4/5]` — the aspect ratio was never actually inconsistent. The real bug (`front-page.php`, Industries loop): `$itoi_wide = ( 0 === $itoi_i % 2 ) ? 'basis-[340px]' : 'basis-[400px]';` — width alternated by array *index*, not by anything about the industry itself, so which industries landed on the narrow vs. wide class was arbitrary and shifted if any industry post were ever unpublished/reordered (Casinos & Gaming happened to land on `basis-[340px]` this session; a different post order would've put a different industry there). Fixed by removing the alternation entirely — every tile now uses the same `basis-[300px] min-[640px]:basis-[320px]`, standardized aspect ratio **4:5** (not changed from what was already there, just made to actually mean something now that width is also fixed).

**Per-photo object-position, checked individually rather than left at a blanket `object-center` guess.** Pulled the real `hero_image` for all 7 published industries and viewed each one before deciding: banking-finance (1024×576 landscape reception desk), casinos-gaming (1024×683), hospitality (1024×683 bar), retail (1024×683 market hall), stadiums-events (1024×683 crowd) all read correctly centered once cropped to 4:5 — no override needed. government-councils (768×1024, a tall parliamentary-chamber interior) needed `object-top`: centering it crops into the empty carpeted floor at the bottom and pushes the flag/upper gallery — the more distinctive part of the shot — toward the crop edge; `object-top` keeps the flag and chamber architecture in frame instead. logistics-warehousing (768×1152) reads fine centered — the aisle's vanishing point sits close to the frame's vertical center already. Confirmed visually via screenshots after the change (see Verification), not assumed from dimensions alone.

**Part 2 — flip-card mechanic reused, not rebuilt, per instruction.** Same `.flip-card`/`.flip-card-inner`/`.flip-card-front`/`.flip-card-back` markup and CSS as the About page's "Partners, not vendors" cards and the homepage solutions grid (`src/tailwind.css`, `initFlipCards()` in `assets/js/main.js`) — no new component. Front face: unchanged look — photo, gradient scrim, industry name overlaid at the bottom. The floating white "Learn more" pill that used to straddle the tile's bottom edge is gone; that link now lives on the back face only, alongside the industry's own `summary` ACF field text, reused verbatim (confirmed non-empty for all 7 industries by querying the CPT directly — retail/hospitality/casinos-gaming/banking-finance/government-councils/logistics-warehousing/stadiums-events all have real, already-written summary copy; none of it was rewritten for this task). Back face uses `bg-teal-900` to match the other two flip-card instances' back-face color.

**Click-through behavior — genuinely different from the other two flip-card usages, built as an opt-in extension rather than forking the shared component.** The other two usages only ever navigate via the explicit "Learn more" link inside `.flip-card-back` — the card body itself is never a navigation trigger. Industries needed more: clicking/tapping the card itself (once flipped) also navigates, but a single accidental tap on touch must not skip straight past the back-face content. Implemented via an opt-in `data-href` attribute (only the Industries cards set it — the other two usages don't, so their behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged) plus a small addition to `initFlipCards()` in `main.js`:
- Desktop (`hover: hover`): CSS `:hover`/`:focus-within` already flips the card before any click can land, so a click anywhere on the card body (that isn't the inner link) navigates immediately via `data-href`.
- Touch (`hover: none`): first tap only toggles `.is-flipped` (reveals the back face, does not navigate); a second tap — card already `.is-flipped` — navigates. Tapping the explicit "Learn more" link navigates on the first tap, same as the other two usages, since that's real anchor behavior the code never intercepts.
- Keyboard: tabbing onto the card (`tabindex="0"`) already triggers `:focus-within`'s flip before Enter/Space is even pressed, so Enter/Space navigates directly rather than needing a second keypress — no separate "reveal first" step is needed for keyboard the way it is for touch, since focus already did that job.

Verified with Puppeteer (`scripts/_industries-verify-temp.js`): forced `matchMedia('(hover: none)')` to `false` via `evaluateOnNewDocument` to reach the desktop code path (headless Chromium's CDP cannot force the `hover` media feature at all — same documented limitation as `_flip-cards-verify-temp.js` from the concurrent session, not a bug) — confirmed a click on the card body navigates to the industry permalink. On a real touch viewport (375px, `hasTouch: true`): first `.tap()` flips only (URL unchanged, confirmed by comparing `page.url()` before/after), second `.tap()` navigates to the real permalink; tapping the explicit "Learn more" link navigates as well. Carousel prev/next arrows (`#indPrev`/`#indNext`) confirmed still functional and independent of the flip mechanic — `carousel.scrollLeft` changes on click, no interference either direction.

**Part 3 — the actual root cause of the recurring bracket-artifact bug, found by asking a different question than the previous two sessions did.** Full current-state audit (`grep` for `itoi_reveal_class\|itoi_reveal_markup` across every `.php` file, re-run after the concurrent-edit discovery above to make sure the list was current) found **14 call sites**, exactly two shapes:

- **10 bare-text instances** — a heading/paragraph wrapped in `itoi_reveal_class()` + `itoi_reveal_markup()`, with no border, background, or photo of its own: `archive-industry.php`, `archive-solution.php`, `archive-case_study.php`, `archive-insight.php` (each archive's eyebrow+H1 intro block), `page-about.php` ("Partners, not vendors" H2), and `front-page.php` ("Explore by use case", "One integrated platform", "Why teams choose ITOI", "Modern protection for any space" — today's reported bug, "Explore our solutions").
- **4 genuinely-framed instances** — real bordered/photo content: the tile-grid cards on `archive-industry.php`/`archive-solution.php`/`archive-case_study.php`, and the metric cards on `single-case_study.php`.

**Why the first two fixes (Retail funnel card, then the platform-demo teaser) didn't actually stop this from recurring: they answered a different question than the one this bug needed answered.** Both were real bugs and both fixes held — an interactive element had been nested *inside* a `.itoi-reveal` wrapper, so the bracket/tag rendered offset and collided with real UI. Both sessions' fix was "un-nest it" (make the interactive part a `.no-detect-reveal` sibling instead). That's the correct fix for a nesting problem. But "Explore by use case" and "Modern protection for any space" were never nesting problems — they're two of the 10 bare-text instances above, correctly scoped to just the heading, never nested inside anything. The actual defect: `.itoi-reveal-bracket` (`-2px` inset) and `.itoi-reveal-tag` (`top:-11px`) are positioned to trace the edge of a real visible box — they only read as "a detection frame" when there's an actual border/photo/background there to frame. Applied to a bare heading wrapper (an `inline-block` div sized to just the text, or worse, a `max-w-[1280px]` block div where the text is centered/left-aligned but the *div* spans the full container width — `front-page.php`'s "One integrated platform" section was this second, worse case), two of the four brackets land nowhere near the visible text at all. Every previous fix chased the one reported instance and never asked whether *headings in general* should have this decoration — so the same defect kept surfacing on a different heading each time, four occurrences in, because 8 of the 10 bare-text instances had never been touched by either prior fix.

**The fix: separate the two things `itoi_reveal_class()`/`itoi_reveal_markup()` were bundling together, keep one, drop the other — for bare-text instances only.** `itoi_reveal_class()` alone (`.itoi-reveal`'s opacity/translateY rule) is the scroll fade-in and is safe on any element, headings included — this stays on all 10 bare-text instances, so the intended "subtle fade/rise on scroll-in" motion (PROJECT.md §3) is unaffected. `itoi_reveal_markup()` — the actual bracket-span + tag-span output — was removed from all 10 bare-text instances, and left untouched on the 4 genuinely-framed ones, where it always looked correct. This is a root-cause fix precisely because it's the same change applied uniformly across every instance of the defect, not another instance-specific patch: confirmed via Puppeteer site-wide sweep (`/`, `/solutions/`, `/industries/`, `/case-studies/`, `/insights/`, `/about/`) that `.itoi-reveal-bracket`/`.itoi-reveal-tag` node counts are now zero everywhere except the tile-grid/metric-card instances, where the counts exactly match "4 brackets + 1 tag per framed card" and nothing else.

**Documented the distinction directly in `inc/reveal.php`'s docblock** (not just here) so a future session reaching for `itoi_reveal_markup()` sees the rule at the point of use: call it only on a framed/bordered/photo element, never on a heading or paragraph wrapper. This is the belt-and-suspenders piece — the NOTES.md writeup explains what happened, the code comment is what a future session actually reads before adding the 15th call site.

**Verification:** `npm run build` (Tailwind compiles clean, all new utility classes — `object-top`, the fixed `basis-*` values — resolve). `php -l` clean on every touched file; `node --check assets/js/main.js` clean. `node scripts/axe-check.js` — 0 violations on `/` and `/about/` at both 375px and 1440px (the one homepage color-contrast violation found belongs to `.turnstile-card-compact`, the Delivery Model stepper — a different, concurrently-in-progress feature, not touched by this session). Lighthouse desktop on `/`: performance 100, accessibility 96 (same pre-existing/concurrent turnstile issue), SEO 100, best-practices 78 (dragged down entirely by `is-on-https`/`redirects-http` — this sandbox serves plain HTTP, an environment characteristic, not a regression from this session). Screenshots at 375px and 1440px in `temporary-screenshots/` (`38`–`46` prefix) confirm: all 7 tiles render at equal width at both breakpoints, the flipped back face shows real per-industry summary copy + working "Learn more" link, and the "Modern protection for any space" heading has zero corner-bracket/tag artifacts. `temporary-screenshots/` was **not** cleared at session end, despite CLAUDE.md's normal end-of-session rule, because it's actively shared with at least one other concurrent session (see the concurrent-editing note above) — clearing it would destroy that session's own verification evidence, not just mine.

## 2026-07-23 — "Partners, not vendors" trust section (About page, flip-cards) + homepage solutions grid converted to the same flip-card mechanic

**Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md in full first, per instruction.** Two related prompts, done together since Part 2 explicitly reuses Part 1's flip-card component: (1) a new "Partners, not vendors" trust section with 4 flip-cards, real content from the client's capability deck; (2) the homepage's existing 8-tile solutions grid ("Products Row") converted to flip-cards using the *same* mechanic, front face = category number + name, back face = tagline + outcome + "Learn more" link, plus confirming the individual solution pages keep their own static tagline under the H1.

**Placement decision (flagged per instruction, not silently picked): About page, not the homepage.** The homepage is already dense — mega-hero, use-cases, Delivery Model, platform-demo teaser, traffic widget, Why Choose ITOI, customers preview, review-badge slot, industries carousel, solutions grid, bottom CTA — and "Partners, not vendors" is fundamentally a company-positioning/leadership-credibility statement (30+ years experience, hardware-agnostic stance, account management model), which reads as About-page content, not homepage conversion content. The About page (`page-about.php`) was comparatively thin (hero + WYSIWYG narrative + team CTA band), had clear room, and the new section's framing ("who we are as partners") leads naturally into the page's existing narrative right below it. Placed directly after the About hero, before the existing narrative section.

**Redundancy flag, resolved by the user before building:** card 4 ("Always-On Support") meaningfully overlaps the existing "Why choose ITOI" pill-tab section's "24/7 specialist support" tab (`assets/js/main.js` `whyData`, front-page.php) — both promise round-the-clock support; "Dedicated Account Manager" (card 3) also echoes that same tab's "Dedicated account specialist per client" bullet. Raised this directly to the user (not just logged) before writing any copy. **User's call: build both as specified, log the overlap here for a future content pass — no copy changes made.** Flagging again here in case a future session wants to differentiate or consolidate: the Why-ITOI tab and this section currently say almost the same thing about support in two different places on the site.

**Reduced motion — no bespoke logic needed, reused the site's existing blanket rule.** `src/tailwind.css` already has a sitewide `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *,*::before,*::after { transition: none !important; animation: none !important; } }` (line ~149, predates this session). The flip-card's `transition: transform 0.6s` on `.flip-card-inner` is caught by that rule automatically, so hover/tap still swaps to the back face under reduced motion, just instantly rather than animated — this is the "show the back-face content immediately without animation" option from the two offered, chosen because it's what the codebase already does everywhere else (documented precedent at the `.pd-bar`/`.pd-risk-bar` comments) rather than inventing a second reduced-motion story. Verified directly (see Verification below), not assumed from reading the CSS alone.

**Built, shared between both prompts (one mechanic, not two):**
- `src/tailwind.css` — `.flip-card`/`.flip-card-inner`/`.flip-card-front`/`.flip-card-back`: genuine 3D flip (`perspective` on the outer element, `rotateY(180deg)` + `backface-visibility: hidden` on the inner/faces), gated to `@media (hover: hover)` for the CSS-only desktop hover (and keyboard `:focus-within`, so tabbing to a real link inside `.flip-card-back` — the homepage grid's "Learn more" — reveals it first). `.flip-card.is-flipped .flip-card-inner` (no media guard) is the touch/keyboard-toggle class.
- `assets/js/main.js` — `initFlipCards()`: touch devices (`window.matchMedia('(hover: none)').matches`, per the task's explicit detection method) get a click-to-toggle listener (ignoring clicks on a real `<a>` inside the back face, so "Learn more" still navigates instead of just flipping back); all cards get a keydown Enter/Space toggle for keyboard users without a real pointer. `role="button"` + `aria-pressed` are only added to cards with **no** nested link (the About page cards) — axe-core's `aria-allowed-attr` correctly flagged `aria-pressed` on the homepage grid's cards on the first pass, since that attribute isn't ARIA-allowed on a plain container role when the real interactive element is the nested `<a>`; fixed by gating both the attribute and its updates on `!hasLink`, re-verified 0 violations after.
- Solution CPT (`acf-json/group_1e5502ad2d38.json`) gained 2 new fields: `tagline` (text) and `outcome_description` (text) — **not previously on this repo's field list**, see the "found, not assumed" note below.
- New ACF options page "Partners, Not Vendors" (`inc/acf.php`, `acf-json/group_7f2a4c9e01d3.json`): `partners_headline`, `partners_intro`, `partners_cards` (repeater, min=4/max=4: `front_title`, `back_description`) — all 4 cards' real deck copy seeded as `default_value` in the JSON and via `update_field()` into the DB (repeater `default_value` doesn't auto-hydrate through `get_field()`, same documented gotcha as the Delivery Model options page — seeded via a one-off `wp eval-file` script, not left to chance).

**Found, not assumed — Prompt 2 Part B's premise didn't hold in this repo.** The task said the individual solution page's static tagline "was already built/specified in the earlier tagline prompt" and asked me to "confirm it's still in place." Checked `single-solution.php` and every `acf-json/*.json` field for `solution` before writing anything: **no `tagline` field and no static-tagline markup existed anywhere in this codebase.** Rather than silently building around a false premise, built it fresh this session: added `tagline`/`outcome_description` to the Solution CPT (above), added the static italic tagline paragraph directly under the H1 in `single-solution.php` (between the headline and the `dek`), and pointed the homepage grid's back-face at the exact same two fields — so "same field, both places" is true going forward, it just wasn't already true coming in. Confirmed via `curl` grep on a live solution page (`intelligence-analytics`) that both the static tagline and the flip-card grid's back face render the identical string sourced from the one field.

**Homepage solutions grid ("Products Row") — number order is the deck's explicit order, not the CPT's alphabetical default.** The existing query sorted `orderby=title ASC`; switched to an explicit slug array (`intelligence-analytics, customer-engagement-signage, sensory-intelligence, workforce-ops-robotics, cctv-video-loss-prevention, back-of-house-integration, security-access-inventory, it-network-infrastructure`) resolved via `get_page_by_path()` per row — same pattern the Industries carousel already used for its own locked slug order — so 01-08 always matches the task's given numbering regardless of how titles happen to sort. `it-network-infrastructure` (08) has an empty `tagline`, per the deck; the back face's tagline `<p>` is conditionally omitted for that card (outcome line still always prints) — confirmed via rendered-HTML grep, not just by reading the template.

**Verification, both prompts:**
- `php -l` clean on `page-about.php`, `single-solution.php`, `front-page.php`; `node -c` clean on `main.js`; `npm run build` clean.
- Data seeded and resolving: `get_field()` checked directly via `wp eval` for the options page and one solution post before touching templates.
- **Puppeteer/headless Chromium caveat, found and worked around, not glossed over:** CDP's Emulation domain has no support for forcing the `hover`/`pointer` media features (`page.emulateMediaFeatures` throws "Unsupported media feature: hover"), and headless Chromium reports `(hover: none)` regardless of `setViewport`'s `hasTouch`/`isMobile` flags — so automated mouse-hover can't exercise the `@media (hover: hover)` desktop path directly in this sandbox. Isolated the variable before concluding anything: injected an unconditional `.flip-card:hover{...}` rule with no media guard and moved the (virtual) mouse onto a card via `page.hover()` — the browser applied real `:hover` state and mid-transitioned the transform correctly, proving the underlying flip mechanism is sound and the gap is purely this sandbox's inability to simulate a hover-capable device, not a code defect (same category of caveat as the Delivery Model pin section's real-mobile-chrome note, 2026-07-23). Desktop "flipped" screenshots below instead toggle the real shipped `.is-flipped` class via JS — the same class touch devices use, not a mock — to depict the settled back-face state.
- **Touch path verified for real, not worked around:** `setViewport({hasTouch:true, isMobile:true})` correctly made `matchMedia('(hover:none)').matches` true; `element.tap()` correctly toggled `.is-flipped` true then false on a second tap (flip-back confirmed), on both the About page and the homepage grid.
- **Reduced motion verified for real:** `page.emulateMediaFeatures([{name:'prefers-reduced-motion', value:'reduce'}])` + toggling `.is-flipped` showed `transition-duration: 0s` and the fully-settled `rotateY(180deg)` matrix (`matrix3d(-1,0,0,...,-1,...)`) within 50ms of the toggle — instant, no animation, as intended.
- **axe-core: 0 violations on the About page, both breakpoints**, after the `aria-pressed` fix above. **Homepage: 0 violations attributable to this work** — isolated by checking every reported `color-contrast`/`aria-*` node's selector directly: all remaining homepage violations trace to two sections built by concurrent/other work this same day, neither part of this task — the Industries carousel's own flip-card back-face text (`.shrink-0.basis-[300px]...`, a *different* flip-card usage that also reused this session's shared component, per its own comment in `front-page.php`) and the Delivery Model turnstile's `.turnstile-card-compact-name`. Confirmed directly, not assumed: none of the flagged selectors match this task's `.aspect-[4/5]` Products Row grid or any About-page `.flip-card`. Investigated the carousel one further step — same `bg-teal-900`/`text-white` pairing as this task's own cards (which axe passes cleanly), but axe intermittently reports the *page* background (`#fcfcfb`) instead of the card's own `teal-900` for specific carousel positions, which only reproduces on cards sitting inside that `overflow-x-auto` scroll container — looks like an axe-core quirk with contrast-checking a 3D-transformed `backface-visibility:hidden` face inside horizontally-clipped content, not a defect in the shared `.flip-card-back` CSS itself. Not fixed — out of scope (different section, different session's work) — flagged here for whoever owns the Industries carousel next.
- **Concurrent-session awareness, confirmed not assumed:** `front-page.php`, `src/tailwind.css`, `assets/js/main.js`, and `page-about.php` were all independently touched by other work landing mid-session (the Delivery Model's pinned-scroll mechanic was rebuilt into a time-based "turnstile" carousel; the Industries carousel was itself converted to reuse this session's `.flip-card` component; `page-about.php`'s reveal-bracket call on the new H2 was removed). Re-read every affected file after each external change landed and re-ran `php -l`/`node -c`/`npm run build`/the flip-card verify script/axe before treating anything as done — confirmed this session's own Products Row and Partners-cards markup survived every one of those edits intact (e.g. `initFlipCards()` gained an opt-in `data-href` click-through extension from the concurrent Industries-carousel work; harmless for this task's cards since none of them set `data-href`).

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`): `20/29-about-partners-resting-1440`, `21/30-about-partners-flipped-1440` (card 1 fully rotated, dark teal-900 back face, white text legible), `24/33-about-partners-resting-375`, `25/34-about-partners-flipped-375` (single-column mobile, tap-flipped), `22/31-home-solutions-resting-1440` (all 8 numbered 01-08 in deck order), `23/32-home-solutions-flipped-1440` (card 01 flipped: italic tagline, outcome line, "Learn more →" link to `/solutions/intelligence-analytics/`), `26/35-home-solutions-resting-375`, `27/36-home-solutions-flipped-375`, `19/28/37-solution-tagline-1440` (intelligence-analytics page, static italic tagline under H1, confirmed unaffected by the homepage grid work). Verification script kept as `scripts/_flip-cards-verify-temp.js` (scratch, same convention as `_edu-hub-verify-temp.js`).

## 2026-07-23 — Hero slideshow RESTORED (a prior session's simplification over-reached), height reduction kept, + new RetailNext co-branding slide

**Scope — correcting a genuine over-reach, not a new design decision:** the original "shrink the hero" task asked only for reduced vertical padding/height. That session also removed the entire multi-slide dot-nav slideshow (auto-advance, ring-fill, click-to-jump), collapsing 4 headline/subcopy variants down to one static headline — never requested, flagged today as incorrect, and reverted in full. The height reduction itself was correct and stays. On top of the revert, a 5th slide was added for a RetailNext partnership announcement.

**Genuinely restored, not just visually reproduced — confirmed via the actual mechanism, not appearance:**
- `#dotNav` (the vertical progress-dot column) is back in `front-page.php`, populated by `initHeroSlideshow()` in `main.js` — same function name and structure as the original pre-simplification version, not a new invented approximation.
- The `itoi-ringfill` `@keyframes` + `.dot-btn.active circle.ring { animation: itoi-ringfill 5s linear forwards; }` CSS rule (removed in the simplification) is back in `tailwind.css`, verbatim.
- **Auto-advance genuinely confirmed working, not assumed from the code:** Puppeteer test clicked dot 1 (slide 1 active), waited 5.6s past the 5s interval, and confirmed the active dot had actually moved to index 1 — the interval-based auto-advance is really running, not just present as dead code.
- **Click-to-jump confirmed on every dot**, including the new 5th one, each showing genuinely distinct headline/subcopy content — not five dots all pointing at the same slide.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion` confirmed correctly disabling ONLY the auto-advance**, not the whole mechanism: emulated reduced-motion, waited 5.6s, active dot stayed at index 0 (no auto-advance) — this matches the original slideshow's own reduced-motion behavior (manual dot clicks still work, the interval timer just never starts), not a new/different reduced-motion story invented for this restoration.

**Height reduction genuinely kept, not silently reverted alongside the slideshow restore:** hero height measured at 380px (mobile, 375px viewport) and 480px (desktop, 1440px viewport) after this fix — identical to the reduced-hero task's own numbers, nowhere near the original oversized ~561px/~828px. The mobile-specific `max-w-[22ch] → min-[768px]:max-w-[14ch]` headline-wrap fix from that same task (needed to make the mobile reduction non-marginal) was also kept, not reverted.

**Content architecture — closed a real, long-standing gap rather than reintroducing it:** the original slideshow (before either the shrink or this restore) had its 4 slides hardcoded directly in `main.js`'s `heroSlides` array; a `hero_slides` ACF repeater field existed on the Site Settings options page but its own instructions text admitted "front-page.php currently has these hardcoded in assets/js/main.js — wiring the template to read from here is follow-up work, not done in this phase," and no template ever actually read it. This restoration doesn't reintroduce that gap: `hero_slides` is a fresh repeater (new field keys — the field name existed twice before with two incompatible structures, see the "own mistake" note below) actually localized via `wp_localize_script('itoi-main', 'itoiHeroSlides', ...)` in `inc/enqueue.php`, read by `initHeroSlideshow()`, with a small hardcoded array kept only as a defensive fallback if the localized data is ever empty (same pattern already used for `itoiFinderData`/`itoiWhyPhotos`). Non-technical staff can now actually edit these 5 slides in wp-admin; the pre-restoration version, despite having the same-named field, could not.

**RetailNext slide — position and mechanism:** added as **slide 5 (the last position)**, after the original 4, per the task's explicit "not a replacement for the others." Two new repeater sub-fields drive it: `is_partnership` (true/false) and `partner_name` (text, "RetailNext"), plus an optional `partner_logo` image field (empty — no RetailNext logo asset exists anywhere in this environment, confirmed via a filesystem + uploads search before assuming text-only was needed, not guessed). When `is_partnership` is checked, `initHeroSlideshow()`'s `renderHeroHeadline()` renders a co-branding lockup — partner logo-or-name, then a smaller/muted "×" (sized in `em` against the H1's own `clamp()` so it scales correctly at every viewport, not a fixed px value), then "ITOI" styled to match the header's own wordmark treatment (small signature-bright dot + bold text) — instead of running the headline through as plain text. The `headline` field is still filled in for this row too ("RetailNext × iTOi Solutions", plain text) as a fallback/reference value, per the field's own instructions, even though the on-screen rendering uses the lockup markup instead.

**Own mistake caught mid-task, not shipped:** the field name `hero_slides` had been used for the ACF repeater twice before in this project's history — the very first version (built early on, sub-fields `headline`/`subcopy` under key `field_87ce3342f5ec`) and then briefly as two plain text fields (`hero_headline`/`hero_subcopy`) during the incorrect simplification. Seeding this restoration's data via `update_field('hero_slides', $rows, 'option')` initially wrote successfully but `get_field()` read back every row missing `is_partnership`/`partner_name` entirely (PHP "undefined array key" warnings, not silently wrong data — caught immediately). Root cause, confirmed by reading the raw `wp_options` table directly rather than guessing: the DB's `_options_hero_slides` reference row still pointed at the original, years-earlier `field_87ce3342f5ec` key from the very first version of this field, which ACF trusted over the current field group definition — the new sub-fields didn't exist under that stale reference, so they were silently dropped. Fixed by deleting all `%hero_slides%` option rows outright (22 rows — clean slate, no partial/mixed state) and re-seeding fresh against the current field definition; re-verified the readback showed all 5 fields correctly on every row afterward.

**Live Detection background — confirmed unaffected, not just assumed:** `initHeroDetectionBoxes()` was never touched by either the original simplification or this restoration (it's a separate function, always still called from `initHero()`); Puppeteer confirmed 5 detection-box elements present under `#heroBg` on every slide including the new RetailNext one, drifting/repositioning the same as before. The task's own wording called this "amber" — per PROJECT.md §3, `--signature` was migrated from a placeholder amber to brand navy on 2026-07-22, and CLAUDE.md hard-forbids reintroducing amber; kept the current navy/`--signature-bright` tokens (unchanged, never touched) rather than reading "amber" as a literal instruction to regress the color, same call made on the Delivery Model task for the same reason.

**Verification:** `php -l`/`node -c` clean on every touched file. `npm run build` clean. `axe-core`: 0 violations at both breakpoints, checked in BOTH the default (slide 1) state AND with the RetailNext slide clicked active (an interacted state, not just default — CLAUDE.md's own screenshot+verify-loop rule). `debug.log` diffed clean (same pre-existing `post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` notices, nothing new). Screenshots: `hero-slide1-{375,1440}.png` (slide 1, 5 dots visible, reduced height), `hero-slide5-retailnext-{375,1440}.png` (the co-branding lockup, dot 5 active/ring-filled). `scripts/_hero-slideshow-verify-temp.js` kept as the current scratch verification tool for this mechanism (same convention as `_edu-hub-verify-temp.js`).

**PROJECT.md corrected in place** (§3 mechanic #2, §4 Site Settings): both previously described the simplified single-headline version as current/correct, which is no longer true — updated to describe the restored 5-slide dot-nav as the actual current behavior, with the brief incorrect simplification noted as history rather than silently erased. **Own mistake caught while editing §4:** the first edit accidentally dropped `office_hours, support_hours` from that field list entirely while rewriting the `hero_slides` description around them — caught immediately by rereading the diff, fixed before moving on.

## 2026-07-23 — "Delivery Model" rebuilt as a PINNED, one-step-at-a-time scroll-driven slideshow (replaces the progressive-reveal-and-stay mechanic entirely)

**Scope:** explicit instruction to replace the section's interaction mechanic wholesale, not tweak it — the previous version (all 6 steps stack vertically and progressively reveal, staying visible once activated) is gone. New mechanic: the section pins in the viewport while the visitor scrolls through it; only ONE of the 6 steps is ever visible; scrolling advances through them one at a time; scrolling up reverses exactly; after step 6 it unpins and normal scroll continues. This is a genuinely different mechanism, not a CSS skin on the old one — IntersectionObserver (what every other scroll mechanic on this page uses) can't drive a pin/scrub, since it only reports a boolean in/out-of-view per element, not a continuous scroll-position value. Built on a throttled `scroll` listener instead, computing progress directly from the wrapper's own `getBoundingClientRect()`.

**Structure:**
- `#deliveryModel` (`.delivery-pin-outer`) — a plain-flow wrapper with `height: {step_count}00dvh` (currently 600dvh) set inline from PHP, coupled to the actual ACF repeater row count rather than hardcoded, so it stays correct even if the field's min/max=6 lock is ever changed. Exists purely to give the page enough scroll DISTANCE to drive the sequence; no visual treatment itself.
- `.delivery-pin-inner` — `position: sticky; top: 72px; height: calc(100dvh - 72px)`. 72px matches the site header's own fixed height (`header.php`, `h-[72px]`) exactly, so the panel sits flush below the sticky nav rather than under or overlapping it — confirmed via `getComputedStyle`, not eyeballed. `dvh`, not `vh`: declared after a plain-`vh` fallback in the same rule (cascade — non-supporting browsers just keep the vh value). This matters specifically for mobile, where address-bar chrome shows/hides on scroll and classic `100vh` is sized against the chrome-hidden state even while chrome is showing, which is exactly the kind of thing that makes pinned sections misbehave on phones.
- `.delivery-slide` (×6) — `position: absolute; inset: 0` (stacked on each other), default `opacity: 0; visibility: hidden; pointer-events: none`. Only `.is-current` gets `opacity: 1; visibility: visible; pointer-events: auto`. Used `visibility`, not `display: none`, specifically because the task wants a ~0.25s cross-fade and `display: none` can't transition — `visibility: hidden` still removes the element from layout, hit-testing, AND the accessibility tree (confirmed: screen readers don't see `visibility: hidden` content either, same as `display: none`), so "not present in a scrolled-past state" holds while still allowing the requested fade. Transition delays `visibility` behind `opacity` on the way out and flips it instantly on the way in — the exact pattern `.mega-menu` already uses elsewhere in this file, reused rather than invented.
- `.delivery-progress` — 6 small bars (same proportions as Find Your Fit's `.finder-dot`), `.is-current` filled `var(--signature)`, others `var(--line)`.

**JS (`initDeliveryModel()`, main.js) — fully rewritten, not adapted:** on scroll, `progress = (-outerRect.top) / (outerRect.height - innerHeight)`, clamped 0–1, `index = floor(progress * 6)` clamped to a valid index, `setIndex()` swaps `.is-current` between slides/dots. This is a pure function of current scroll position recomputed on every scroll tick (rAF-throttled) — reversible by construction, unlike the old mechanic's `IntersectionObserver.unobserve()` one-shot pattern, which could only ever move forward. `reduceMotion` short-circuits the function before it does anything (no listener attached at all, not just a no-op state) — the reduced-motion fallback is 100% CSS (see below), so JS has nothing to do for those users.

**Reduced motion — pinning itself is the motion being disabled, not just the fade:** a `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block in `tailwind.css` collapses `.delivery-pin-outer` back to `height: auto`, `.delivery-pin-inner` back to `position: static`, and forces every `.delivery-slide` to `position: static; opacity: 1; visibility: visible` (all 6 stacked in normal document flow) — same markup as the pinned version, no duplicated content, only the layout changes via CSS. Confirmed via Puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures`: `outerHeight` collapses from 5400px/4872px (pinned) down to 966px/1199px (desktop/mobile), `pinPosition` reports `static`, all 6 slides report non-zero opacity, `.delivery-progress` is `display: none` (the dots don't mean anything once everything's shown at once).

**Verification — actually scrolled through it and checked computed styles, not just class names:** `scripts/_delivery-pin-verify-temp.js` samples 8 points across the full pinned range (before entering, progress 0.02 through 0.97, and past the end) at 1440px, reading BOTH `classList.contains('is-current')` AND the real computed `opacity`/`visibility` for every slide at each point — every single checkpoint showed exactly one slide passing the "genuinely visible" check, matching the expected step (progress 0.2→Stage, 0.42→Install, 0.6→Handover, 0.8→Manage, 0.97→SLA). Confirmed the pin engages exactly at `pinTop === 72` (the header-height offset) once scrolled into the section and releases (`pinTop` goes negative, `stuck: false`) only after scrolling past the wrapper's full height, with the last step (SLA) still showing in that final frame before the page continues to "One integrated platform" beneath it. **Reverse explicitly tested, not assumed from the forward pass:** scrolled to progress 0.97 (step 6) then back to progress 0.08 — returned to step 1 exactly, confirming this isn't one-directional. `scripts/_delivery-pin-mobile-temp.js` repeated the same progress sweep at 375×812 — identical one-step-at-a-time behavior, same reversal check, confirmed working.

**Screenshots — four states, not just a before/after pair:** `pin-a-step1-1440.png` (step 1 alone, section pinned directly under the nav, progress dot 1 filled), `pin-b-step3-1440.png` (step 3 alone — Install; steps 1-2 and 4-6 not on screen), `pin-c-reversed-step1-1440.png` (scrolled back up from step 6 to the step-1 zone, confirms exact reversal), `pin-d-unpinned-1440.png` (scrolled past the section — normal page flow resumed, "One integrated platform" and the traffic widget visible, Delivery Model panel gone). Plus `pin-mobile-step3-375.png` / `pin-mobile-step1-375.png` for the mobile sweep, and `pin-reducedmotion-{375,1440}.png` showing all 6 steps as a plain static list, no pin, no dots.

**Own mistake caught before it mattered:** first draft's progress dots were going to reuse `.finder-dot`'s actual CSS class directly (coupling this section to the Find Your Fit modal's own styling) — backed out in favor of a new `.delivery-progress-dot` class with the same visual proportions instead, so a future edit to the quiz's dots doesn't silently reskin this section too (and vice versa). Not a shipped bug, just flagging the near-miss since "matching the site's existing... convention" could too easily have been read as "share the actual class."

**Genuinely open, not resolved — needs the user's call, not a unilateral decision (matches the task's own explicit instruction to flag this rather than guess):** Puppeteer's mobile-viewport emulation sets a fixed-size, chrome-less viewport — it does NOT simulate a real phone browser's address-bar show/hide-on-scroll behavior, which is the specific, well-known failure mode for pinned/sticky sections on mobile. Everything I could actually test (the scroll-to-index math, the sticky CSS, the reduced-motion fallback, the `dvh` sizing) checks out cleanly at mobile widths, and `dvh` was used specifically as a defensive measure against the classic chrome-related sizing bug — but I have no way to confirm real mobile Safari/Chrome chrome-hiding behavior in this sandboxed environment. Flagging this to the user directly rather than either (a) claiming mobile is fully verified when it isn't, or (b) unilaterally deciding to fall back to a static list on mobile without asking, since that's a real product/risk-tolerance tradeoff, not a technical question with one right answer.

**Cleanup:** deleted `scripts/_delivery-model-verify-temp.js` and `_delivery-progressive-shots-temp.js` — both tested the now-fully-replaced `.delivery-step`/`.is-active` mechanic (that DOM structure no longer exists at all), so keeping them around would have been actively misleading for a future session. Kept `_delivery-pin-verify-temp.js` and `_delivery-pin-mobile-temp.js` as the current scratch verification tools, same convention as `_edu-hub-verify-temp.js`.

**Also verified:** `node -c main.js` clean, `php -l front-page.php` clean, `npm run build` clean, `debug.log` diffed clean (same pre-existing `post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` notices every session this month has logged, nothing new), `axe-core` 0 violations at both breakpoints.

**Mobile decision, resolved:** presented the open question above to the user directly rather than guessing. User chose to ship the pinned slideshow on mobile too (not a static-list fallback) — no code change needed, this is what was already built and tested. Flagging so a future session doesn't second-guess this as an oversight: it's a deliberate choice, made with the real-device caveat known and accepted, not an untested default that slipped through.

## 2026-07-23 — "Delivery Model" scroll-reveal bug report: investigated, one real fix applied, root cause narrowed down but not 100% pinned to a single environment

**Scope:** user reported all 6 Delivery Model steps rendering fully activated immediately on page load, with the scroll-triggered progressive reveal not working at all. Asked to debug against a specific checklist (observer attachment/threshold, default CSS state, conflicting animation systems, viewport-fit) and fix.

**Investigation — extensive, not a quick guess:** wrote two scratch Puppeteer scripts (`scripts/_delivery-debug-temp.js`, `_delivery-debug-temp2.js`) and tested the *already-deployed, unmodified* code across the full realistic range before touching anything: every mobile size (375×667 through 414×896) and every desktop height from 550px to 1800px, plus 1100px width sweep. **Could not reproduce the reported symptom under normal motion preference at any realistic size** — every one of those loads correctly started all 6 steps inactive (confirmed via `classList.contains('is-active')` reads, not just visual inspection), matching the computed style check too (`circleBackground: rgb(255,255,255)`, `nameOpacity: 0.5` on load — genuinely the outline/dim rest state, not just a class that happened to be absent). Also ruled out, by direct check rather than assumption, all four things the task asked to specifically verify:
- IntersectionObserver *is* attached individually to all 6 `.delivery-step` elements (one observer, six `observe()` calls) — confirmed via `main.js` read, not changed.
- The inactive CSS state *is* the unconditional default in `src/tailwind.css` — `.delivery-circle`'s base rule is the outline/white state; the filled navy state only exists under the `.delivery-step.is-active` compound selector. Nothing about it depends on JS successfully removing a class.
- No conflicting animation library: grepped the whole theme for AOS/GSAP/animate.css/ScrollReveal/WOW.js — none present. Only one `<script>` tag loads `main.js` (checked via rendered `document.querySelectorAll('script[src]')` — no duplicate enqueue). No inline `style` attributes on any delivery element (would indicate something else writing directly to the DOM).

**What DID reproduce the exact symptom:** `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` (emulated via Puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures`) shows all 6 steps + all 5 connectors active immediately on load, every time — this is correct, spec'd behavior (the task's own point 5), not a bug. If whoever observed "everything active on load" was testing on a machine/browser with OS-level "Reduce Motion" enabled (common on macOS/Windows accessibility settings, and on some automated screenshot/QA tooling that defaults to it), that fully explains what they saw without any code being broken. Flagging this plainly rather than silently assuming it, since I can't directly confirm what produced their original report.

**A real, separate bug found and fixed regardless — a genuine edge case the task's own point 4 anticipated:** at very tall viewports, the original `rootMargin: '-45% 0px -45% 0px'` (percentage-based, same pattern as the pre-existing longform scrollspy elsewhere in `main.js`) produces a trigger band whose *absolute pixel height* scales with viewport height. At a normal viewport this 10%-tall band (e.g. 90px on a 900px-tall screen) is smaller than a single step's row height (~88-108px on desktop), so steps trigger one at a time correctly — confirmed already. But at ~2200px+ viewport height, the same 10% band is 220px+, wider than a single step's row, so two adjacent steps can both fall inside it and activate simultaneously without any scrolling. Fixed by computing the band in JS from the real `window.innerHeight`, **capped at a fixed 80px** regardless of viewport size (`main.js`, `initDeliveryModel()`), so the trigger zone can never exceed a single step's row height no matter how tall the screen is.

**Own mistake caught before shipping, worth recording:** the first version of that fix computed `margin = (innerHeight - band) / 2` and used it as a **positive** rootMargin (`margin + 'px 0px ' + margin + 'px 0px'`) — forgetting that IntersectionObserver rootMargin needs *negative* values to shrink the root inward; a positive value expands it outward instead. Tested it before moving on (per this project's own screenshot+verify-loop habit) and it was dramatically worse: at 900px viewport height, 3 of 6 steps activated on load; at 1100px+, all 6 did — i.e. this exact mistake reproduces the reported bug's symptom precisely. Caught by re-running the same verification sweep immediately after the change, not assumed correct because it compiled; fixed by negating both sides (`'-' + margin + 'px 0px -' + margin + 'px 0px'`), re-verified clean across 550–1800px.

**Residual, accepted limitation, documented rather than silently left:** at truly extreme heights (~2200px+ — effectively a single monitor taller than any real consumer display at 100% browser zoom; not reproducible on any realistic visitor viewport including 4K at typical scaling) a step can still activate early, because "the vertical center of the viewport" stops corresponding to "where the visitor scrolled to" once the viewport is taller than the content itself — an inherent conceptual limit of any center-of-viewport scroll trigger, not something the band-cap can fix without abandoning the "roughly viewport center" requirement entirely. Not chasing this further; flagging as a known, accepted edge case.

**Verification:** `node -c main.js` clean. Re-ran the full progressive-scroll suite (`scripts/_delivery-model-verify-temp.js`, incremental 40-step scroll, not an instant jump — an instant `scrollTo()` was already known from the prior session to understate activation since the browser never registers intersection for elements a jump skips past) at both 375px and 1440px: on load `[false,false,false,false,false,false]`; mid-scroll `[true,true,true,false,false,false]`; scroll-up-to-top afterward still `[true,true,true,false,false,false]` (stays activated, not reset). `prefers-reduced-motion` re-confirmed: all steps + connectors active immediately, both breakpoints. `debug.log` diffed clean (only the same pre-existing `post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` textdomain notices every session this month has logged). `axe-core`: 0 violations at both breakpoints (re-ran after the JS change since it touches the same section axe already checked once this project).

**Screenshots — three distinct scroll depths per breakpoint, proving progressive reveal, not just before/after:** `scripts/_delivery-progressive-shots-temp.js` (kept, scratch script, same convention as `_edu-hub-verify-temp.js`) scrolls incrementally to three checkpoints and captures + logs the active-state array at each: `progressive-a-step1-{375,1440}.png` (step 1 active, 2-6 inactive), `progressive-b-step1to3-{375,1440}.png` (steps 1-3 active, 4-6 inactive), `progressive-c-allsix-{375,1440}.png` (all 6 active). Visually confirmed all three at 1440px and the first at 375px — filled navy circles + drawn-in line + full-opacity headline for active steps, outline circles + dashed dim line + ~50%-opacity headline for inactive ones, exactly matching the spec. Had to pre-seed `sessionStorage.finderAutoShown` before navigating in this script too — the pre-existing site-wide "Find Your Fit" modal's 3.5s auto-open timer fired mid-capture on the first pass and obscured the section (unrelated to this bug, same known behavior documented in earlier sessions).
