## 2026-07-23 — Homepage: hero shrink + new "Delivery Model" scroll-triggered stepper section

**Scope:** two related homepage changes at the user's explicit request. Part 1: shrink the mega-hero and cut its 4-variant auto-advancing dot-nav slideshow down to one fixed headline+subcopy. Part 2: a new "Delivery Model" section — a vertical scroll-triggered stepper with 6 real steps from the client's capability deck. **Note for whoever reads this next:** a separate, concurrent session was also live on this same theme today (the "Solutions restructure" — CPT migration/redirects/SEO, entries above/below this one) — its own NOTES.md entry at the time confirms it observed this session's hero edit landing mid-flight and confirmed no collision with its own PRODUCTS ROW work; re-verified from this end too, `front-page.php` renders both sessions' sections correctly with no overwritten content as of this entry.

**Part 1 — hero shrink:**
- Height: was `h-[calc(78vh-72px)] min-h-[520px]` mobile / `h-[calc(100vh-72px)] min-h-[640px]` desktop (viewport-height-driven, effectively near-fullscreen). Now padding+min-height driven: `py-10 min-h-[380px]` mobile / `py-24 min-h-[480px]` desktop (640px+) — content height, not viewport %. Measured via Puppeteer against what the old formula would have produced at the same viewport sizes: **desktop 828px → 480px (42% shorter)**, **mobile 561px → 380px (32% shorter)** — both a meaningful reduction, not marginal. Mobile needed a second pass: the first attempt (padding cut alone) only got 18% because the H1's `max-w-[14ch]` wraps to 4 short lines at 375px width regardless of padding — widened to `max-w-[22ch]` below the 768px breakpoint only (`min-[768px]:max-w-[14ch]` restores the original desktop wrap exactly), cutting it to 2 lines and unlocking the real reduction. Desktop layout/wrap unaffected.
- Slideshow removed: `heroSlides` array, `initHeroSlideshow()`, the `#dotNav` element, and its SVG ring-fill CSS (`.dot-btn`, `itoi-ringfill` keyframe) are gone from `main.js`/`tailwind.css`. `initHero()` now only calls `initHeroDetectionBoxes()` — the Live Detection background is untouched, per the task's explicit instruction to keep it, both CTAs, and the scrim/photo treatment as-is.
- Content model: the options-page `hero_slides` repeater (4 rows, never actually wired to the template — its own ACF instructions said so) is replaced with two plain fields, `hero_headline` (text) + `hero_subcopy` (textarea), on the same Site Settings options page (`acf-json/group_73b8c1766c9f.json`), defaulted to the mockup's first/primary slide copy. Front-page.php now reads these via `get_field(..., 'option')` with the same copy as a PHP fallback — this is a real fix, not just a rename: the old dot-nav's headline/subcopy were hardcoded twice (once in `front-page.php`'s H1, again in `main.js`'s `heroSlides[0]`), never actually CMS-editable despite CLAUDE.md's "no hardcoded copy" rule; now it's one ACF-backed source.

**Part 2 — "Delivery Model" section:**
- **Placement — after "Explore by use case," before "One integrated platform"** (front-page.php, `<section id="deliveryModel">`). Rationale: hero establishes the value prop, Use Cases establishes relevance ("is this for me?"), and Delivery Model immediately answers a question enterprise buyers (retail ops directors, facilities managers, council procurement per PROJECT.md §1's audience) ask early — "how would this actually get delivered to my organisation?" — building execution-capability trust before the platform-feature pitch (One Integrated Platform, traffic-demo widget, Why Choose ITOI) goes deeper. Placing it later (e.g. next to Why Choose ITOI) would read more like a reassurance/proof-point, which isn't quite right for a logistics/procurement-flavoured section.
- Content: real copy from the client's deck, exactly as given (Supply/Stage/Install/Handover/Manage/SLA + eyebrow/headline), stored as an ACF repeater (`delivery_model_steps`, min/max 6 rows — number/step_name/description all separately editable) on a **new dedicated "Delivery Model" options page** (`acf-json/group_13112f7e3503.json`, registered in `inc/acf.php`) — matches the existing "Find Your Fit Settings" precedent (its own options page) rather than the generic Flexible Content `page_builder` field, since that field is registered but never actually rendered by `front-page.php` (confirmed by grep — nothing in the homepage template calls `get_field('page_builder', ...)`; it's real infrastructure for other pages, not wired to the homepage in this build).
- **Gotcha, caught by testing not assumed:** ACF repeater `default_value` does NOT auto-hydrate through `get_field()` the way plain text-field defaults do — confirmed by curling the homepage right after committing the JSON: the eyebrow/headline (plain text fields) rendered their defaults fine, but the 6-step repeater rendered zero rows. Seeded the real data via `wp eval` + ACF's own `update_field()` (writes the correct underlying option rows/reference keys, not a raw DB hack) — re-curled afterward, all 6 steps present with correct numbers/names.
- **Animation grammar — deliberately reused, not invented,** per the task's explicit instruction to match the Find Your Fit quiz's progress-dot mechanism: the literal component that has a stroke-dashoffset "ring-fill" is actually the (now-removed) hero dot-nav, not the Find Your Fit modal's own progress dots (those are plain `.finder-dot` background-color toggles, confirmed by reading `src/tailwind.css` directly rather than trusting the task's naming). Reused what's actually analogous and available: the text fade/rise is the exact `itoi-reveal` opacity+transform/`0.6s ease` pair already used for every other scroll-reveal on this page; the connecting-line draw-in reuses the (removed) ring-fill's "static track + animated fill" concept, expressed as a plain absolutely-positioned div pair (`.delivery-connector-track` dashed/dim, `.delivery-connector-fill` `scaleY(0)→scaleY(1)`, `transform-origin: top`) since a straight vertical segment doesn't need an SVG circle.
- **Color: used `var(--signature)` (navy), not literal amber,** even though the task's own wording says "circle fills solid amber." PROJECT.md §3 confirms `--signature` was migrated from a placeholder amber to brand navy on 2026-07-22, and CLAUDE.md's hard rules explicitly forbid reintroducing amber anywhere. Read as stale terminology from before that migration, not a real instruction to regress the design system — flagging explicitly since it's a case where I overrode the task's literal word choice based on the current, more authoritative spec.
- **Layout — no JS measurement needed, responsive by construction:** each `.delivery-step` is a 2-column CSS grid (rail | body); the rail column is stretched by the grid's default `align-items: stretch` to match the row's real height (set by the body's actual text length), so each step's `.delivery-connector` (`flex: 1 1 auto`) fills exactly the gap down to the next circle regardless of description length or viewport width — confirmed working correctly at both 375px and 1440px without any special-casing.
- **IntersectionObserver — six independent activations, not one all-or-nothing reveal:** one observer watching all six `.delivery-step` elements, `rootMargin: '-45% 0px -45% 0px'` (a thin band at the viewport's vertical center, matching the existing longform-scrollspy's approach in `main.js` rather than inventing a new rootMargin convention), each entry `unobserve()`'d after activating so it can't reverse on scroll-up. Activating step N also marks step (N-1)'s connector active (the "line above" the newly-active step lives in the previous step's own markup, connecting down to it).
- **Real testing bug caught and fixed in the verification script itself, not the feature:** the first Puppeteer verification used a single instant `scrollTo()` jump to the mid-scroll target and only ever saw ONE step active (the target step), which looked like the "stays activated" behavior was broken — actually a test artifact: an instant jump lets the browser skip straight to the final scroll position without the IntersectionObserver ever registering intersection for the steps in between. Rewrote the script to scroll in 40 small increments (matching how a real user scrolls); re-ran and got the expected `[true,true,true,false,false,false]` for both breakpoints when stopped at step 3, confirmed unchanged after scrolling back to the top (scroll-up persistence working as specified).
- **Real accessibility bug found via axe, not guessed, and fixed:** applying the task's literal "~40–50% opacity at rest" to the full `.delivery-body` (headline + description together) passed a real `axe-core` `color-contrast` check on the headline (20px/700-weight qualifies as WCAG "large text," 3:1 threshold, cleared comfortably) but **failed it on the description** (regular-weight ~15.5px, bound by the stricter 4.5:1 threshold — measured 1.9:1, `serious` impact, 6 nodes, one per step). Fixed by keeping the opacity dim on the headline only (verified it stays ≥3:1 at 0.5 opacity) and leaving the description at its normal `var(--text-muted)` full-opacity color always (already the site's standard de-emphasized body-copy tone, ~5.3:1, legible in both rest and active states). Re-ran axe after the fix: **0 violations at both 375px and 1440px** (was 1 `serious` violation × 6 nodes at each width before).
- **`.no-detect-reveal` applied defensively, structurally redundant but added anyway per the established pattern:** this section never uses `.itoi-reveal`/`itoi_reveal_class()`/`itoi_reveal_markup()` at all, so it's already outside `initRevealObserver()`'s `.itoi-reveal:not(.no-detect-reveal)` selector by construction — the same "opt-in only, nothing gets the corner-bracket/confidence-tag treatment unless explicitly given `.itoi-reveal`" situation as the single-industry.php photo-hero funnel branch noted earlier in this file. Added the `.no-detect-reveal` marker on the section wrapper anyway as the same documented "never add reveal here" belt-and-suspenders layer used on the platform-demo teaser and interaction cards elsewhere on this page, specifically because this is a brand-new use of scroll-triggered animation and the corner-bracket bug has now recurred three times on unrelated elements. Confirmed via rendered-HTML grep: `no-detect-reveal` present exactly once on `#deliveryModel`, zero `itoi-reveal` occurrences anywhere inside it.
- Mobile (375px) verified directly, not assumed "naturally fine": screenshotted the progressive mid-scroll state and the reduced-motion full state at 375px — vertical layout, activation-zone timing, and text wrapping all read cleanly; no special mobile-only CSS was needed beyond what the grid/flex layout already provides.
- `prefers-reduced-motion`: mirrors `initRevealObserver()`'s exact pattern — short-circuits before creating the observer, adds `is-active` to every step + connector immediately, no partial state ever rendered. A CSS-level `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` override backs this up too (same belt-and-suspenders approach as `.itoi-reveal`'s own reduced-motion block), so the final state holds even if JS somehow didn't run. Verified via Puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures` at both breakpoints: all 6 steps + all 5 connectors report `is-active` immediately on load, no scrolling.

**Verification:** `php -l` clean on every touched PHP file; `node -c` clean on `main.js`; `npm run build` clean (one CSS syntax error caught and fixed along the way — an accidental literal `*/` inside a comment's prose, `.finder-*/.itoi-reveal`, closed the comment early and broke the whole stylesheet parse; caught immediately by the build failing, not shipped). `debug.log` diffed before/after: zero new entries traceable to this session's edits (the only new lines are the same pre-existing `post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` textdomain-timing notices every other session this month has also logged as unrelated). `axe-core` on the homepage: 0 violations at both 375px/1440px (after the contrast fix above). Screenshots: hero before/after at both breakpoints (`temporary-screenshots/315-hero-before-*`, `9-hero-final-*`), Delivery Model mid-scroll partial-activation at both breakpoints (`delivery-midscroll-*`), reduced-motion full-activated state at both breakpoints (`delivery-reducedmotion-*`) — all via a scratch verification script, `scripts/_delivery-model-verify-temp.js` (kept, not deleted, matching the existing `_edu-hub-verify-temp.js` precedent for task-scoped scripts).

**Also found, unrelated to this task, flagged not fixed:** `git fsck` shows 43 of 454 loose objects in `.git/objects/` are corrupt (`inflate: data stream error`) — `git log`/`git status` still work but `git diff` fails outright. Pre-existing (also independently flagged in the 2026-07-17 Phase 4 entry above, still unresolved), not caused by this session, no repair attempted (data-loss risk, needs the user's go-ahead).

## 2026-07-23 — Solutions restructure, Steps 3–6 (redirects, callout, SEO metadata, PROJECT.md rewrite) — checkpoint, restructure now fully live

**Scope:** continuation of the same-day Step 2 session, user said "fix and continue" in response to the flagged gap (old solution URLs 404ing). Completed Step 3 (redirects), the still-outstanding cross-link callout from the original smart-security split resolution, a final Step 4 sweep, Step 5 (SEO metadata), and Step 6 (PROJECT.md rewrite).

**Step 3 — redirects, `inc/redirects.php`:** first checked actual current behaviour before assuming all 7 old slugs were broken — WP core's native `wp_old_slug_redirect()` already auto-redirects 5 of 7 (the in-place post renames) via the `_wp_old_slug` postmeta WP writes automatically on a published post's slug change. Only `facial-recognition` and `cleaning-robots` (trashed/merged, not renamed) genuinely 404'd. Added all 7 explicitly to `inc/redirects.php` anyway rather than relying on the implicit core behaviour, plus updated the pre-existing bare legacy-URL redirects (`smart-security`, `liquor-management`, etc. — pre-dating the `/solutions/` prefix) to point straight at the final new-category destination instead of the now-dead intermediate old slug. **Own mistake caught and fixed before it shipped:** first edit accidentally replaced the bare legacy keys with `/solutions/{slug}/`-prefixed ones instead of adding the new ones alongside the old — would have silently broken the original pre-launch redirects. Caught by rereading the diff, not by testing after the fact. Final map has 16 entries, all verified via `curl -w "%{redirect_url}"` — every one 301s to the correct single final URL, no double-hops.

**Cross-link callout (from the original explicit smart-security-split resolution, not yet built as of yesterday's Step 2):** added to `single-solution.php`, gated on `post_name === 'security-access-inventory'` (slug check, not ID, so it survives a future content edit), linking to `cctv-video-loss-prevention` via a live `get_page_by_path()` lookup rather than a hardcoded URL. Confirmed via rendered-HTML grep: present once on security-access-inventory, absent everywhere else including the CCTV page itself.

**Step 4 final sweep:** grepped the whole theme again for the 7 old slugs as literal strings in PHP/JS/ACF-JSON — zero remaining outside `inc/redirects.php` itself. Fixed the 2 loose-prose lines flagged (not fixed) in yesterday's Step 1 inventory — `page-glossary.php`/`page-education.php` no longer say "retail-analytics and security," now "analytics, security and operations."

**Step 5 — SEO metadata:** wrote real, search-intent-phrased Yoast title/description for all 8 pages directly to `_yoast_wpseo_title`/`_yoast_wpseo_metadesc` postmeta (e.g. security-access-inventory's title is "Security & Access Control Systems," not the internal category label verbatim). **Gotcha caught:** this Yoast install (14+) caches computed SEO output in a `wp_yoast_indexable` table separate from the raw postmeta — writing postmeta alone risked serving a stale cached title/description. Cleared the 8 posts' existing indexable rows so the new postmeta gets picked up fresh; confirmed via a direct `<title>`/`<meta name="description">` grep on all 8 rendered pages, not assumed from the write step alone.

**Step 6 — PROJECT.md §4/§5 rewrite:** §4's `solution` CPT entry now documents the current 8-slug structure as the locked structure, with the old 7-slug list kept directly underneath marked "Superseded 2026-07-23" (not deleted) plus the full old→new mapping and rationale, consistent with the document's own existing pattern for superseded decisions (§1's prior-design-doc note, the CTA-color correction in §3). Added the sensory-intelligence disclaimer as a documented hard content rule (not just something living in this session's memory) so a future session enforces it too. §5's redirect note updated with the real current mapping and the `inc/redirects.php` deviation-from-spec note (previously undocumented in PROJECT.md itself, only in NOTES.md).

Find Your Fit `fyf_solution_routes`: populated all 5 previously-empty rows with the confirmed need→category mapping (a genuine fix — this had never resolved a real result before).

**Discovered mid-migration, not present during the 2026-07-22 Step 1 inventory:** the `guide` CPT had grown from 5 posts to **15** overnight (10 new guides exist that weren't there yesterday — outside this session's own edits, cause unconfirmed). 2 of the 15 pointed `related_solution` at facial-recognition (23483), which this session trashed — caught via a full post-migration verification pass (not assumed), fixed by repointing both to 23485/security-access-inventory. Also ran a DB-wide grep for any remaining reference to 23483/23487 after the fact: the only survivors were the 2 nav menu items already being replaced in this same session (see below) — nothing else missed.

**Two real bugs hit and fixed during this session's own migration scripts, logged since they're non-obvious WP-CLI/PHP gotchas worth remembering:**
1. `wp-cli eval-file` executes the file's contents via `eval()` inside a method of WP-CLI's `EvalFile_Command` class, not in PHP's true global scope — so a top-level variable in the eval'd file is *not* reachable from a nested `function` via `global $varname`, even though it looks identical to normal top-level/global code. A helper function (`itoi_remap_solution_list()`) relying on `global $ACCESS, $WORKOPS, $CCTV` silently got `null` for all three, corrupting several industries' `longform_solutions`/`related_solutions` arrays with cast-to-string `""` entries (which ACF's relationship-field save then round-tripped into literal `0`s). Caught by a full post-migration re-read (not assumed correct from the write step's own echoed output) and fixed with a second pass that filters both `''` and `'0'` before rebuilding each array. Lesson: inside a `wp-cli eval-file` script, pass values as function parameters, don't rely on `global` to reach top-level variables.
2. `wp_update_nav_menu_item( $menu_id, $existing_item_id, $args )` does **not** merge `$args` with the item's current state — any field left out of `$args` gets reset to WP core's hardcoded default (`menu-item-parent-id` defaults to `0`, `menu-item-object`/`type` to `'custom'`, object-id to `0`), even when only trying to change the title. A retitle-only call on 5 existing nav items silently detached them from their "Solutions" parent and blanked their post binding, turning them into 5 dead top-level nav entries. Caught immediately by a follow-up verification query (not assumed) and fixed by re-issuing the call with the full field set (title + object + object-id + type + parent-id) every time, not just the field being changed.

**Verification performed (not assumed):** `php -l` clean on all 3 edited templates (header.php, footer.php, front-page.php); full DB re-read confirming all 8 solution posts' final slug/status/content counts, all 42 use_cases rows' final solution IDs + resolved titles/statuses, all 15 guides' `related_solution` resolving to a `publish` post, the sensory-intelligence disclaimer appearing exactly 4 times in rendered HTML, the 2 confirmed-real facial-recognition stats appearing on security-access-inventory's rendered HTML, a DB-wide grep confirming zero remaining references to the 2 trashed post IDs outside what this session itself was actively fixing; HTTP 200 on the homepage, `/solutions/`, all 8 new solution URLs, `/industries/retail/`, `/education/guides/`, `/education/faq/`; `debug.log` diffed before/after — zero new entries beyond pre-existing, already-flagged noise (post-smtp/wordpress-seo textdomain notices, `remove-wp-branding` plugin warning). **Not done:** Lighthouse/axe, Puppeteer screenshot comparison at 375/1440px, click-testing the Find Your Fit quiz end-to-end — deferred to the task's own Verification phase once Step 3 (redirects) and Step 4 (remaining downstream references) land, since screenshotting mid-restructure with old URLs still 404ing isn't representative.

## 2026-07-22 — Education Hub, Part 1 of rollout: architecture + Retail (5 guides, 21 glossary terms, 28 FAQs)

**Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md first per instruction.** New content build, out-of-sequence from PROJECT.md §10's phase list (same pattern as the platform-demo feature and the industry-page rollout before it) — a large "Education Hub" section (Guides, Glossary, searchable FAQ) spanning all 7 industries, explicitly scoped this session to **architecture + Retail only**, stopping for confirmation before the remaining 6 industries, matching the discipline already established by the industry-page rollout (one at a time, confirm before next).

**Architecture decisions, logged per the task's own explicit instruction:**

1. **`glossary_term` is a CPT, not an Options Page repeater.** The task left this as a judgment call. Chose a CPT because `related_guides` needs to be a real relationship field pointing at actual `guide` posts — an Options Page repeater can't hold a `relationship` sub-field the same way. Registered `public => false`, `publicly_queryable => false`, `rewrite => false` — the exact same "pure data, no single-post URL" pattern already established for `client`/`team_member` (PROJECT.md §4's own precedent), since the spec calls for one alphabetical `/education/glossary/` listing page, not a URL per term.
2. **`guide` is a normal public CPT**, rewrite/archive both nested at `education/guides` (not a bare `guides` slug) so it sits under the Education Hub landing page without colliding with it — confirmed this coexistence works cleanly (WP resolves the more specific CPT rewrite correctly; no conflict with the `/education/` Page).
3. **Guide's `industry` field is a single Post Object (`post_type=industry`, `allow_null=1`), not a multi-value `relationship`.** The spec's own wording ("relationship → industry CPT, or general for cross-industry guides") describes a single-industry-or-none relationship, not a multi-select — a Post Object with null-as-General is the simpler, more accurate fit. Same reasoning for `related_solution` (single Post Object, not a multi relationship) since a guide realistically points at one primary solution.
4. **`guide.title`/`glossary_term.term` mirror the Industry CPT's own existing `name`-field-with-post_title-fallback pattern** (`get_field('name') ?: get_the_title()`, already used throughout `archive-industry.php`) rather than inventing a new convention — same `get_field('title') ?: get_the_title()` / `get_field('term') ?: get_the_title()` fallback used everywhere in the new templates.
5. **FAQ reuses the existing `faqs` repeater on the `solution` CPT — no new CPT, per the task's explicit instruction.** `/education/faq/` aggregates via a small query helper (`inc/education.php`, `itoi_edu_get_all_faqs()`) that walks every published solution and skips any with an empty repeater — a genuinely empty group is simply absent, never a broken/empty section (CLAUDE.md's "empty states are real states" rule).
6. **Nav: added "Education Hub" as a new 6th top-level primary-menu item, not a child of the existing "Resources" grouping.** Checked `header.php` before deciding — the full-screen mega menu only renders **top-level** menu items (`menu_item_parent == 0`); a parent's children (the existing Resources → Insights/About/Team structure) aren't rendered as a visible sub-list anywhere in that component, they only affect the parent's fallback link target. Nesting under Resources would have made Education Hub technically present in the menu tree but **not actually visible** anywhere on the rendered site — directly contradicting the task's explicit "genuinely visible... not buried" requirement. Added as item 06 (after Resources) via `wp menu item add-custom` + a matching row appended to the `mega_menu_previews` repeater on Site Settings (position-matched to the new item's index, same mechanism every other nav item already uses) — confirmed visible via screenshot, correct numbered index, correct preview swap on hover.

**Real data gap found before building Part 3, surfaced to the user rather than guessed past:** every one of the 7 `solution` posts' `faqs` repeater was completely empty (confirmed via direct postmeta inspection, not just an empty-looking page) — matching a comment already in `single-solution.php` ("every solution currently has zero FAQs sourced from the live site"). Since the task's own aggregation + working-search deliverable can't be demonstrated against zero rows, asked the user directly rather than silently building an empty shell or silently inventing content. **User chose: write real FAQs now.** Wrote 4 grounded FAQs per solution (28 total) — every answer sourced directly from that solution's own existing `dek`/`narrative`/`features`/`specs` copy already in the database, populated via `update_field()` (not raw postmeta) for repeater safety.

**A real bug found during verification, not just visually inspected — full root-cause, not a workaround:** the shared filter-as-you-type mechanism (Part 4) initially toggled Tailwind utility classes (`opacity-0`/`max-h-0`/`pointer-events-none`) on hidden items, while the item's own static template classes included the "visible" counterparts (`opacity-100`, `max-h-[600px]`). `classList.toggle()` only manages the one class named, so both the static and dynamic classes ended up present simultaneously on a hidden item — and Tailwind's generated stylesheet orders `.opacity-0`/`.opacity-100` (and different arbitrary `max-h-*` values) by its own internal ordering, not by which was added to the DOM last, so the **static** class silently won the cascade every time. Net effect: the DOM/class state was correct (confirmed via a Puppeteer assertion checking `classList.contains('opacity-0')`, which reported the right count) while the **rendered page showed zero visual filtering at all** — caught only because a screenshot was actually inspected, not just the count assertion. A second layer of the same bug: even after first fixing it by removing the competing static classes, `max-height:0` still couldn't collapse a hidden item below its own static `py-5`/`py-4` padding (border-box `max-height` can't shrink a box below its padding), leaving ~40px-tall "hidden" rows and a large blank gap above the real result. **Fixed at the root, not patched around:** rewrote `initItoiFilterLists()` (`assets/js/main.js`) to use inline `style.opacity`/`style.display` instead of any toggled Tailwind class — inline style always wins the cascade, so there is no class to collide with; hidden items fade via `transition-opacity` (a static, non-conflicting class) then get `display:none` after 200ms (skipped straight to `display:none` under `prefers-reduced-motion`). Re-verified via direct `getBoundingClientRect()` checks (hidden items now genuinely 0px, not ~40px) and full before/after screenshots at both breakpoints.

**No invented statistics anywhere in this session's content — checked, not assumed.** Every FAQ answer traces to that solution's own existing real copy already in the CPT. Every guide claim is either a generic, non-numeric industry statement (e.g. "conversion rate is a widely-used retail metric") or a real figure already in the `retail-analytics` solution's own `specs` field (the 95%+ accuracy figure, its own repeater data) — the two PROJECT.md §6 confirmed figures (99.87% LFW, sub-100ms) belong to Facial Recognition and were correctly *not* used in any Retail guide, since Retail Analytics is a different solution with its own real, separately-sourced figures. No "X% of retailers report Y"-style invented statistic anywhere.

**Built:**
- `inc/post-types.php` — `guide`, `glossary_term` CPT registrations.
- `acf-json/group_b295a4c975ec.json` (Guide), `acf-json/group_7e5d515c4ec7.json` (Glossary Term).
- `inc/education.php` (new, registered in `functions.php`) — `itoi_edu_get_glossary_terms()`, `itoi_edu_get_all_faqs()` shared query helpers.
- `page-education.php` (hub landing — intro, 3 section cards with live counts, a combined quick-search over every guide title + glossary term + FAQ question using the same shared filter pattern), `archive-guide.php` (industry filter pills, same mechanic as `page-customers.php`'s category filter, inline styles for hide/show), `single-guide.php`, `page-glossary.php`, `page-faq.php` (grouped by solution, group hides itself when all its items are filtered out).
- `assets/js/main.js` — `initItoiFilterLists()` (shared filter-as-you-type, built once, applied to Glossary/FAQ/hub quick-search per Part 4), `initGuideFilter()` (Guides index industry pills).
- 3 new WP Pages: Education Hub (`/education/`), Glossary (`/education/glossary/`, child page), FAQ (`/education/faq/`, child page) — template picked up automatically via the existing `page-{slug}.php` convention (`page-about.php`/`page-customers.php` etc.), no explicit template assignment needed.
- Nav: "Education Hub" top-level menu item + `mega_menu_previews` row (see architecture point 6).

**Content populated (Retail scope only, per explicit instruction not to write all 7 industries in one pass):**
- 5 Retail guides, 545–591 words each (within the 500-800 word target): "What is Retail Analytics?", "How Foot Traffic Analytics Improves Sales", "People Counting vs. Retail Analytics", "How to Measure Retail Conversion Rate", "Retail Analytics KPIs Every Manager Should Track" — each links to `/solutions/retail-analytics/`, each with the title's target phrase worked into the first paragraph and a subheading, "How to Measure Retail Conversion Rate" also links to the homepage's platform-demo section by name (no deep-link exists into the JS modal itself, so linked to the homepage with descriptive text rather than fabricating an anchor).
- 21 glossary terms (the task's full cross-industry list — counted 21 distinct entries, not 20, since "False Positive / False Negative" is one combined item in the task's own list), built complete in this pass per the task's explicit instruction (not Retail-scoped). Cross-linked to a guide only where one genuinely covers that concept (6 of 21 terms link out; the other 15 — security/access-control/robot-patrol terms — correctly have no related guide yet, since none of the 5 Retail guides cover them, and forcing a weak link would be worse than none).
- 28 FAQs across all 7 solutions (see data-gap note above).

**A real, unrelated bug hit and fixed along the way:** the guide/glossary population scripts set an explicit `post_date` slightly ahead of the sandbox's actual current time (a timezone assumption that didn't hold), which silently made `wp_insert_post(..., 'post_status' => 'publish')` schedule every post as `future` instead of publishing it — WordPress's own documented behavior when `post_date` is ahead of now, not a bug in this theme's code. Caught because the Guides archive and Glossary page rendered as empty even after population; root-caused via `wp post list --fields=post_status`, fixed by bulk-updating all 26 posts to `publish` with a corrected `post_date`/`post_date_gmt`.

**Verification (all re-run after the filter-mechanism fix, not before):** `php -l` clean on every touched file, `node -c` clean, `npm run build` clean. `debug.log`: zero new entries from any new code across every page load this session (confirmed by diffing against the pre-existing plugin-level noise already flagged in every prior NOTES.md entry — WP core widget/customizer warnings, `login_headertitle` deprecation, `ssh-sftp-updater-support` plugin warnings, the pre-existing `single-industry.php` funnel-variable notice, none of which trace to this session's files). axe: **0 violations**, both widths, all 5 new page types (Hub, Guides index, a guide detail page, Glossary, FAQ), re-checked after the filter fix. Lighthouse (desktop): **100/100/78/100** across all 5 page types — Best Practices 78 is the same sandbox-wide HTTPS-only cap flagged in every prior session's Lighthouse run, not new; SEO briefly dipped to 92 on the Guides pages (missing meta description — Yoast auto-registers new CPTs with an empty `metadesc` template) and was fixed by configuring `metadesc-guide`/`metadesc-ptarchive-guide` in Yoast's own settings, per PROJECT.md §8 ("configure, don't hand-roll"), not a template workaround. Functional filter tests via Puppeteer (not just visual inspection): Guides index filtered to Retail (5→5, all 5 guides are currently Retail-only, correctly not 0); Glossary searched "conversion" → 21 visible items collapse correctly to 1 ("Conversion Rate"), confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the 20 hidden items are genuinely 0px, not just class-flagged; FAQ searched "pour" → 28 questions collapse to the 2 real Liquor Management questions, and the other 6 solution groups correctly disappear entirely (group-hide-when-empty logic). Screenshots at 375px/1440px: Hub landing, Guides index (default + filtered to Retail), all 5 guide detail pages, Glossary (default + searched), FAQ (default + searched), plus the mega menu open state confirming "Education Hub" renders as a genuinely visible, correctly-indexed top-level item — all in `temporary-screenshots/`, kept (not cleared) since they're this checkpoint's own requested review evidence.

**Rollout plan for the remaining 6 industries, flagged per the task's explicit either/or:** chosen to **propose each industry's 5 guide topics myself for approval**, rather than waiting on the user to supply them — matches the working pattern already established across this project's industry-page rollout (Hospitality through Casinos & Gaming), where solution/use-case pairings were proposed with reasoning and flagged for review rather than asked about one at a time. **Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md first per instruction.** Task: replace the "Live Detection" signature color sitewide from placeholder amber to ITOI's real brand navy, sampled from the actual logo — explicitly flagged by the task as a legibility problem, not a value swap, since navy (unlike amber) is itself a dark color and would go dark-on-dark against the site's several dark-background contexts (hero scrim, teal-900 sections, dark modals).

**Final token values:**
```
--signature:        #004B89  (was #FF8A1E) — light-background instances, ~8.9:1 on white
--signature-dim:    #003665  (was #B85F0E) — darker navy for hover/pressed on light backgrounds, ~11-12:1 on white/hero-bg
--signature-bright: #5AACFF  (new token)   — dark-background instances, ~7.9:1 on ink, ~5.2:1 on teal-900, ~8.1:1 on the hero scrim
--signature-glow:   rgba(0, 75, 137, 0.10) (was rgba(255,138,30,0.16)) — see the contrast-driven opacity reduction below
```
`--signature-bright` chosen over the task's own example `#4A9FE8` after computing both against teal-900 specifically (the "Why Choose ITOI" section, one of the four named dark-background spots): `#4A9FE8` measures 4.35:1 there — failing WCAG AA by a hair — while `#5AACFF` measures 5.16:1, passing with real margin. Computed every contrast ratio before choosing a value, not eyeballed.

**The dual-token light/dark split, audited instance-by-instance against the actual rendered site (not assumed from component names) — this took most of the session's time.** Went through every CSS rule and every Tailwind utility-class usage referencing `--signature`/`--signature-dim`/`--signature-glow` across `src/tailwind.css`, `tailwind.config.js`, and every `.php`/`.js` file, and for each one, loaded the real page and checked what it actually sits on:

- **Switched to `--signature-bright` (dark backgrounds):** the mega-hero's detection-box borders + "PERSON XX%" tags and LIVE indicator dot (`assets/js/main.js`, `front-page.php` — sits on the hero's dark gradient + scrim); the full-screen mega menu's numbered index labels, eyebrow text, and logo dot (`header.php` — `bg-teal-900`); the "Why ITOI" long-form grid's six SVG icons (`inc/longform-icons.php` — same `bg-teal-900`); the industry-page photo-hero's "INDUSTRY / [Name]" eyebrow (`single-industry.php` — sits on the dark photo scrim); the floating "Find Your Fit" trigger button's dot (`footer.php` — the button itself is a solid `bg-ink` fill); the scroll-triggered detection-reveal's corner brackets + confidence tag, but *only* where that reveal happens to wrap a `bg-teal-900` section (the homepage's "Why teams choose ITOI" headline is the only such instance found — a `.bg-teal-900 .itoi-reveal-bracket`/`.itoi-reveal-tag` CSS override handles this without touching the shared component's markup, since every other instance of the same reveal component is on a light background); `:focus-visible` gets a scoped override to bright inside the two permanent dark containers (`.mega-menu`, `#megaHero`) and the dark-filled Find-Your-Fit trigger button, while the sitewide default stays the light-background token.
- **Corrected two of the task's own stated assumptions after checking the real render — flagging this explicitly since the task anticipated it might happen:** the "Find Your Fit" modal's progress dots and the platform-demo modal's active-tab underline were both named by the task as dark-background instances needing the bright variant. Checked both directly: `.finder-card` and `.pd-modal` are each a solid `background: #fff` card floating over a dark *overlay* (the scrim behind the card, not the card itself) — so these are genuinely light-background instances and correctly kept the plain `--signature`/`--signature-dim` tokens. Confirmed visually (screenshots) and via computed style (`rgb(0, 75, 137)` on both, not the bright variant).
- **Everything else** (all interactive-mechanic active-states across all 7 industry pages — Hospitality's stage pills, Banking's scenario cards/result icon/flag badge, Government's checkboxes/chart bars, Logistics's event dots/gap labels/drag handle, Stadiums's density markers, Casinos's zone selector, the Retail funnel's slider/values, the platform-demo's bars/dots/risk-bar, the header/footer logo dots, the sticky long-form sub-nav's active-link state) sits on light (white or `bg-hero-bg`) surfaces and correctly kept the plain `--signature`/`--signature-dim` tokens.

**A second, related legibility problem found during the audit, not just the light/dark split: several elements use the signature color as a solid FILL with `color: var(--ink)` text on top.** Ink-on-amber (the original pairing) measured a fine 8.02:1; ink-on-navy measures **2.13:1**, badly failing — computed before touching any code, not assumed. Every such instance found and switched from ink to white text (white-on-navy is 8.87:1): `.hospitality-stage-pill.is-active`, `.funnel-marker--qualified::after` (the Retail funnel's checkmark badge), `.banking-result-icon`, `.banking-flag-badge`, `.logistics-gap-label--with`, `.logistics-handle-grip`, `.pd-risk-bar.med` (platform-demo). The inverse pairing also needed checking: where `--signature-bright` is used as a fill (the dark-context `.itoi-reveal-tag` override), ink text is correct there (7.91:1) and white would fail (2.39:1) — the two tokens need opposite text-color pairings, not the same one.

**A real, axe-caught regression found and fixed during verification, not just assumed passing:** `.banking-scenario-card.is-active`/`.government-checkbox-label.is-active`/`.casino-zone.is-active` all use `--signature-glow` as a soft active-state background wash. The straight value swap (same 0.16 opacity, new navy RGB) passed visually but **axe caught a real contrast failure**: navy's blue channel contributes far less light at a given alpha than amber's did (amber kept its red channel at full 255 against a white base; navy's is 0), so the composited wash came out measurably darker than before — muted-gray description text sitting on it dropped to 4.11:1, failing WCAG AA 4.5:1, on Banking's active scenario card specifically (axe: `color-contrast`, 1 violation, both widths). Fixed by reducing `--signature-glow`'s opacity to 0.10 (composited contrast ~4.58:1, re-verified via axe: 0 violations after). This is exactly the "legibility issue, not just a value swap" the task warned about, showing up somewhere the task didn't explicitly name.

**Hardcoded amber-hex instances found and fixed — 8 total, comprehensive codebase search (not just the components the task named):**
1. `src/tailwind.css` — the three token definitions themselves (`--signature`, `--signature-dim`, `--signature-glow`), which is the intended base-layer change.
2. `front-page.php:141` — `accent-[#FF8A1E]` hardcoded directly on the traffic-widget's own slider input, bypassing the token entirely → `accent-signature`.
3. `src/tailwind.css` — `.logistics-timeline-track--with { background: rgba(255,138,30,0.4) }` → `rgba(0,75,137,0.4)`.
4. `src/tailwind.css` — `.stadium-marker { background: rgba(255,138,30,0.15) }` → `rgba(0,75,137,0.15)`.
5–8. **`#9c520d`, a close-variant hex found in 6 places** (`single-industry.php` ×5: Hospitality's stage-label prefix and completeness %, Logistics's "With ITOI" label, Stadium's slider value, Casino's "Related solution" link; `front-page.php` ×1: the traffic widget's "Live Detection — illustrative" label) — this isn't one of the two hexes named in the task, but it's a manually-picked WCAG-workaround for `--signature-dim` introduced across earlier sessions specifically *because* the amber token (#B85F0E) failed contrast at small bold sizes and needed a manually-darkened stand-in. Now that `--signature-dim` is a real navy with 11-12:1 contrast (no workaround needed), replaced all 6 with the proper `text-signature-dim` utility class, finally retiring that long-running workaround.

No other hardcoded amber/near-amber values found anywhere in `.php`/`.js`/`.css` sources after a comprehensive `grep` sweep (`FF8A1E`, `B85F0E`, `9c520d`, `rgba(255, 138, 30...)` all return zero matches outside the compiled `assets/css/style.css`, which rebuilds clean from source).

**Deliberately left unchanged — functional identifiers, not color values or copy:** the `'amber'` string keys/CSS classes driving the platform-demo's highlighted-bar/dot logic (`.pd-bar.amber`, `.pd-mini-dots .pd-d.amber`, `$amber_index` parameter, `array(6 => 'amber')` calls in `template-parts/platform-demo-modal.php`), and `.funnel-zone-value--amber`. These are semantic identifiers wired through JS/PHP logic, not hex values or prose — renaming them is a separate, purely-cosmetic refactor the task didn't ask for (item 5 was scoped to "hardcoded hex values," item 6 to "comments, copy, or documentation"), and doing it now would risk breaking working conditional logic for no functional benefit. Flagging as a minor naming mismatch (these components are now navy, not amber) in case a follow-up rename pass is wanted.

**Comments/documentation updated — design-rule language, not historical records:** every code comment describing the CURRENT design rule using "amber," "signal amber," or "REC light"-style language across `src/tailwind.css`, `tailwind.config.js`, `header.php`, `footer.php`, `front-page.php`, `single-industry.php`, `assets/js/main.js`, `inc/reveal.php`, `inc/longform-icons.php`, **CLAUDE.md**, and **PROJECT.md** (§3's color token block, the mega-hero/traffic-widget mechanic descriptions) — updated to describe the color as ITOI's brand navy, with the two-token light/dark split explained inline so a future editor doesn't reintroduce the pre-migration assumption. PROJECT.md's existing "primary CTA is black, not blue" correction note was extended with a clarifying sentence so it doesn't read as contradicted by `--signature` now also being blue — the CTA and signature-layer are two separate roles that must stay separate, addressed explicitly rather than left for a future reader to puzzle out.

**One historical bug-report comment deliberately left as-is** (`front-page.php`, the "One Integrated Platform" section): describes a real past incident where a nesting bug produced "a stray amber '99%' tag/corner-bracket floating outside the frame's real boundaries." That bug genuinely produced an amber artifact at the time it happened (before this migration) — rewriting it to say "navy" would misrepresent what was actually observed and debugged. Left alone on the same principle as NOTES.md's own append-only history: past entries describe what was true when written.

**No user-facing copy needed a rewrite — checked, not assumed.** Searched every page's actual rendered visible text (not just source) for "amber"/"REC light"/"signal color" language; zero matches anywhere on the site. Every "amber" reference found and changed was in a code comment, CLAUDE.md, or PROJECT.md — internal documentation, never copy a site visitor sees. Nothing flagged for your review on the content side.

**Verification:** `php -l` clean on every touched `.php` file, `node -c` clean on `assets/js/main.js`, `npm run build` clean, compiled `assets/css/style.css` re-confirmed to contain zero remaining amber hex values and the new `signature-bright` utilities present. `debug.log` clean before/after for every one of the 8 pages actually touched by this task (homepage + all 7 industries), verified by direct per-page before/after line-count testing — a handful of unrelated entries appeared in the log during this session (a `wp-login`-related deprecated-hook notice and a `str_replace()` null-parameter deprecation, both WordPress-core-level, both timestamped together outside any of my own page loads and not reproducible by re-running the exact same requests) that don't correlate with any page this task touched; left uninvestigated further as out of scope, consistent with this session's established note that concurrent background activity on this shared sandbox can independently touch debug.log. axe: 0 violations on the homepage and all 7 industry pages, checked in BOTH default and interacted/active states (Hospitality's active pills, Banking's alert state, Government's boosted checkboxes, Casinos's active zone with the solution link visible) — the interacted-state pass is what caught the glow-opacity regression above; a default-only check would have missed it. Screenshots captured at both 375px/1440px for every surface the task named: homepage hero (default + reveal-triggered), traffic widget, "Why Choose ITOI" section (including the scroll-reveal effect specifically), Find Your Fit modal (default + an option selected, confirming the progress-dot color), platform-demo modal (default + tab-switched), and all 7 industry pages' hero cards + their distinct interactive mechanics in an active/triggered state — every one confirmed visually legible against its actual background, not just passing an automated contrast check in isolation.

## 2026-07-22 — Homepage platform-demo feature: teaser + 6-tab interactive modal

**Scope:** out-of-sequence feature build, task-specified in full (not from PROJECT.md §10's phase list) — a browser-chrome-framed teaser in the "One integrated platform" homepage section, and a full interactive 6-tab dashboard modal behind its "Learn more"/play-button triggers. Ground truth was an attached static reference file, `platform-demo.html` — a working mockup with the exact tab structure, chart values, and CSS. Read PROJECT.md and CLAUDE.md first per instruction.

**Blocker at session start, resolved by the user:** the task referenced two attachments — `platform-demo.html` and a source screen recording — neither of which existed anywhere on the filesystem when the session began. Rather than guess at "ground truth" fidelity, asked the user to supply the files before building anything. The user then pasted the full `platform-demo.html` content directly into the conversation; the screen recording was never supplied, resolved as described below.

**Format decision — static image, not video (Part 1's judgment call):** chose a static screenshot over a muted looping video. Reasoning: (1) no actual screen recording was ever supplied, so a "video clip" would have meant fabricating dashboard footage rather than extracting a real frame; (2) since this session was already building the exact interactive demo described in `platform-demo.html` inside the real theme, the cleanest and most *accurate* option was to screenshot my own rendering of the real, live Operations tab (via Puppeteer, `.pd-body` element screenshot) and use that as the teaser image — more faithful than a stale/synthetic recording would have been, and it satisfies the spec's own suggestion ("the Operations tab view... is a good candidate since it shows a complete, uncluttered dashboard state") literally. That screenshot is saved at `assets/images/platform-demo-teaser.jpg` and used as the fallback whenever the new ACF field (below) is empty.

**ACF scoping decision (Part 5, logged explicitly per the task's own instruction):** the teaser media is a real ACF field — `platform_demo_teaser_image` (image), added to the existing "Site Settings" options page group (`acf-json/group_73b8c1766c9f.json`), following the same precedent as that group's existing `why_choose_photos` field (a homepage-section image living on Site Settings despite not being part of the unused Flexible Content Page Builder — see Phase 4 notes on that gap). The demo modal's 6 tabs' stat/chart/table values are **not** ACF-backed — left in template/JS logic (`inc/platform-demo.php` helpers + `template-parts/platform-demo-modal.php`), per the task's own explicit instruction that this is illustrative demo content, not real site copy, and an intentional, scoped exception to PROJECT.md's usual "everything through ACF" rule — not an oversight.

**Built:**
- `front-page.php` — "One integrated platform" section restructured (see bug fix below), teaser frame (chrome bar, 3 dots, image, centered play button), "Learn more" converted from a dead `<a href="#">` to a `<button>` (it now only opens the modal, so an anchor with no real destination was the wrong element).
- `template-parts/platform-demo-modal.php` (new) — overlay/modal/topbar/tabs/body structure, all 6 tab panels with every stat tile, chart, table, and risk-bar value from the spec, rendered server-side in PHP.
- `inc/platform-demo.php` (new) — small render helpers (`itoi_pd_stat_tile`, `itoi_pd_bars`, `itoi_pd_mini_dots`, `itoi_pd_risk_row`, `itoi_pd_heat_class`) shared across the 6 tabs' repeated markup patterns; registered in `functions.php`'s thin bootstrap.
- `assets/js/main.js` — `initPlatformDemo()`, added to the existing init dispatcher. Modal open/close **reuses the exact pattern already established for Find Your Fit** (`initFinder()`): `.open` class toggle + `document.body.style.overflow` lock, same function shape, per the task's explicit instruction to reuse rather than invent new modal logic. Also handles tab switching and the Staff Planning live calculator.
- `src/tailwind.css` — new `--teal-500` token (added to `tailwind.config.js` too) for the demo's internal data-viz secondary color, and a `pd-*` semantic-class block (matching the theme's existing per-feature-prefix convention — `.finder-*`, `.funnel-*`, `.banking-*`, etc.) for the dense data-UI chrome that doesn't map cleanly onto plain utilities (bars, heat-shading, risk bars, tab states).
- Weekly Traffic By Hour (Tab 2) and Past Performance — Traffic In (Tab 4) values are regenerated via `wp_rand()` server-side on every page load, satisfying "feels live" without needing client JS for it.

**Root-cause bug fix — recurring corner-bracket/"99%" tag bug, confirmed via full codebase audit:** the task described this as the second occurrence of a bug where the site-wide scroll-triggered `.itoi-reveal` decoration (amber corner brackets + confidence tag, `inc/reveal.php`) incorrectly applies to interactive components (first occurrence: the Retail industry funnel card). Investigation found the exclusion mechanism (`.no-detect-reveal`, paired with a `:not(.no-detect-reveal)` guard in `initRevealObserver()`, `assets/js/main.js`) **already exists and is already applied** to all 6 per-industry interactive hero cards from the 2026-07-21 rollout — so this session's job was applying the same established pattern to the new teaser, not inventing the mechanism. The actual bug: the "One integrated platform" section's headline/sub-copy wrapper already carried `itoi_reveal_class()`/`itoi_reveal_markup(11)` (index 11 → the "99%" tag, matching the task's own description exactly), and the new interactive teaser frame would have been nested inside that same wrapper had it been added carelessly. Fixed at the root by splitting the section: headline/sub-copy stay inside the `.itoi-reveal` wrapper (still get the passive scroll-reveal treatment, correctly, since they're not interactive), while the teaser frame + Learn More button are a **sibling** block marked `.no-detect-reveal`, never nested inside `.itoi-reveal`.
- **Site-wide audit performed, not assumed:** grepped every `itoi_reveal_class()`/`itoi_reveal_markup()` call site (`front-page.php`, `archive-industry.php`, `archive-solution.php`, `archive-case_study.php`, `archive-insight.php`, `single-case_study.php`, plus the 6 industry-page interactive cards in `single-industry.php`). Confirmed every other usage wraps genuinely passive content (section headers, archive tile links, case-study metric cards) — none of them are interactive tools, so none needed the exclusion. The homepage's traffic-demo widget (signature layer, interactive slider) was also checked and confirmed to already sit outside any `.itoi-reveal` wrapper. **The one-integrated-platform section was the only remaining unguarded instance** — now fixed.

**Real bugs found via testing, not just visual inspection — all fixed:**
1. **Table first-column mis-styling:** `.pd-data-table td:first-child` is styled as a muted/left-aligned label (correct for tables like Weekly Traffic By Hour, which have a genuine "Hour" label column) — but Tab 4's "Past Performance — Traffic In" table has no label column at all (every cell is a real Mon–Sun data value), so Monday's value was rendering grayed-out and left-aligned as if it were a label. Fixed with an opt-out modifier class, `.pd-data-table--uniform`, applied to that one table.
2. **WCAG AA color-contrast failures (axe-core, `serious`/`minor` impact), found by scanning each tab, not just the default one:** the spec's literal `#d9534f` red and `#1f9d55` green (stat-tile deltas) measure 3.96:1 / 3.49:1 against white — both fail the 4.5:1 threshold at this text size/weight. Also found: white text on the risk-bar "Medium" amber background (2.36:1) and on the "High" red background (3.96:1), and white text on the `pd-heat-4` teal-500 heat cells (2.75:1) — all serious failures. Fixed by darkening the red/green slightly (`#c34a47`/`#1a8548`, ~4.7-4.8:1, still recognizably the same colors) and switching the amber/teal-500 backgrounds to `--ink` text instead of white — the latter matches the site's own existing convention for amber backgrounds (`itoi-reveal-tag` already uses ink-on-amber, 8.02:1) rather than inventing a new pattern. PROJECT.md's WCAG 2.2 AA success criterion is non-negotiable, so the task's literal hex values were adjusted rather than shipped failing — full reasoning left in `src/tailwind.css` comments at each change.
3. **Empty table header (axe-core, `minor`):** the Performance tab's "This Period vs. Previous Period" table has a blank leading `<th>` above the row-label column — added `<span class="sr-only">Metric</span>` inside it (Tailwind's built-in utility, no custom CSS needed).
4. **Keyboard-inaccessible scrollable regions (axe-core, `serious`, `scrollable-region-focusable`):** the horizontally-scrollable tab bar (`.pd-tabs`) and every horizontally-scrollable wide-table wrapper, plus the modal's own vertically-scrolling `.pd-body`, had no way for a keyboard-only user to focus and then scroll them. Added `tabindex="0"` + `role="region"` + a descriptive `aria-label` to each (five elements total).
5. **Cross-feature bug: Find Your Fit's 3.5s auto-open timer vs. the new demo modal.** Both modals use `z-index: 100` and are `position: fixed`; Find Your Fit's overlay is later in the DOM (footer.php loads after front-page.php), so if its auto-open timer fired while a visitor had the platform-demo modal open past 3.5 seconds, it would silently stack on top and swallow the demo's own clicks — found via testing (tab-switching stopped working partway through a longer test run, traced to `elementFromPoint` returning `finder-opt`, not a `.pd-tab`, at the click coordinates). First fix attempt (retry-on-close polling) was worse — it made Find Your Fit pop up the instant a visitor closed the demo, i.e. immediate second-modal fatigue. Final fix in `initFinder()`: skip the auto-open outright if the demo modal is open at the 3.5s mark, and deliberately leave `sessionStorage.finderAutoShown` unset so it still gets a fresh try on the visitor's next pageview this session, rather than nagging them the moment they dismiss the demo.
6. **Minor, narrow-viewport-only:** `.pd-risk-bar`'s label text (e.g. "Overall Risk: Low" on a 24%-width bar) truncated at the narrowest phone widths — added a `@media (max-width:480px)` font-size reduction, matching the existing `.logistics-event-label` precedent for the same class of problem.

**Flagged, not fixed (pre-existing, out of scope):** a full-page axe scan (not scoped to the modal) surfaced a `color-contrast` finding on `.itoi-reveal-tag` (the decorative, `aria-hidden="true"` amber "99%"/"96%"/etc. tag used by the site-wide scroll-reveal effect) — but only intermittently, tied to the tag's own opacity fade-in transition at the exact moment of the scan; its final rendered state (ink-on-amber, computed 8.02:1) passes comfortably, and `inc/reveal.php`/its CSS were not touched this session. Logged here in case it's a real, separately-worth-investigating timing issue, not silently fixed since it's unrelated to this task's scope.

**Verification, all via Puppeteer + axe-core (not just visual inspection):**
- Staff Planning calculator confirmed genuinely live-recalculating with 2 different values: 150 hours → "21 labor hours/day" (150/7=21.43→round 21 ✓), 77 hours → "11 labor hours/day" (77/7=11 exactly ✓), "(Illustrative calculation)" rendered in `#9A3412`.
- Weekly Traffic By Hour heat-shading: 56 cells checked programmatically against the spec's exact thresholds (<200/<500/<800/<1100/≥1100) — 0 mismatches. Benchmarks percentile column: all 4 rows' heat classes read back exactly as specified (`pd-heat-4:78th`, `pd-heat-2:44th`, `pd-heat-4:81st`, `pd-heat-3:66th`).
- Modal opens via both the play button and the Learn More button; closes via the ✕ button, backdrop click (verified the click actually lands on the overlay via `elementFromPoint`, not just assumed), and Escape — each independently tested.
- `prefers-reduced-motion` respected in both the teaser (play-button hover scale removed — confirmed via computed `transform` matrix, no scale component) and the modal (open/close still functions correctly and instantly, confirmed via `emulateMediaFeatures`).
- axe-core (via Puppeteer + the local `axe-core` npm package, since the sandboxed `@axe-core/cli` couldn't find a system Chrome binary): **0 violations across all 6 tabs at both 375px and 1440px**, modal-scoped scan, after the fixes above.
- Screenshots: `pd-teaser-final-{375,1440}.png`, `pd-tab-{operations,traffic,performance,optimization,asset,benchmarks}-{375,1440}.png`, all in `temporary-screenshots/` — kept (not cleared) since they're this task's own requested deliverable evidence.
- `debug.log` confirmed clean before and after (stable at the pre-existing 63 lines) through the entire build-and-test cycle, including the accessibility-fix round. `php -l` clean on every PHP file touched. `npm run build` clean.
- Regression spot-check: homepage, `/industries/retail/`, and `/solutions/` all still return 200 with no new `debug.log` entries after the `tailwind.config.js`/`src/tailwind.css` changes.

**Not done this session (out of scope, not silently skipped):** Lighthouse wasn't run for this feature specifically — the task's own explicit verification list (staff calculator, heat-shading, modal triggers, reveal-bug audit, reduced-motion) didn't call for it, and it's a homepage-section addition rather than a new page/template, so the standing Phase 6/9 Lighthouse pass already covers the homepage's baseline. Flagging in case a follow-up pass is wanted specifically for the added modal weight (six tabs of markup, all server-rendered, no additional JS framework — expected impact is small but not measured here).

## 2026-07-21 — Per-industry hero interactions, Part 6 (final): Casinos & Gaming floor-plan zone selector

**Reused the same hero-card structure and `$itoi_slug` branching as Parts 0-5** — added `casinos-gaming` as the last new branch in `single-industry.php`.

**ACF:** added `casino_zones` (repeater, 4 rows: Gaming Floor / Cashier Cage / Bar / Entrance, each with `zone_description` and an optional `zone_solution` post-object field pointing at the `solution` CPT). Zone position on the floor plan is fixed by row order, documented in the field's own instructions, rather than adding separate x/y/grid-area fields for what's fundamentally a layout decision, not content. Populated Casinos & Gaming (post 23491) with the exact spec content and headline "Protect the Floor. Protect the Revenue." — critically, **Bar's `zone_solution` is set to the real "Liquor Management System" solution post (ID 23488, confirmed to exist via `wp post list --post_type=solution`)**, not a placeholder, per the spec's explicit instruction to tie that zone to the real solution rather than generic security copy.

**Built:** 4 clickable zone buttons arranged in an illustrative floor-plan layout (Entrance spanning the top, Gaming Floor spanning the middle, Cashier Cage and Bar side-by-side at the bottom — via CSS Grid `grid-template-areas`). Clicking a zone marks it active (amber border + tint, same treatment as every other Part's "selected" state) and replaces the description panel's content — confirmed via Puppeteer that only one zone is ever active at a time and the panel never stacks. When the active zone has a `zone_solution` set, a "Related solution: {title} →" link appears pointing at that solution's real permalink (`get_permalink()`); when it doesn't, the link is hidden — verified by clicking Bar (link appears, correct href to `/solutions/liquor-management/`, correct title "Liquor Management System") and then a different zone (link correctly disappears again, not left stale from the previous click).

**A real bug found and fixed, not shipped broken — a Tailwind content-purge gotcha, not a logic bug:** the floor-plan's grid layout initially rendered as a plain unordered 2×2 grid instead of the intended Entrance/Gaming-Floor/Cage+Bar arrangement. Root cause: the per-zone position classes were built by string-concatenating the loop index in PHP (`casino-zone--<?php echo $index ?>`), so the literal strings `casino-zone--0` through `--3` never appear anywhere in the theme's source files as plain text — only the unconcatenated `casino-zone--` prefix does. Tailwind's JIT content scanner matches class names as literal substrings in scanned files, not by executing PHP, so it silently tree-shook the `grid-area` rules for all 4 position classes as "unused" (confirmed by grepping the compiled CSS for `grid-area:` and finding zero matches, versus the `.casino-zone.is-active` rule which *did* survive, since both of its class tokens do appear literally elsewhere in the file). Fixed by replacing the concatenation with a literal PHP array of the 4 full class-name strings (`array('casino-zone--0', 'casino-zone--1', 'casino-zone--2', 'casino-zone--3')`) indexed by loop position — since the complete strings now exist verbatim in the PHP source, Tailwind's scanner finds them and keeps the CSS. Re-verified: all 4 `grid-area` declarations present in the compiled stylesheet, floor plan renders in the intended layout.

**Verification:** `php -l`/`node -c` clean, axe 0 violations both widths — checked twice, once untouched and once with the Bar zone active (its description panel, including the extra solution link, is the most content-rich state and the one most likely to surface a contrast or focus-order issue). Lighthouse 95/100/78¹/100. `debug.log` clean before/after. Confirmed Retail, Hospitality, Banking & Finance, Government & Councils, Logistics & Warehousing, and Stadiums & Events all still pass axe unaffected — the full set of 6, re-checked together at the end of this run. Screenshots: `casinos-default-1440.png`, `casinos-default-375.png`, `casinos-gaming-floor-1440.png`, `casinos-bar-1440.png`.

¹ Same sandbox-wide HTTPS-only cap as every other page — see Phase 6/9 entries.

**All 6 industry interactions are now complete** (Part 0/Retail was already done per the original instruction; Hospitality, Banking & Finance, Government & Councils, Logistics & Warehousing, Stadiums & Events, and Casinos & Gaming were built this session, one at a time, each independently verified). Every interaction's copy — headlines, sub-copy, every label and description — is ACF-driven on the `industry` CPT, nothing hardcoded; amber is used exclusively as the active/selected/output signal across all 6, with zero new colors introduced anywhere; every number or outcome shown is explicitly captioned illustrative; and every interactive card carries `.no-detect-reveal` so none of them can accidentally pick up the scroll-triggered corner-bracket effect.

## 2026-07-21 — Per-industry hero interactions, Part 5: Stadiums & Events live density visualization

**Reused the same hero-card structure and `$itoi_slug` branching as Parts 0-4** — added `stadiums-events` as a new branch in `single-industry.php`.

**ACF:** added `stadium_zones` (repeater, 4 rows: Main Concourse/Gate A/Gate B/Concessions, each with a `zone_weight` — the zone's share of the total marker pool, e.g. 40/20/20/20) plus `stadium_min_attendees`/`stadium_max_attendees`/`stadium_default_attendees` (slider bounds, 5,000/50,000/15,000) and `stadium_max_markers` (the total DOM marker cap across all zones combined at max density, default 50). Populated Stadiums & Events (post 23495) with the exact spec content and headline "Thousands Moving at Once. You'll Know Exactly Where."

**Built:** an "Event size" range slider (5,000-50,000, live numeric readout) driving a 2×2 zone grid. Each zone gets a pool of markers pre-generated once on page load — position (left/top %), size, and opacity per marker are randomized but generated a single time and never re-rolled — sized to that zone's proportional share of the ACF-configured marker cap (weight 40 → 20 markers, weight 20 → 10 markers, when the cap is 50). Moving the slider only toggles how many of each zone's *existing* pool are visible (`Math.round(poolSize × densityFraction)`); it never regenerates or repositions markers, so nothing "jumps" as density changes — confirmed by reading back three markers' `left` values before and after a slider move and diffing them (identical). Markers reuse the hero's detection-box visual language (`rounded-[3px]`, `1.5px` amber border) scaled down to 6-10px for density display, per the shared rule that amber marks the active/output state.

**Marker cap verified, not assumed:** at the slider's maximum value, total rendered `.stadium-marker` elements across all 4 zones was read back as exactly 50 (the configured cap) — confirming density above that point is implied through the existing markers' arrangement, not literally rendered as more DOM nodes. At the slider's minimum, zero markers are visible (sparsest state), and the default (15,000) landed at the mathematically-correct fractional count for each zone's pool size.

**Reduced motion is satisfied by construction, not just a media-query patch:** there's no drift/repositioning animation anywhere in this component to begin with (unlike the homepage hero's detection boxes, which do drift) — visibility is a plain class toggle backed by a CSS rule, not a keyframe animation reaching a final state, so under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` markers still correctly show/hide (verified: 50/50 visible at max density with reduced motion emulated), only the 0.25s opacity fade itself is skipped by the sitewide blanket transition-disable rule.

**Verification:** `php -l`/`node -c` clean, axe 0 violations both widths — checked twice, once untouched and once with the slider dragged to maximum (all 50 markers rendered), since a dense DOM state is exactly where contrast/overlap issues tend to surface. Lighthouse 90/100/78¹/100. `debug.log` clean before/after. Confirmed Retail, Hospitality, Banking & Finance, Government & Councils, and Logistics & Warehousing all still pass axe unaffected. Screenshots: `stadiums-default-1440.png`, `stadiums-default-375.png`, `stadiums-min-1440.png`, `stadiums-max-1440.png`.

¹ Same sandbox-wide HTTPS-only cap as every other page — see Phase 6/9 entries.

## 2026-07-21 — Per-industry hero interactions, Part 4: Logistics & Warehousing drag-to-compare incident timeline

**Reused the same hero-card structure and `$itoi_slug` branching as Parts 0-3** — added `logistics-warehousing` as a new branch in `single-industry.php`.

**ACF:** added `logistics_events` (repeater, 4 rows: Incident occurs / Discovered / Reported / Claim filed, each with a `without_position` and `with_position` 0-100 value placing it along its own timeline) plus four plain text fields (`logistics_without_label`, `logistics_with_label`, `logistics_gap_label_without`, `logistics_gap_label_with`). Populated Logistics & Warehousing (post 23494) with the exact spec content — headline "See the Incident Before It Becomes a Claim.", "Without ITOI"/"With ITOI" labels, "4 hours" vs "90 seconds" for the Incident→Discovered gap, and the 4-event position data (without: 0/35/65/100, with: 0/8/45/100).

**Built the classic before/after image-comparison-slider mechanic, but with two DOM timelines instead of two images** (per the spec's explicit instruction not to fake it with an actual image diff): the "Without ITOI" timeline is the always-visible base layer; "With ITOI" is an overlay wrapper clipped to the drag handle's position, containing a timeline rendered at the *stage's full width* (measured and set via JS on load + resize) rather than the clipped wrapper's own width — this is what makes the revealed events land at their correct absolute position as the handle moves, instead of stretching/squashing. The handle is a proper `role="slider"` with `aria-valuemin/max/now`, draggable via pointer events (mouse + touch) and fully keyboard-operable (arrow keys ±5%, Home/End to jump to 0/100) — not just mouse-only, since a drag-only interaction with no keyboard path would fail WCAG operability.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during first render, not shipped broken:**
1. Both timelines render the same 4 event labels, and "Incident occurs" is defined at position 0 on *both* lines (it's the same real-world moment regardless of ITOI) — this meant both layers drew an identical "Incident occurs" label stacked exactly on top of each other, plus the "with" layer's nearby "Discovered" label (at 8%, since detection is almost instant) collided with it, rendering as garbled overlapping text. Fixed two ways: the "with" layer's loop now skips index 0 entirely (that shared start point only needs to be drawn once, by the base layer), and event labels were split vertically — "without" labels sit below the track, "with" labels sit above it — so even closely-spaced events on the two different layers no longer share the same text row.
2. At the 375px breakpoint, "Incident occurs" (without, anchored flush-left at 0%) and "Discovered" (without, at 35%) still didn't have enough horizontal room and visually collided, and the "90 seconds" gap pill (positioned at the midpoint of 0% and 8%, i.e. very near the left edge) was partly clipped by the stage's `overflow:hidden`. Fixed with a `max-width:640px` block that hides just the "Incident occurs" *label* (keeping its dot — still reads as the timeline's start) on the without layer, shrinks label/gap-pill font size, and — more generally — gave every gap-label the same near-edge anchor logic already used for event labels (left-anchored under 5%, right-anchored over 95%, centered otherwise, computed once in PHP) so a gap pill positioned close to either edge never gets clipped, at any breakpoint.

**Verified the actual drag mechanics, not just the visuals** — Puppeteer-tested: dragging the handle via real mouse-down/move/up events to ~80% and reading back the clip width and `aria-valuenow`; clicking directly on the stage (not the handle) to jump position; all 4 keyboard paths (ArrowLeft/Right ±5%, Home→0%, End→100%); and read back every event marker's actual rendered `left` percentage to confirm it matches the ACF data exactly (without: 0/35/65/100, with: 8/45/100 — index 0 correctly absent from the "with" layer per the fix above) rather than just eyeballing the screenshot.

**Verification:** `php -l`/`node -c` clean, axe 0 violations both widths — checked twice, once untouched and once with the handle focused and moved via keyboard (since a custom ARIA slider's states are exactly where axe violations tend to hide). Lighthouse 97/100/78¹/100. `debug.log` clean before/after. Confirmed Retail, Hospitality, Banking & Finance, and Government & Councils all still pass axe unaffected. Screenshots: `logistics-default-1440.png`, `logistics-default-375.png`, `logistics-dragged-1440.png`.

¹ Same sandbox-wide HTTPS-only cap as every other page — see Phase 6/9 entries.

**Continuing per "part 4 onwards" instruction** — Stadiums & Events (Part 5) next, reporting back after Part 6 rather than pausing after each remaining part, since the per-part rigor (ACF → build → Puppeteer-verify → axe → Lighthouse → regression check → NOTES.md) is unchanged and each part's own findings are logged here as they happen either way.

## 2026-07-21 — Per-industry hero interactions, Part 3: Government & Councils live funding-allocation chart

**Reused the same hero-card structure and `$itoi_slug` branching as Parts 0-2** — added `government-councils` as a new branch in `single-industry.php`, no new template scaffolding.

**ACF:** added two repeaters to the same "Industry — Hero Interaction" group. `government_categories` (4 rows: Evening foot traffic / Weekend attendance / Dwell time / Safety perception, each with a 0-100 illustrative `baseline_value`) and `government_checkboxes` (4 rows: More benches / Extended library hours / Better lighting / Additional bins, each with an `impact_map` text field — a comma-separated `category_index:boost_amount` list, e.g. `"0:20,1:15"`). The impact mapping is genuinely data-driven: nothing pairing a checkbox to a category is hardcoded in PHP or JS, an editor could rewire which checkbox affects which bar (or add a 5th category/checkbox) purely through ACF. Populated Government & Councils (post 23493) with the exact spec content and headline "Let Your Public Spaces Tell You Where the Budget Should Go."; reused the generic `funnel_headline`/`funnel_intro`/`funnel_disclaimer` fields, disclaimer set to "Illustrative — for demonstration only, not based on real council data." per the exact spec wording.

**Built:** 4 independent checkboxes (native `<input type="checkbox">`, any combination, in any order — not mutually exclusive, unlike Banking's single-result-at-a-time simulator) driving a 4-bar CSS chart. Each bar's height = its baseline + the sum of every currently-checked box's impact on that category (parsed client-side from `impact_map`), capped at 100 and rendered against a fixed 160px max-height track (`Math.round(total/100*160)+'px'`). A bar turns amber (`.is-boosted`) whenever its current total exceeds its baseline; otherwise it stays the muted teal-gray resting color (`rgba(15,74,87,0.18)`, matching the same muted-teal "ordinary chrome" language as the retail funnel's drift markers) — no new colors introduced. Checking a box also gives its label row an amber border/tint via `.is-active` (same treatment language as Hospitality's active pill and Banking's active scenario card).

**Verified the arithmetic, not just the visual, matches the ACF data exactly** — Puppeteer-tested reading each bar's actual computed `style.height` after specific checkbox combinations and hand-checked it against the `baseline + Σ(impact)` formula for every category in three different combinations (single box, two boxes together, then unchecking one), plus a targeted test that temporarily inflated one category's `data-baseline` past 100 client-side to force and confirm the `Math.min(100, …)` cap actually clamps the bar at the 160px ceiling rather than overflowing past it (a combination the real seeded data doesn't happen to reach on its own, so this was checked in isolation rather than left unverified).

**Verification:** `php -l`/`node -c` clean, axe 0 violations both widths — checked twice, once on the untouched default state and once with two boxes actually checked (bars boosted, labels amber), since the default-only run wouldn't exercise the boosted-bar contrast at all. Lighthouse 90/100/78¹/100. `debug.log` clean before/after. Confirmed Retail, Hospitality, and Banking & Finance all still pass axe unaffected. Screenshots: `government-default-1440.png`, `government-default-375.png`, `government-one-checked-1440.png`, `government-two-checked-1440.png`, `government-all-checked-1440.png`.

¹ Same sandbox-wide HTTPS-only cap as every other page — see Phase 6/9 entries.
