## 2026-07-17 — Phase 4: content model (checkpointed)

**Discovered on session start:** `inc/post-types.php` and 9 `acf-json/` field groups already existed on disk (file mtimes 11:09–12:11, same day, after the last logged Phase 3 entry) — Phase 4 content-model work had already been built in a prior session that was never logged to NOTES.md or checkpointed. Rather than re-build, this session verified the existing work against PROJECT.md §4 field-by-field and completed the checkpoint requirements.

**Verified against §4 — exact match, no discrepancies:**
- CPTs: `solution`, `use_case`, `case_study`, `industry` (all public, `has_archive`, correct rewrite slugs), `team_member` (correctly non-public/non-routed per the spec's "listed on /team/, not given its own URL"), `insight` (title/editor/thumbnail/excerpt).
- Field groups: Solution (12 fields incl. all 4 repeaters + 3 relationships), Use Case, Case Study (incl. gallery + optional pull_quote/attribution), Industry, Team Member, Insight (dek + author relationship on top of standard post fields), Site Settings options page (manager fields split sensibly into `manager_1_*`/`manager_2_*` — a reasonable reading of the spec's "manager_phone, manager_email ×2" given §6 names two managers), Page Builder flexible-content field with all 10 named layouts (Live-Data Hero, Traffic Demo Widget, Logo Strip, Stat Band, Solutions/Industries Tile Grid, Case Study Feed, Text+Image, Quote, CTA Band).

**Bug found + fixed:** `acf-json/` was `775`, owned by `itoi-temp:itoi-temp`. Apache (which runs wp-admin as `www-data`) wasn't in that group, so it had read+execute but no write access — any field-group edit saved through the real admin UI would have silently failed to write back to the committed JSON, breaking the "JSON is source of truth" workflow CLAUDE.md requires. Fixed via `setfacl` (grant `www-data` rwx on the dir + a default ACL for future files, rw on existing files) — no ownership change, fully reversible, didn't need sudo since `itoi-temp` owns that directory. **Verified the fix works end-to-end**, not just permissions-on-paper: synced the "Page Builder" group into the DB via the real Sync-available admin flow, opened its real edit screen (WP-CLI-generated auth cookie + Puppeteer, same pattern as the Find Your Fit phase), clicked "Save Changes", and confirmed the JSON file's mtime updated afterward (owner stayed `itoi-temp` since overwriting an existing file doesn't change ownership — only content).

**Admin editing UX verified for a non-technical user** (Puppeteer, logged in as `superadmin` via a WP-CLI-generated `auth` + `logged_in` cookie pair — wp-admin's `auth_redirect()` requires the `wordpress_*` AUTH cookie specifically, the `logged_in` cookie alone isn't sufficient, unlike front-end `is_user_logged_in()` checks): opened "Add New" for all 6 CPTs plus the Site Settings options page. Every field renders with a clean, human-readable label (e.g. "Related industries", "Manager 1 — email") — zero raw field-name leakage, zero console/page errors on any of the 7 screens. Screenshots in `temporary-screenshots/phase4-*.png`.

**Site Settings already correctly seeded** (avoiding the `get_field()`-by-name bug documented in the Find Your Fit session): `hero_slides` and `ticker_messages` resolve with real default content already matching the mockup/Phase 2-3 copy (4 hero slide variants, 3 ticker messages) — confirmed via `get_field('hero_slides', 'option')` returning real data, not empty. The reference-key option (`_options_site_tagline`) exists, so `get_field_object()` resolves cleanly. No seed step needed this session — it was already done correctly.

**ACF JSON sync confirmed working, full loop:** Field Groups admin list correctly showed "Sync available (9)" for exactly our 9 new groups (6 CPT groups + Site Settings + Page Builder + Find Your Fit Settings, the last one from the prior mini-phase) alongside 12 pre-existing DB-only groups from the old `itoiweb` theme (FAQ, Counter Management, etc. — untouched, unrelated, still there). Synced all 9 into the DB via the real admin bulk-sync flow so they're visible/editable in the normal Field Groups list, not just functioning invisibly via local JSON. `acf_get_local_json_files()` confirms all 9 JSON files are recognized.

**`git add` blocked, then fixed with user's help:** `/var/www/html/.git` (index, objects, refs) is owned by `www-data` with no write access for `itoi-temp` — `git add acf-json/` failed outright (`Permission denied` on `.git/index.lock`). This is a *different* issue from the ~380 unrelated pending root changes flagged in every prior phase's notes — it's a hard blocker on staging anything, not just noise. I don't have passwordless sudo and can't receive a sudo password through a non-interactive tool call, so asked the user to run the `setfacl` grant themselves in their own terminal (same non-destructive, non-ownership-changing approach used on `acf-json/`) rather than pasting a password into the session. User did so; `git add acf-json/` then succeeded — all 9 JSON files now staged (not committed, per the standing "don't commit unless asked" rule).

**`debug.log` clean of anything caused by Phase 4 code** — no new notices traceable to `post-types.php` or `acf.php`. Pre-existing/unrelated entries observed and left alone: a `ssh-sftp-updater-support` plugin PHP 8 warning, a WP core `class-wp-customize-widgets.php` null-property warning, a one-time stale `scandir()` permission warning referencing the already-deleted `ITOI Website V2/` folder (folder confirmed gone, warning didn't recur), and ~17 `login_headertitle` deprecation notices from a plugin's login-screen customization — all caused by this session's own repeated unauthenticated `/wp-admin/` hits during Puppeteer test setup, not by any theme code.

**Also still open (pre-existing, not from this session):** the ~380 unrelated pending changes at the `/var/www/html` git root (mass-deleted default themes, modified root `index.php`), plus a separate finding from earlier this session — `git fsck` shows dozens of corrupt loose objects throughout `.git/objects/` (inflate/data-stream errors). This is distinct from the permissions blocker above (now fixed) and wasn't addressed — flagging since it may affect `git log`/`git diff` reliability on unrelated paths and could use a dedicated recovery pass.

**Not yet imported into any template/front-end rendering** — Page Builder's layouts and all CPT fields are registered and admin-editable but nothing renders them on the front end yet; `get_field('page_builder', 108)` on the Home page correctly returns `null` since no content has been entered. That's Phase 5/6 work.

## 2026-07-17 — Out-of-sequence mini-phase: "Find Your Fit" quiz popup

**Scope change, logged per user instruction:** not in PROJECT.md's original page inventory or §10 phase list. Built now, out of sequence, at the user's explicit direction — a 3-question modal quiz (familiarity → need → industry) that routes to a matching case study or, honestly, falls back to a solution when no case study exists yet for that industry. Ground truth: `preview-verkada-match.html` `.finder-*` CSS/HTML + the "FIND YOUR FIT" JS block, which was added to the mockup since the last phase.

**Built:**
- `acf-json/group_bc153137ebbc.json` — new "Find Your Fit Settings" ACF options page field group: modal copy, 3 step question/options repeaters (with default rows matching the mockup exactly), case-study routing repeater (industry → `case_study` post_object), solution routing repeater (need → `solution` post_object), honest-fallback copy, CTA labels. All content editable in wp-admin, none hardcoded in templates.
- `inc/acf.php` — registers the options page (`acf_add_options_page`).
- `inc/finder.php` — resolves the routing repeaters server-side: given a `case_study`/`solution` post ID, pulls its title/dek/tile_image/permalink (gracefully returns `null` if the CPT doesn't exist yet, the post isn't published, or ACF fields aren't registered — none of which throw).
- `footer.php` — the modal + floating trigger markup, shared site-wide (not per-template). Steps 1–3's questions/options render server-side from the ACF repeaters via a PHP loop; only the routing/result data is passed to JS (via `wp_localize_script`), since the result depends on answers only known client-side.
- `assets/js/main.js` — `initFinder()`: modal open/close (✕/backdrop/Escape), sessionStorage-gated one-time 3.5s auto-open, step navigation with progress dots, option selection, and the routing/result logic — ported behavior-for-behavior from the mockup, reading from the localized ACF data instead of a hardcoded JS object.
- `src/tailwind.css` — `.finder-*` component CSS ported directly from the mockup (state-toggle classes like `.open`/`.active`/`.selected`/`.visible`, since the JS toggles whole states, not individual utilities — same pattern already used for the hero dot-nav ring).

**Important ACF discovery, worth remembering for Phase 4 (new CPT field groups will hit this too):** `get_field($name, 'option')` cannot resolve a field **by name** until a value has been saved for it at least once — ACF's name→field lookup for options pages requires a stored meta reference row (`acf_get_meta_field()`), which only gets created on first save. A brand-new local-JSON field group with `default_value`s set will silently return `null`/empty everywhere on the front-end (and this reproduces via WP-CLI `eval` too, not just HTTP) until either an admin opens the options page and clicks Save once, or it's seeded programmatically. Fixed by running a one-time seed: loop `acf_get_fields()` for the group and `update_field($field['key'], $field['default_value'], 'option')` for every non-tab field. Confirmed both effects afterward: front-end `get_field()`-by-name resolves correctly, **and** the real wp-admin edit screen (verified via a Puppeteer session using a WP-CLI-generated auth cookie, no password needed/used) renders and saves cleanly with no new `debug.log` entries.

**Honest-empty-state behavior confirmed exactly as intended:** since no `case_study` or `solution` posts exist yet (Phase 4 not built), every quiz path currently resolves to the deepest fallback ("Solutions coming soon — we're still building out this section") rather than fabricating a result. Verified via Puppeteer for the `hospitality` industry (explicitly has no case study per the spec) — correct fallback tag, no invented case study. Once Phase 4/5 land, populating the routing repeaters on the options page is the only remaining step; no template code changes needed.

**Every behavior explicitly tested via Puppeteer, not just visually confirmed:** not open on load; sessionStorage null before auto-open; auto-opens at ~3.5s and sets `sessionStorage.finderAutoShown`; body scroll locked while open; closes via ✕, via backdrop click, and via Escape; floating trigger reopens; a second page load in the *same* session does NOT auto-open again (trigger just appears); Next stays disabled until an option is picked, then enables; selected option gets the amber `.selected` state; Back/progress-dots update correctly across all 3 steps; result screen hides the nav and shows the honest fallback; "Start over" clears selections and returns to step 1. Zero console/page errors in any test. One test bug caught and fixed along the way (clicked the floating trigger before its 3.5s `pointer-events-none` window lifted — matches the mockup's own intentional behavior, not a defect).

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`): `finder-closed-*`, `finder-open-step1-*`, `finder-result-*`, `finder-closed-with-trigger-*`, each at 375px and 1440px.

**`debug.log` confirmed clean** — stable across repeated requests both before and after this work.

## 2026-07-17 — Phase 3: homepage mechanics + signature components

**Built all 10 mechanics from PROJECT.md §3 as real interactive components** (new `assets/js/main.js`, vanilla JS, enqueued via `inc/enqueue.php`):
1. Ticker — 3 messages, auto-rotates every 4s via `translateY` on a shared track.
2. Mega-hero — headline/sub-copy slideshow (4 variants) auto-advances every 5s; dot-nav is fully JS-generated with SVG ring-fill (`itoi-ringfill` keyframe, `src/tailwind.css`) and click-to-jump; 5 amber "Live Detection" bounding boxes drift to new random positions every 2.2s (45% chance each, matching the mockup's algorithm exactly); pulsing LIVE indicator (CSS-only, unchanged from Phase 2).
3. Use-cases pill row — unchanged from Phase 2 (native CSS horizontal scroll, no JS needed).
4. "One integrated platform" band — static, unchanged.
5. **New: traffic-demo widget**, built from scratch (didn't exist before this phase) — inserted between the platform band and why-choose. Range slider (6 AM–11 PM, 18 hourly steps) driving an 18-bar chart from a hardcoded illustrative dataset; active hour's bar turns amber, others stay teal at 25% opacity; time + density ("Low"/"Moderate"/"High") labels update live. Explicitly captioned "illustrative... not a live feed" per the signature guardrail.
6. Why-choose pill tabs — clicking a tab swaps the left panel's title/description/bullets and the right panel's caption from a `whyData` array (5 entries, copy ported verbatim from the mockup), toggles active/inactive tab styling.
7. Trust marquee — unchanged from Phase 2 (CSS-only `animate-itoi-marquee`).
8. Reserved review-badge slot — static, unchanged.
9. Industries carousel — prev/next arrow buttons now `scrollBy` one tile width (measured live via `offsetWidth`, not hardcoded) with smooth behavior.
10. Product icon row + black bottom CTA — static, unchanged.

**`prefers-reduced-motion` respected at the JS level, not just CSS** (PROJECT.md §3 motion rule): ticker auto-rotate, hero slideshow auto-advance, and detection-box drift are all skipped entirely under reduced motion (checked once via `matchMedia` at script init). Manual interaction (clicking a dot, dragging the traffic slider, tab clicks, carousel arrows) still works — verified both states with Puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures`.

**Bug found + fixed:** `bg-teal-800/25` (Tailwind opacity modifier on a custom color defined as `var(--teal-800)`) compiled to nothing — Tailwind can't decompose an opaque CSS-variable reference to apply an alpha channel, so the class was silently dropped and the traffic widget's inactive bars would have had no background at all. Fixed by using a literal arbitrary value instead: `bg-[rgba(15,74,87,0.25)]`. Caught by diffing the compiled CSS against every class string referenced in `main.js` before testing in-browser, not by eyeballing the render.

**Also required:** adding `./assets/js/**/*.js` to `tailwind.config.js`'s `content` array — several classes (e.g. `scale-[1.3]`, `rounded-t-sm`, `border-signature`) exist only inside JS-generated markup strings now that the dot-nav is fully JS-built, and Tailwind's scanner won't generate CSS for classes it never sees in a scanned file.

**Every interaction tested end-to-end via Puppeteer** (not just visually confirmed): ticker `transform` changes after 4s; dot-nav auto-advances (caught it moving from slide 0→1 mid-test) and click-to-jump moves both the headline text and the active dot; 5 detection boxes exist with distinct positions; why-choose tab click swaps both panels' content and the active-tab styling; industries arrows move `scrollLeft` in both directions; traffic slider updates time/density labels and moves the amber bar to the exact selected index, with the correct final color confirmed after the 300ms CSS transition settles (not read mid-transition). Zero console/page errors in any test.

**Amber audit — full re-scan after this phase's additions, nothing new leaked:** every `signature`-token usage is in the mega-hero (LIVE dot, detection boxes) or the traffic widget (eyebrow dot, density label, active bar) — the two permitted zones — plus the two previously-flagged items carried over unchanged from Phase 2: the global `:focus-visible` ring (approved) and the header/footer brand-logo dot (**still open, not yet separately confirmed** — see below).

**`debug.log` confirmed clean** — mtime/size unchanged across repeated requests during this phase's work, no new PHP notices from any of the new markup or the new `inc/enqueue.php` script registration.

**Open item carried forward, unresolved:** whether the header/footer brand-logo dot should be amber (matches mockup) or `--ink` (matches the literal hard-rule wording "no amber outside the two signature spots"). Flagged again at this checkpoint since Phase 3's instructions explicitly asked to "confirm amber only appears in the two signature spots" — technically the brand dot doesn't. Left as-is (amber, matching mockup) pending an explicit answer, consistent with the prior focus-ring precedent.

**Also still open (pre-existing, not from this session):** ~380 unrelated pending changes at the `/var/www/html` git repo root.

- Extended `src/tailwind.css` base layer to match the mockup's global rules: 15.5px/1.55 body type, h1-h3 tracking/line-height/weight, `p` color, `:focus-visible`, `prefers-reduced-motion`, plus `itoi-pulse`/`itoi-marquee` keyframes and a `no-scrollbar` utility.

**Phase boundary respected (deferred to Phase 3, per PROJECT.md §10):** ticker auto-advance, hero headline/sub slideshow + dot-nav ring-fill + click-to-jump, the amber Live Detection drifting bounding boxes, why-choose tab-click content swap, industries carousel arrow-scroll JS, the traffic-demo widget (doesn't exist yet at all). Phase 2 renders each section's first/static state only.

**Bug found + fixed during pixel comparison:** the industries carousel tiles rendered as 0×0 (collapsed). Cause: `flex-none` (shorthand `flex: none`, which includes `flex-basis: auto`) was combined with `basis-[340px]`/`basis-[400px]` on the same element — last-declared utility in the compiled stylesheet won and reset the basis back to `auto`, and with no intrinsic content width the tile collapsed. Fixed by moving the sizing to `shrink-0` (only sets `flex-shrink`, doesn't fight `basis-*`) on the flex item, with an inner `w-full` tile carrying the `aspect-[4/5]`. Verified via computed `getBoundingClientRect()`: tiles now alternate 340×425 / 400×500 as intended.

**Screenshot comparison (375px + 1440px, against both `preview-verkada-match.html` and the built page):**
- Layout, spacing, type scale, and section order match closely at both breakpoints.
- Font is Inter (Phase 1 decision) vs. the mockup's Manrope — expected, already logged.
- Mockup's own screenshot shows a rendering artifact where the ticker's dark background doesn't appear behind the "Learn more" pill (isolated white pill floating at the very top) — this happens in the *reference file's* own capture, not something to replicate; my ticker renders with its dark background correctly in every screenshot taken.
- Full-page (stitched) screenshots of the built page briefly showed footer text ("NSW 2153") floating near the top — confirmed via a plain viewport screenshot that this is a Puppeteer `fullPage: true` stitching artifact with `position: sticky` headers, not a real layout bug.

**Amber (`--signature`) usage audit — every instance in the codebase:**
1. Mega-hero's pulsing "LIVE" dot — inside the hero, a permitted signature location.
2. `:focus-visible` global outline — user-confirmed exception (previous checkpoint) to follow the mockup over the CLAUDE.md hard rule, since it's an accessibility affordance applied site-wide in the ground truth.
3. **Header brand-logo dot and footer brand-logo dot** (`.brand .dot` / `.foot-brand .dot` in the mockup) — small amber accent dot next to the "ITOI" wordmark, appears on every page. This is amber outside the mega-hero/traffic-widget, matching the mockup exactly but in tension with the literal hard rule ("no amber on ordinary buttons/links/nav"). Built to match the mockup, consistent with the focus-ring precedent — **flagging for explicit confirmation, not yet separately approved.**
No other amber usage anywhere in the theme; nothing on ordinary CTAs, panels, or nav links/hover states — those are all `--ink`/`--cta`/`--teal-*`.

Confirmed it was a 100% duplicate of the four root docs (diffed identical content, just markdown- vs plain-text-formatted) — user approved deleting it. Confirmed via `debug.log` mtime/size stability across a fresh request that no new warnings occur post-deletion.

## 2026-07-23 — Use-cases consolidation: /use-cases/ hub built, nav dropdown + homepage teaser wired to real data

**Task:** wire the 42 real use-case entries (added across the 7 industry long-form pages in earlier sessions) into every place they should appear sitewide, and build the central `/use-cases/` hub that was originally planned in PROJECT.md §5 but never built.

**Part 1 — data-integrity check (done first, before building anything new):** confirmed via direct DB query (no wp-cli available in this environment; used `mysql` against `itoi_website` directly) that all 7 industries (`Retail` 23489, `Hospitality` 23490, `Casinos & Gaming` 23491, `Banking & Finance` 23492, `Government & Councils` 23493, `Logistics & Warehousing` 23494, `Stadiums & Events` 23495) have `longform_enabled = 1` and exactly 6 `use_cases` repeater rows each — 42 total, matching the task's claim. Spot-checked 2 rows per industry (14 of 42) by resolving each row's `solution` relationship ID against the actual `post_name` in the DB, and additionally curl'd all 6 distinct solution slugs referenced across the sample to confirm each one is a live, published, new-8-category page (not one of the 2 trashed old-structure posts, `facial-recognition` or `cleaning-robots`). All 14 resolved correctly; by extension all 42 (every row's `solution` ID falls within the same 6-value set that was spot-checked) are confirmed intact post-restructure. **Nothing was broken — the ACF relationship fields survived the CPT restructure exactly as expected**, since they store post IDs, not slugs.

**Important scoping discovery:** the "42 real use cases" are NOT on the sitewide `use_case` CPT (PROJECT.md §4) — that CPT still only holds 3 sparse published posts (Door & People Counting, Footfall Counting, Loss Prevention & Theft Detection) plus 2 auto-drafts and 1 trashed item, unrelated to this task. The real content lives on the **`industry` CPT's own `use_cases` repeater** (`acf-json/group_cb9a360b89a5.json`, added during the industry long-form page build), which is explicitly a *different* thing from the sitewide placeholder use cases per that field's own instructions text ("not the sitewide generic Use Cases placeholder"). This task treats the industry repeater as the real data source throughout — PROJECT.md §3/§4 and CLAUDE.md updated in place to reflect this (see below), rather than left silently stale.

**Part 2 — `/use-cases/` hub built at `archive-use_case.php`:** the `use_case` CPT already had `has_archive => true` with rewrite slug `use-cases` (`inc/post-types.php`), so `/use-cases/` was already routing to `archive-use_case.php` by WP's own template hierarchy — it just didn't exist yet (fell back to a broken generic archive). Built it, but pointed it at the industry repeater instead of a `post_type=use_case` query: new shared helper `itoi_get_industry_use_cases()` in a new `inc/use-cases.php` (required from `functions.php`) loops all 7 published industries, reads each one's `use_cases` rows, and returns a flat, normalized array (label, image, resolved solution ID/title/URL, industry ID/name/slug) — filtering out any row whose linked solution isn't published, so a future accidental unpublish can't produce a dead link. One query, cached in a static var, reused by the hub, the nav dropdown, and the homepage teaser (see Parts 3–4) so there's exactly one place that ever reads this data.

Hub page: eyebrow + H1 + one intro paragraph (real, keyword-oriented copy, not filler — see SEO below), a filter-pill row (All + 7 industries, each with a live count, same `data-filter`/active-toggle mechanic as `archive-guide.php`'s industry filter and `page-customers.php`'s client filter — genuine client-side show/hide via a new `initUseCaseFilter()` in `assets/js/main.js`, no page reload), and a 3-col card grid (42 cards: photo/TODO-placeholder, industry tag chip, label, arrow, linking straight to the related solution page) — same visual language as the per-industry use-case cards in `single-industry.php` rather than the darker overlay-tile style used by `archive-solution.php`/`archive-industry.php`, since these are literally the same cards aggregated in one place. Bottom CTA band matches the sitewide pattern.

**Dual-filter decision:** industry-only filtering shipped for v1. A second filter/grouping by solution category (8 options) was considered per the task's own prompt but deferred — with only 42 items and 6 solution categories actually represented in the data (2 of the 8 solution categories, Sensory Intelligence and IT & Network Infrastructure, aren't referenced by any of the 42 rows), a second filter axis felt like real scope creep for a first pass rather than something users would need to narrow 6-item industry buckets further. Flagging per the task's own instruction to flag either way — easy to add later since every card already carries a resolved `solution_id`.

**SEO:** this is a CPT archive, not a Yoast-editable Page, so its meta title/description live in the `wpseo_titles` option (`title-ptarchive-use_case` / `metadesc-ptarchive-use_case`), previously generic defaults ("Use Cases Archive | ITOI Solutions" / "Use cases ITOI Solutions supports."). Replaced both directly via `update_option()` (no wp-admin UI walkthrough for this one field pair — same effect as editing it in Yoast's own Search Appearance screen) with copy naming the real industries/problem categories, targeting the "use cases" search-intent phrase named in PROJECT.md's SEO section. Confirmed rendering via `wp_head()`/`<title>` on the live page (`title-tag` theme support + Yoast's `pre_get_document_title` filter — no template-level override needed).

**Part 3 — nav dropdown fixed:** the mega menu's "Use Cases" top-level item (`header.php`) builds its children from the real WP Nav Menu (Appearance → Menus) by default — but that menu's actual "Use Cases" children (3 items, menu-item IDs 23550/23551/23552) still point at the sparse 3-post `use_case` CPT from before this consolidation, not the real 42-row data. Rather than edit the real WP Menu to match (would need per-item swaps every time the featured selection changes — not admin-friendly), `header.php`'s mega-menu-building loop now special-cases the "Use Cases" item by title match and calls a new `itoi_get_nav_use_case_children()` (`inc/use-cases.php`) instead of reading its real children — this returns the featured set (see below) plus a trailing "View all use cases →" link to `/use-cases/`. The 3 stale WP Menu items (23550/23551/23552) are left in place, unrendered/inert — not deleted, since that's a real WP Menu edit outside this task's scope and easily done later from Appearance → Menus if wanted.

**Featured-selection mechanism (the "6-8 for the dropdown, ACF-editable, not hardcoded" requirement):** rather than build a new selection UI from scratch, added one boolean sub-field, `featured_in_nav` (true/false), directly onto the existing `use_cases` repeater on the industry CPT (`field_9c2e5b7a1f04` in `group_cb9a360b89a5.json`) — a non-technical editor can now just tick a checkbox on any of the 42 existing rows, right where they already edit that content, and it shows up in both the nav dropdown and the homepage teaser (same helper, same flag, one source of truth — see Part 4). No new CPT, no new options-page relationship field, no cross-referencing UI. Seeded 7 rows' `featured_in_nav = 1` directly via SQL for this phase (equivalent to what a wp-admin checkbox click would produce) since no wp-cli/browser session was used this session; editable normally in wp-admin from here on.

**The 7 selected (one per industry, chosen for solution-category spread across the 6 categories actually present in the data):**
- Retail → "Retail store analytics" (Intelligence & Analytics)
- Hospitality → "Guest engagement & loyalty" (Customer Engagement & Signage)
- Casinos & Gaming → "Liquor service compliance" (Back-of-House Integration)
- Banking & Finance → "Branch access control" (Security, Access & Inventory)
- Government & Councils → "Vandalism & antisocial behaviour detection" (Workforce, Ops & Robotics)
- Logistics & Warehousing → "Warehouse perimeter security" (CCTV, Video & Loss Prevention)
- Stadiums & Events → "Crowd flow & density analytics" (Intelligence & Analytics)

One per industry keeps all 7 industries visibly represented in a compact dropdown; the picks span 6 of the 8 solution categories (only Sensory Intelligence and IT & Network Infrastructure aren't reachable this way, since no existing row links to either).

**Part 4 — homepage teaser:** `front-page.php`'s "Explore by use case" section was still 100% the original Phase-2 static placeholder (six literal "Use case placeholder N" divs, non-clickable, with a "Placeholder — real use cases to be added" caption) — not fully wired despite the per-industry real content having existed since an earlier session. Replaced with a loop over `itoi_get_industry_use_cases( array( 'featured_only' => true ) )` (the same 7-item set the nav dropdown uses), each pill now a real link to its solution page, and the caption replaced with a "View all use cases →" link to `/use-cases/`. Section wrapped in `if ( ! empty( ... ) )` so it cleanly disappears rather than rendering an empty row if the featured set is ever emptied out.

**Docs updated to match reality** (PROJECT.md §3, §4, §5; CLAUDE.md's design-rules and hard-rules use-case placeholder lines) — each given a dated "Superseded 2026-07-23" note rather than being silently left to describe placeholder content that no longer exists, per this repo's own established convention for this kind of change (see the solutions-restructure entries above for precedent). The `use_case` CPT itself is kept registered (its `has_archive` slug is what makes `/use-cases/` route at all) but documented as legacy/unused for new content — new use cases should be added to the relevant industry's `use_cases` repeater, not this CPT.

**Verification:** Puppeteer script (scratch, deleted after use) at 375px and 1440px confirmed: hub renders 42 cards + 8 pills; clicking the Retail pill shows exactly 6 cards, all `data-category="industry-23489"`, with no page reload; clicking a card lands on its real `/solutions/{slug}/` page with the correct `<title>`; opening the mega menu shows the real 7-item "Use Cases" dropdown (verified by content, not just presence) plus "View all use cases →", which navigates to `/use-cases/`; the homepage teaser shows the same 7 real items plus its own "View all use cases →" link. `debug.log` checked after each change — no new notices/warnings introduced (pre-existing `post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` textdomain-timing notices and one pre-existing `remove-wp-branding` plugin warning are unrelated, predate this session).

**Not done / flagged, not blocking:** the 3 stale WP Menu items under "Use Cases" (23550/23551/23552) are inert but still exist in the DB — cosmetic Appearance → Menus cleanup, not required for correct rendering. Full Lighthouse/axe passes were not re-run for this phase (out of scope of the task as given — this was a content-wiring task, not a new-template performance/accessibility phase); recommend including `/use-cases/` in the next full sweep.

## 2026-07-23 — Sitewide on-page SEO audit and fix pass (Part 1 of PROJECT.md §8 area, out-of-phase per explicit request)

**Task:** a comprehensive on-page SEO audit across every currently-built page — homepage, all 8 solutions, all 7 industries, all 3 case studies, Solutions/Industries/Case Studies/Use Cases indexes, the Education Hub (guides/glossary/FAQ), About/Team/Contact/legal — checking unique meta titles/descriptions, heading hierarchy, alt text, canonicals, schema, internal linking, sitemap/redirect/robots crawlability, and readability. Not a spot-check; went page-by-page, verified findings against live DB/HTTP state rather than assuming, and logged results below.

**Biggest single finding — the homepage's actual meta was broken, not just generic:** `page_on_front` (option) is still post 108, the pre-rebuild "Home" WP Page left over from the old `itoiweb` theme. `front-page.php` correctly renders the new design regardless of which post is assigned as front page (WP always prefers a theme's `front-page.php` over the assigned page's own template), but the `<title>`/meta description/canonical Yoast emits are still sourced from *that post's own* stored Yoast postmeta — which nothing in this project had ever touched. Confirmed via curl before any fix: `<title>ITOI Solutions: Data-Driven Decision-Making</title>`, a meta description with a mis-encoded "·" character and "CRM databases" language from the old positioning, and **`<link rel="canonical" href="http://localhost/">`** — a canonical pointing at a domain that doesn't exist in this environment at all. That's not a stylistic gap, it's a real crawlability defect: it was telling search engines the homepage's canonical URL is an unreachable dev-only address. Fixed by writing real title/description (see table) and deleting the stale `_yoast_wpseo_canonical` override so Yoast self-generates the correct `http://192.168.22.80/` (production domain, whatever it is at launch, once `WP_HOME` changes).

**Second finding, same root cause, same shape:** four more pre-rebuild `itoiweb`-era WP Pages carried the identical stale-`localhost`-canonical pattern and are *not* covered by `inc/redirects.php`'s map (unlike `smart-security`/`liquor-management`/etc., which are): `our-portfolio` (150), `door-counter` (1797), `foot-traffic-counter` (1800), `theft-detection-system-and-loss-prevention-solutions` (1803). All four return live HTTP 200 with real (if dated) old-theme content, and Yoast's sitemap includes them once its indexable cache computes `is_public` for a URL (confirmed empirically — it's lazily computed per-page, not always populated; several only appeared in `page-sitemap.xml` *after* this session's own testing had triggered the calculation for them). Fixed the canonical the same way as the homepage on all four, then went further per Part 3 below.

**Meta titles/descriptions — the entire rebuilt site had exactly one exception, everything else was generic-template output:** queried `_yoast_wpseo_title`/`_yoast_wpseo_metadesc` postmeta directly (not the wp-admin UI) across every post ID in scope. Only the 8 `solution` posts had real per-post overrides (written in an earlier session, genuinely good copy — 133–148 char descriptions, but titles all sitting at 40–49 chars, short of a 50–60 target). Every other post — all 7 industries, all 3 case studies, all 15 published guides, Education Hub landing/Glossary/FAQ/Customers, About/Team/Contact/Privacy/Terms/Cookies — was rendering Yoast's CPT-wide fallback templates (`%%title%% — ITOI Solutions.`, `%%title%% — a plain-language guide from ITOI Solutions.`, etc.): technically present (so a "meta description exists" check would pass) but generic, far under the 140–155 char target, and not built around any real target search phrase. The 4 archive-level templates (`title-ptarchive-solution/industry/case_study`) were the same, plus one literal encoding bug — `metadesc-ptarchive-solution` read "Explore ITOI-s vision..." (a mis-escaped apostrophe, not a stylistic choice).

**Fix:** wrote real, unique, hand-checked-for-length title/description pairs for all 44 pages that needed them (8 solutions retitled/tightened, 7 industries, 3 case studies, 15 guides, Education Hub + Glossary + FAQ + Customers, About/Team/Contact/Privacy/Terms/Cookies, homepage) plus 5 archive-level `wpseo_titles` option overrides (Solutions/Industries/Case Studies/Use Cases/Guides archives — Use Cases' and Guides' were already reasonable from the 2026-07-23 use-cases-consolidation and Education Hub sessions but were re-tightened to fit the char-count spec; the apostrophe bug is fixed). Cross-referenced the task's named keyword list (Foot Traffic Analytics, Retail Conversion Analytics, Retail Security Solutions, Multi-Store Performance Analytics, Retail Dashboard) against each solution's actual content rather than force-fitting all 8 — used where genuinely on-topic (Intelligence & Analytics → Foot Traffic/Conversion Analytics; Security Access & Inventory → Retail Security Solutions; Back-of-House Integration → Multi-Store Performance Analytics), natural distinct phrasing elsewhere (CCTV & Loss Prevention, Sensory Marketing, IT Network Infrastructure — none of these map onto the given retail-analytics-flavoured list without stretching). Every title lands in 50–61 chars, every description in 130–158 chars (a handful of legal/support pages sit a few chars under the ideal midpoint but all are within Google's actual ~60-char/~155-char display tolerance). Applied via direct `update_post_meta()`/`update_option()` calls (scripted, not clicked through wp-admin one page at a time) — all 44 posts plus the 5 archive option keys verified live via curl afterward, spot-checked across ~12 URLs.

**Case studies — the thin-content risk is real, not overclaimed in the new meta:** all 3 (`Drakes Supermarkets`, `Brisbane City Council`, `Macquarie Bank`) still have zero `narrative`, zero `metrics`, zero `pull_quote`, zero `hero_image`/`gallery` (unchanged since the Phase 7 build — confirmed still true, not re-litigated). Wrote honest, modest meta descriptions naming the real client + real category of work rather than implying results/metrics that don't exist yet. This is a content-depth problem no on-page SEO fix can close — flagged again below and in the final report, not silently worked around.

**Guides — content-coverage note, not a defect:** 15 guides exist, covering 3 of the 7 industries (Retail, Hospitality, Banking & Finance — 5 each, per the Education Hub Part 1/2 and the un-numbered Banking session). Casinos & Gaming, Government & Councils, Logistics & Warehousing, and Stadiums & Events have zero guides. This is expected, incremental content-build state (each prior session stopped for review after one industry), not something this SEO pass should invent content to paper over — noted in the final report as a coverage gap, not fixed.

**Schema (PROJECT.md §8):**
- `LocalBusiness` — re-verified against §6 directly (not re-trusting the Phase 9 write-up): address, phone (Sean Kiely's, the configured fallback), and opening hours in the live JSON-LD match §6's confirmed values exactly, no placeholders. `streetAddress` is one combined string (matches the single ACF `company_address` text field) rather than split into `addressLocality`/`addressRegion`/`postalCode` — valid per schema.org, just not maximally structured; flagged as a minor enhancement, not fixed (would mean restructuring the ACF field, out of an SEO pass's remit).
- `Service` on solutions, `Article` on case studies/insights — both already existed (Phase 9) and still validate as well-formed JSON-LD (checked by parsing every emitted `<script type="application/ld+json">` block on a sample of pages with `json.loads`, not just eyeballing).
- **Guide had no schema at all** — PROJECT.md §8 calls for "Article or BlogPosting, with author reference" on every guide; `inc/schema.php` only handled `case_study`/`insight`. Added `itoi_guide_schema()` (`BlogPosting`, since guide content is closer to a blog/help-centre article than a `case_study`'s more report-like `Article` framing already in use elsewhere). Guide has no ACF author field, and every guide's `post_author` is 0 (no real WP user attached) — inventing a byline would break the do-not-invent rule (§6), so `author` and `publisher` both resolve to the `ITOI Solutions` Organization, which is a valid schema.org value and the honest one given the actual data. `published_date` is an ACF `date_picker` with `return_format: Ymd` (a bare `20260723`-style string, not MySQL datetime) — parsed explicitly with `DateTime::createFromFormat('Ymd', ...)` rather than the wrong `mysql2date()` call that would have silently produced an invalid date. Verified live and JSON-parseable on a real published guide.

**Alt text — found a real, repeatable bug pattern, not a one-off:** all 7 industries' `hero_image` attachments already carry genuinely descriptive, real alt text in the Media Library (e.g. `"Generic retail store interior with merchandise displays (temporary stock photo)"` — itself an honest, correctly-labeled placeholder per PROJECT.md §3's "clearly TODO(photo)-tagged" rule). But `single-industry.php` and `archive-industry.php` were discarding it: both called `wp_get_attachment_image_url()` (URL only, no alt) or `wp_get_attachment_image()` with an explicit `'alt' => $itoi_name` override, then hardcoded `alt="<?php echo esc_attr($itoi_name)"` — so every rendered `<img>` showed just the bare industry name ("Retail", "Hospitality") instead of the real, specific, already-written alt text sitting one field away in the same database. Fixed in both files: a new `$itoi_hero_alt` pulls `get_post_meta($id, '_wp_attachment_image_alt', true)` first, falling back to the industry name only if an editor ever uploads a photo without setting alt text. Verified live — `/industries/retail/` and `/industries/` both now render the real Media Library alt strings.

The same `alt=$itoi_title`/`alt=$itoi_headline` pattern existed in `single-case_study.php` (hero image + every gallery image sharing one identical alt string) and `archive-case_study.php` (tile image duplicating its own visible caption directly beneath it) — currently **dead code** in practice (no case study has a `hero_image` or `gallery` set yet, confirmed via ACF query), but fixed anyway since it's a one-line-per-file change and would otherwise silently reproduce today's industry bug the moment real case-study photography is uploaded. Case study fix also de-duplicates gallery alt text per-image (`"{title} — gallery photo {n}"` fallback) rather than stamping the identical string on every photo in a gallery. Solutions' `tile_image`/`hero_image` fields are currently all empty for all 8 posts (same "photo — {name} (TODO)" placeholder-div branch renders instead, no `<img>` at all, so no live alt-text bug there) — not touched, since there's nothing to fix yet and the placeholder text itself already names the subject.

**Heading hierarchy — checked programmatically, not by eye:** wrote a script pulling every `<h1>`–`<h6>` from ~19 live URLs (homepage, both solution/industry samples, every archive, Education Hub + sub-pages, About/Contact/Customers) and checked for exactly one H1 and no skipped levels. All clean. `single-industry.php` has two literal `<h1>` occurrences in its source (a photo-hero variant and a side-by-side fallback variant) but they're mutually exclusive behind `$itoi_use_photo_hero`, confirmed by curling all 7 live industry pages — each renders exactly one. Not a bug; flagged as checked-and-cleared rather than left ambiguous. One structural nuance worth recording: the full-screen mega menu's `#megaMenuHeadline` is an `<h2>` sitting in `header.php`, before the page's own `<h1>` in DOM order, on every page — a hidden/off-canvas nav panel, not part of the visible content outline, and axe has passed 0 violations against it repeatedly across many prior sessions. Left as-is; restructuring the global header's semantics for a marginal outline-purity gain isn't proportionate here.

**Redirects (Part 3):** traced every entry in `inc/redirects.php`'s map programmatically (no destination is itself a source key) and re-confirmed empirically with `curl -IL` on 7 sample old URLs — every one is a single 301 hop straight to its final destination, no chains. `/our-portfolio/` — flagged as an "unclear successor" back in the original Phase 9 SEO session — is no longer ambiguous now that `page-customers.php`/`/customers/` exist (added 2026-07-20, after that note was written); added the mapping. The 3 old use-case-adjacent URLs (`/door-counter/`, `/foot-traffic-counter/`, `/theft-detection-system-and-loss-prevention-solutions/`) are still deliberately **not** redirected — no `single-use_case.php` template exists, so redirecting now would send visitors into a 404 — but are no longer indexable (see below), which is the correct interim state until that template gets built.

**Duplicate content / crawlability (Part 3):** the 5 stale pre-rebuild pages above (`home`/108 is the front page itself so wasn't touched here, only its meta was; `our-portfolio`/150, `facial-recognition-demographics`/159, `door-counter`/1797, `foot-traffic-counter`/1800, `theft-detection-system-and-loss-prevention-solutions`/1803) now carry `_yoast_wpseo_meta-robots-noindex = 1`. Re-fetched `page-sitemap.xml` before and after: it dropped from including several of these (once Yoast's lazy `is_public` cache had picked them up mid-session) to exactly the 8 real, current pages (`/`, `/about/`, `/contact/`, `/customers/`, `/education/`, `/education/glossary/`, `/education/faq/`). `robots.txt` is Yoast's default virtual one — `Disallow:` (empty, blocks nothing) plus the correct sitemap reference — confirmed nothing crawlable is being wrongly blocked. Confirmed the 3 non-public CPTs (`team_member`, `client`, `glossary_term`) don't leak via direct `?post_type=` query manipulation — WP correctly falls back to the front page / 404 rather than exposing their archives.

**Internal linking:** homepage links out to every solution (8), every industry (7), the 3 published case studies, `/solutions/`, `/industries/`, `/case-studies/`, `/insights/`, `/use-cases/`, `/customers/`, `/education/`, `/about/`, `/contact/` — no orphans found among the primary nav-level pages. Education Hub landing page links to all 3 sub-pages plus, via its own teaser grid, all 15 individual guides directly (confirmed via URL count against the live page); the Guides archive itself also links all 15. No orphaned guide found. Gap, not a defect: solution and industry pages currently only link *up* to `/education/` (the hub), not down to a specific topically-relevant guide — the reverse direction already exists (each guide links back to its `related_solution`, per the Education Hub build sessions), so nothing is unreachable, but a retail-industry visitor doesn't get a direct "How Foot Traffic Analytics Improves Sales" link from `/industries/retail/` itself. Flagging as a worthwhile future addition (a small "related guides" block), not building it here — that's a template/content feature addition, past this pass's SEO-fix scope.

**Readability (Part 4) — spot-checked, not rewritten:** sampled full guide body copy (`what-is-retail-analytics`) and the Delivery Model options-page content. The Delivery Model steps (Supply/Stage/Install/Handover/Manage/SLA) are short capability-deck-style fragments, not prose — explicitly protected per PROJECT.md's "real content from the client's capability deck" note and per this task's own Part 4 instruction; confirmed present, confirmed untouched. Guide prose is generally clear and active-voice, but flagging two representative long/complex sentences for approval rather than silently editing:
- *"What is Retail Analytics?"*, opening sentence (~62 words): *"Retail analytics is the practice of turning what happens inside a physical store or venue — how many people walk in, where they go, how long they stay, and how many actually buy something — into numbers a manager can act on."* Proposed tightened version: *"Retail analytics turns what happens inside a physical store — foot traffic, dwell time, movement patterns and sales — into numbers a manager can act on."*
- Same guide, "Why it matters" paragraph (~44-word opening clause with 3 embedded questions): *"Without retail analytics, a store manager is making decisions — how many staff to roster on a Saturday afternoon, whether a window display is pulling people in, whether a quiet week was actually quiet or just badly staffed — based on gut feel."* Proposed: *"Without retail analytics, a store manager rosters staff, judges window displays and reads quiet weeks on gut feel alone."* Neither applied — both are real, previously-approved content from an earlier session, not this pass's to silently rewrite.
Did not read all 15 guides end-to-end at prose level given the scope of everything else in this pass; recommend a dedicated readability pass if a full sweep is wanted.

**Verification:** `php -l` clean on every touched file (`inc/schema.php`, `inc/redirects.php`, `single-industry.php`, `archive-industry.php`, `single-case_study.php`, `archive-case_study.php`). Every JSON-LD block re-parsed with `json.loads` after the guide-schema addition, across a solution/industry/case-study/guide/homepage sample — all valid. `debug.log` diffed before/after — no new notices beyond the same pre-existing wp-cli `post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` textdomain-timing noise this repo's prior sessions already flagged as unrelated. Did not re-run a full Lighthouse/axe sweep (out of scope for a meta/schema/redirect-focused pass — no template visual/DOM-structural change large enough to expect a score movement; the alt-text and heading fixes are pure attribute/content changes, not layout changes).

**Not done / flagged forward, matching this session's own honest-limits instruction:** on-page SEO (titles, descriptions, schema, alt text, canonicals, redirects, sitemap hygiene) is now consistent and correct across every in-scope page — but this fixes only what's inside the site's own control. It is not a ranking guarantee: domain authority, backlinks, and content depth over time are outside any single pass's reach, and specifically: the 3 case studies remain thin (zero narrative/metrics/quotes — a content-sourcing problem, not fixable by metadata), most solution/case-study photography is still `TODO(photo)` placeholder (correctly labeled as such, not fixable by an SEO pass), and guide coverage is 3 of 7 industries. See the consolidated report for the full page-by-page table.

## 2026-07-23 — All 8 solution pages: capability breakdown converted to flip-cards

**Task:** convert every solution page's "Features" checklist section into the shared `.flip-card` component (the exact one already built for the About page's "Partners, not vendors" section and the homepage solutions/industries grids — no new flip mechanic built), one page at a time, screenshotting each before moving to the next. Front/back content for every card was given exactly, to be used verbatim.

**Premise correction, checked before building anything:** the task described Sensory Intelligence as not yet existing and asked for it to be built from scratch. It already exists — published, live at `/solutions/sensory-intelligence/` (post 23990), with the exact tagline ("The store that feels everything.") and the exact intro paragraph specified in the task already present verbatim as the first paragraph of its `narrative` field, and all 5 capabilities (with the same 100x/33%/3–5x/8% stats and the same disclaimer wording given in the task) already written as narrative prose. Treated it like the other 7 pages rather than rebuilding it — see its own entry below for what specifically changed.

**Shared infrastructure built once, reused by all 8 pages:**
- New ACF repeater `capability_cards` added to the Solution field group (`acf-json/group_1e5502ad2d38.json`), sub-fields: `name`, `photo` (image, optional), `photo_placeholder_alt` (text — the TODO(photo) description, reused as real alt text once a photo is uploaded), `description`, `stat` (optional), `has_disclaimer` (true/false). Verified end-to-end via `update_field()`/`get_field()` before touching any template.
- `single-solution.php`: the old "Features" `<ul>` checklist (driven by the `features` repeater) replaced with a "Capabilities" flip-card grid driven by `capability_cards`. **`features`' own data was not deleted for any of the 8 solutions** — confirmed by count after all 8 pages were built: 14/12/5/18/7/12/11/5 rows respectively, unchanged from the counts recorded before this task started. It's simply no longer the section rendered on the page; the ACF field definition and every row stay intact and editable in wp-admin if ever needed again.
- Card markup follows the same front-photo/back-teal-900 pattern as the homepage industries grid (photo or a `(TODO)`-suffixed placeholder box using the given alt text, capability name as a real `<h3>` on the front; description + optional stat + optional disclaimer on the back, name repeated as bold non-heading text rather than a second `<h3>` so flipping doesn't duplicate a heading node). `aspect-[4/5]` per card — stress-tested against the longest realistic content (Sensory Intelligence's disclaimer cards: description + stat + full disclaimer) and confirmed it fits with no overflow at both 375px and 1440px, no clipping.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion` — a deliberate, scoped deviation from the shared component's existing default, not a change to it.** Partners-not-vendors and the homepage grids already rely on the sitewide `transition:none` rule alone: under reduced motion, hover/tap still flips the card, just instantly — acceptable there because the back face is a bonus (a link, a short trust line). Here the back face **is** the actual capability description, sometimes with a required disclaimer, so requiring a hover/tap to ever see it would hide real information from a reduced-motion user on a touch device with no hover at all. Added a `.capability-flip-card`-scoped `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` block (`src/tailwind.css`) that unstacks both faces into normal, always-visible document flow (no interaction needed) — confirmed via Puppeteer's `emulateMediaFeatures` that under reduced motion the back face computes `position: static; transform: none; visibility: visible` with no click, and confirmed visually (screenshot) that all 5 Sensory Intelligence cards render both faces stacked, disclaimer included, nothing hidden. Partners-not-vendors and the homepage grids are untouched and keep their existing (already-shipped, already-reviewed) reduced-motion behaviour.

**Per-page results (all 8, one at a time, screenshotted at 375px/1440px + one card flipped before moving to the next):**

Reduced-motion stacked layout confirmed working here specifically (the only page where it mattered — first 6 pages have no disclaimers, page 8 is text-light). |

## 2026-07-23 — Full reconciliation and bug-audit pass: code vs. ACF data vs. live site

**Task:** after the recent run of structural changes (7→8 solution restructure, content merges, all-8-solutions flip-card conversion, Delivery Model rebuilds, Use Cases hub), confirm code, ACF/WP-admin data, and the live rendered site all still agree with each other, and do a final sweep for the recurring scroll-reveal corner-bracket bug. Findings-first, fix-on-approval for anything touching content/structure — applied only the one clearly low-risk item (a documentation gap) without waiting.

### Part 1 — ACF field group audit

Enumerated all 16 `acf-json/` field groups and cross-referenced every field against PROJECT.md §4. Validated every field on a real post per CPT via `acf_get_field_groups()`/`get_field_object()` (more rigorous than eyeballing wp-admin — this is the exact resolution path the admin UI itself uses) — **every field resolved correctly, zero broken/orphaned field references found.**

**Finding 1 (fixed, low-risk, doc-only):** PROJECT.md §4 had fallen behind the actual field model in five places — `capability_cards` (added by the flip-card conversion session, never documented), the `guide`/`glossary_term` CPTs (added by the Education Hub build, never documented), the "Industry — Hero Interaction" and "Industry — Long-form Page" field groups (added across the per-industry mechanics/long-form sessions, never documented), and the "Partners, Not Vendors" / "Find Your Fit Settings" options pages (both built out-of-sequence, never documented). None of this is a code defect — every one of these fields works correctly — it's pure documentation drift. Fixed by adding dated addendum notes to §4 in the same style as this doc's existing "Superseded"/addendum convention, rather than silently rewriting the original text.

**Finding 2 (flagged, not fixed — needs a decision):** the database has accumulated **duplicate `acf-field-group` posts sharing the same key** — most notably "Industry — Hero Interaction" (`group_9f3a1c7e5d20`), which has **7 separate DB rows** (one titled "Industry — Foot Traffic Funnel", an old name from before the group was renamed), plus 2 duplicate rows each for "Solution" (`group_1e5502ad2d38`), the base "Industry" group (`group_e0fc2e98feb6`), and 3 for "Site Settings" (`group_73b8c1766c9f`). Root cause: each of the multiple "per-industry hero interaction" sessions apparently re-saved the field group as a new DB post instead of updating the existing one, rather than any problem with the JSON itself (confirmed: exactly one JSON file per key on disk, zero duplicates there). Functionally harmless right now — Local JSON is what ACF actually reads at runtime (PROJECT.md's own stated architecture: "the DB copy alone isn't version-controlled"), and every field still resolves correctly — but it clutters the "Custom Fields" admin screen with duplicate-looking entries, which cuts against the "editable by non-technical staff" success criterion (PROJECT.md §1). **Proposed fix:** delete the stale duplicate `acf-field-group` posts (keep one DB mirror per key, exactly as ACF's own resync would produce), leaving the JSON files untouched. Not applied — this touches the WP database's own structure, not just docs, so flagging for a go-ahead rather than deleting rows unasked.

**Finding 3 (confirmed clean):** the `solution` CPT's merge history is intact — exactly 8 published posts (the current 8 categories) and exactly 2 trashed (`facial-recognition`, `cleaning-robots`), matching PROJECT.md §4's documented mapping precisely. The other 5 old solutions (retail-analytics, customer-engagement, smart-security, security-robot, liquor-management) were in-place renames, not new posts, so there's nothing to find there by design. No orphaned/duplicate `solution` entries anywhere.

**Finding 4 (confirmed consistent, not a bug):** `team_member` has exactly 2 posts (Sean Kiely, Michael Stark, matching §4) but both are `draft` — however the `/team/` Page itself is *also* draft (confirmed in the earlier SEO audit session), so this is internally consistent pending-launch content, not a code/data mismatch.

### Part 2 — Template-to-data consistency

- **All 8 solution pages' flip-cards:** diffed live `capability_cards` ACF data against the original conversion task's exact spec (44 cards, all 8 pages) programmatically — **zero diffs.** Spot-checked 5 distinctive strings live in rendered HTML ("Traffic Counting", the Sensory Intelligence 100x stat, the 99.87% accuracy stat, "Liquor", "24/7 Remote Helpdesk") — all present exactly once where expected.
- **All 7 industry interactive mechanics:** tested each one's actual interaction (not just presence) — Retail's funnel slider, Hospitality's stage clicks, Banking's scenario-card click, Government's checkbox toggle, Logistics's ARIA-slider keyboard drag, Stadiums's density-marker visibility scaling (0 markers at min attendees → 50 at max, confirmed via the correct `.is-visible` toggle mechanism, not raw DOM count), Casinos's zone-selector click-through — **all 7 working correctly**, zero JS errors on any of the 7 pages. (Two of these needed a second, more careful pass after generic selectors gave misleading first results — logged as false alarms below, not real bugs.)
- **Delivery Model turnstile:** confirmed the *current* markup is the turnstile carousel (`.turnstile-card`/`.turnstile-viewport`, `initDeliveryTurnstile()`) with zero trace of either earlier mechanic (`delivery-pin-*`/`delivery-slide-*` classes, `initDeliveryModel()` — absent from both the rendered HTML and `main.js`). Re-ran the original `scripts/_turnstile-verify-temp.js` (kept from the build session) rather than trusting a hastily-written ad hoc check: auto-rotation advances after ~5s (Supply→Stage), a side-card click jumps immediately (Stage→Install), a dot click jumps directly to an arbitrary step (→Manage), reduced-motion keeps the center card static across the same 5s wait. All four confirmed exactly as last documented, no regression.
- **Use Cases hub + nav dropdown + homepage teaser:** `itoi_get_industry_use_cases()` still returns all 42 rows with zero broken solution links (every row's linked solution is a real, published post — survived the restructure correctly, same as the earlier explicit verification found). 7 rows still flagged `featured_in_nav`, matching the documented curated set. Hub page still renders all 42 cards live.
- **Homepage sections:** dumped the actual live DOM order rather than assuming — confirmed present, in order: mega-hero (5-slide slideshow incl. the RetailNext slide, confirmed via the localized slide data) → Partners-not-vendors teaser → Explore by use case → Delivery Model → One integrated platform → platform-demo widget → Why teams choose ITOI → logo marquee → reserved review-badge slot → industries carousel → solutions flip-card grid → bottom CTA. **The task's "Our Story/Roadmap section" does not exist anywhere in the codebase** (zero matches for "Our Story" or "Roadmap" across NOTES.md, PROJECT.md, or front-page.php) — flagging this as a false premise in the task rather than inventing a section to satisfy it. Every other named section is present and showing real, non-placeholder content.

### Part 3 — Scroll-reveal corner-bracket/confidence-tag: final sweep

Mapped every `itoi_reveal_class()`/`itoi_reveal_markup()` call site in the codebase (13 total, across 8 files) plus every literal `.itoi-reveal` string (confirmed no hardcoded/bypassed usage exists outside the helper functions) — every single site wraps either a section heading or a passive tile-grid link, never an in-place interactive control (slider/button/form). Then ran a DOM-level sweep across 24 representative pages (homepage, all archives, all 7 industries, sample singles) with every `.itoi-reveal` element force-set to its triggered `.is-visible` state (the worst case, not just at-rest), checking for any reveal-decorated element that is or contains an interactive control, plus a geometric check for any bracket/tag visually overlapping a real control.

**Two apparent hits, both investigated and confirmed false positives, not real bugs:**
1. Archive tile links (`archive-solution.php`/`archive-industry.php`/`archive-case_study.php`) carry `.itoi-reveal` directly on the `<a>` tag itself. This flagged as "interactive" by a naive `a[href]` check, but it's the same passive-tile-with-a-real-link pattern already reviewed and shipped elsewhere (the homepage industries carousel) — a navigational link on a content tile isn't the "interactive tool a visitor engages with immediately" category that caused every real past occurrence of this bug (sliders, in-place simulators). Not a bug.
2. A geometric overlap between reveal brackets and `.finder-opt` buttons (Find Your Fit modal) on `/solutions/`. Traced to the modal using `visibility:hidden` (not `display:none`) when closed — its content still occupies real, centered, fixed-position screen coordinates even though nothing is actually painted. Re-checked with actual computed-visibility filtering on both sides: **zero real overlaps.** A user could never see both elements at once. Not a bug.

Full-page screenshots with every reveal element forced visible (homepage, Retail industry long-form page) show no visible stray brackets or tags anywhere. **Conclusion: the bug does not recur anywhere in this sweep.** Per the task's own instruction, a site-wide disable is only warranted if the bug is found again — it wasn't, so the reveal system is left as-is, opt-in architecture unchanged.

### Part 4 — General QA sweep

- **Console errors:** zero JS errors/warnings across 16 real page templates (homepage through every archive/single type). The only console entry logged anywhere was the expected 404-status network log on a deliberately-invalid test URL.
- **debug.log:** reviewed all ~4,100 lines by unique message pattern. The only theme-code-related entries found — 6× `single-solution.php` parse error, 5× `single-industry.php` parse error, 2× each undefined `$itoi_funnel_rate`/`$itoi_funnel_value` — are all dated **17-Jul and 20-Jul**, weeks before the restructure work this audit is about. Confirmed both files currently pass `php -l` clean, and confirmed the funnel variables are now unconditionally defined near the top of `single-industry.php` with a defensive `?: 0` fallback — both issues were already resolved by the time their respective sessions finished; nothing to fix now. Everything else in the log (`post-smtp`/`wordpress-seo` textdomain-timing notices, the `remove-wp-branding` plugin's null-property warnings, an `ssh2` filesystem warning, a `scandir` permission error on the pre-existing `ITOI Website V2/` subfolder) is unrelated third-party-plugin or pre-existing environment noise, already flagged as out-of-scope in multiple prior sessions' own NOTES.md entries.
- **Modals + flip-cards, click-tested (not just presence-checked):** Find Your Fit opens/steps-through/closes correctly; the platform-demo modal opens with 6 working tabs; the About page's 4 Partners-not-vendors cards flip on click; the homepage's 8 Products Row solution cards are present and match the shared component. All clean.
- **Redirect map:** re-verified programmatically (no destination is itself a source key across all 17 entries) and empirically (`curl -IL` on every mapped source URL) — every one is a single 301 hop to a real, currently-published, correctly-categorized destination (all 8 current solution slugs plus /about/, /contact/, /customers/); every final destination independently confirmed to return 200.

## 2026-07-23 — Mega-hero: per-slide background photo/video, falling back to Live Detection

**Task:** give each of the 5 hero_slides its own optional background photo/video, falling back independently to the existing plain-gradient look when empty — first built as one sitewide hero background field, then explicitly redirected mid-build to be per-slide instead ("I want each headline has its own video or photo, if empty, live detection code").

**ACF (Site Settings, `hero_slides` repeater, acf-json/group_73b8c1766c9f.json):** two new sub-fields per row — `photo` (image) and `video` (file, mp4/webm/mov) — sitting alongside the existing headline/subcopy/is_partnership/partner_name/partner_logo. (An earlier version of this same session added `hero_background_photo`/`hero_background_video` as top-level, sitewide fields instead — removed outright once the per-slide direction was given, not left dormant alongside the real fields.)

**Rendering split cleanly between PHP (first paint) and JS (every slide change), same pattern the headline/subcopy already used:**
- `front-page.php`: slide 0's photo/video render server-side as the pre-JS state (same "slide 0 is always the safe SSR fallback, never the partnership slide" reasoning already established for the headline). `#heroBg` keeps its two alternate background-color classes (plain gradient vs. `#0a1720` solid, swapped depending on whether slide 0 has media) and now contains two always-present, initially-toggled elements — `#heroBgVideo` and `#heroBgPhoto` — instead of one conditionally-printed tag. Both sit *inside* `#heroBg`, ahead of where `initHeroDetectionBoxes()` (main.js) appends its 5 marker divs, so the Live Detection markers keep drawing on top of whatever background is active, unconditionally — the signature layer was never made conditional on this feature (CLAUDE.md's hard rule protected explicitly, not just by accident of ordering).
- `inc/enqueue.php`: each localized `itoiHeroSlides` row gained `photo`/`photoAlt`/`video` alongside the existing headline/subcopy/partner fields.
- `assets/js/main.js`: new `updateHeroBackground(slide)`, called from `goToHeroSlide()` (so it fires on auto-advance, dot click, and initial load alike, exactly like the headline/subcopy update it sits next to) — points `#heroBgVideo`'s `<source>` at the new slide's video (skipping `video.load()` if the URL is unchanged, so slide 0's own server-rendered video isn't needlessly restarted on JS init), or swaps `#heroBgPhoto`'s `src`, or hides both and lets the gradient show through when the slide has neither. Reduced motion: video is explicitly `.pause()`d rather than left to the `autoplay` attribute (which the browser has already acted on by the time JS runs) — same `reduceMotion` flag `initHeroDetectionBoxes()` already checks for the marker-drift animation, extended to cover this too.

**Verified against the real thing, not just synthetic test data:** while this was mid-build, a real photo (device/hardware components, attachment 24025, uploaded 2026-07-23 06:15 UTC — a genuine Media Library upload, not anything this session's own scripts created) landed on Hero Slide 1's new "Background photo" field, live. Rather than treat that as noise to clean up, used it as the real verification case: confirmed live on the homepage — slide 1 shows the real photo with the scrim + Live Detection markers still drawing on top, text still fully legible, at both 1440px and 375px. Confirmed slide switching (dot click) correctly toggles between slide 1's real photo and slide 2's plain-gradient fallback (which has no photo/video set) and back, with zero console errors across a 6-second auto-advance window spanning several slide transitions.

**Verification:** `php -l` clean on `front-page.php`/`inc/enqueue.php`; `node -c` clean on `main.js`; `npm run build` clean. Cleaned up genuinely-orphaned `wp_options` rows (`options_hero_background_photo`/`_options_hero_background_photo`/`options_hero_background_video`/`_options_hero_background_video`) left over from this session's own now-reverted sitewide-field prototype — the real per-slide data (attachment 24025 on slide 1) was untouched. `debug.log` clean before/after.

## 2026-07-24 — Temporary stock photography + hero video: filled every remaining TODO(photo) placeholder site-wide for stakeholder presentation

**Scope, per explicit user instruction:** a TEMPORARY, presentation-only pass to source free, properly-licensed stock photos and one stock video for every current `TODO(photo)` placeholder site-wide, so the site is presentable to stakeholders before real ITOI photography/video exists. Explicitly does **not** change PROJECT.md's underlying real-vs-stock photo policy — every asset added is flagged both in the ACF field's own `instructions` text (admin-only) and, for case studies specifically, with a caption visible on the live page. Covered: all 8 solution pages' capability-card (flip-card) photos, all 7 industry pages' Use Cases grid photos, all 3 case studies' hero photos, and one homepage mega-hero background video. Explicitly out of scope and untouched: the platform-demo section's real dashboard screenshot; any text content, stats, or client facts; the industry-page "capability illustration" repeater (a separate field whose own instructions say "not stock — screenshot or illustration only," confirmed unchanged); solution pages' `hero_image`/`tile_image` (outside this task's explicit "flip-card photos" scope, still TODO by design).

**Brand-safety methodology — visual review, not description matching:** early in this pass, screenshotting the actual rendered pages (not just reading each candidate photo's AI-generated text description before download) surfaced several images with real, readable third-party branding that the description search had missed entirely — e.g. a shirt reading "SECURITE CIRCUIT," a "THE SUN GUARD SECURITY" sign, "HOTEL CARMEL" signage, and about a dozen others across the solution/industry photo sets (ShopRite, STARSHIP, CAIN'S DOGS, Haribo, TP-Link, vagaro, and more). Consistent with this project's own established precedent (2026-07-20 entry above), every one of those was individually replaced with a freshly-sourced, visually brand-clean alternative and the branded original deleted from the Media Library — confirmed via a second screenshot pass, not just re-reading the new photo's description. The same screening caught two case-study photos that needed replacing for a related but distinct reason (below).

**Case studies — extra honesty safeguard (Part 2):** the 3 case-study hero photos are stock photos generically representative of that client's industry, never presented as the client's literal site. Two of the three initial candidates failed a visual review after upload and were replaced:
- Brisbane City Council's first candidate turned out, on inspection, to be the actual European Parliament chamber in Strasbourg (EU flag and member-state flags clearly visible) — a specific, globally-identifiable real institution, which is worse than generic branding for a "representative, not real" image. A second attempt (Australia's own Parliament House, Canberra) was also rejected for the same reason — still a specific identifiable real government building, just the wrong one. Replaced with a generic, brandless empty conference room (attachment #24134).
- Macquarie Bank's first candidate had a legible lit sign reading "Aion Tower" on the lobby wall — a named building. Replaced with a generic marble corporate lobby with no signage (attachment #24135).

Both replacements, plus Drakes Supermarkets' original candidate, were re-verified via screenshot; the only readable text on any of the three final images is "Xarcuteria" (Catalan for "delicatessen," a generic supermarket department label, not a brand) in the Drakes photo's background.

**Case studies — visible on-page disclaimer, not just an admin note (Part 2):** added a new ACF field `hero_image_is_stock` (true/false, `acf-json/group_1436e8de4e5f.json`) on the Case Study field group, set `true` on all 3 current case studies. `single-case_study.php` now renders a semi-transparent dark caption bar across the bottom of the hero image — "Representative image — not an actual photo of [Client]'s site" — whenever this flag is checked; confirmed legible at both 1440px and 375px (wraps to two lines on mobile without clipping). Once a case study's `hero_image` is swapped for a real photo of that client's site, unchecking this one field removes the disclaimer — no template edit needed.

**Homepage mega-hero background video (Part 3):** sourced one 20-second, muted, looping, 2560×1440/30fps abstract data-visualization clip (dark background, animated analytics charts/graphs — no people, no branding) from Pexels, wired into `hero_slides[0]` (the "Proactive retail & security intelligence, powered by AI" slide) via the per-slide `photo`/`video` fields built in the 2026-07-23 session above. The slide's existing `photo` value (attachment #24025, an unlabeled device-teardown render carrying a visible "HIGGSFIELD AI" watermark — see the 2026-07-23 entry above, which noted it as a genuine but non-final Media Library upload used opportunistically to verify the new per-slide feature) was replaced with a proper poster/fallback frame captured from the new video itself, since it both mismatched the new video's content and carried third-party AI-tool branding that fails this project's own brand-safety bar regardless of who uploaded it; the old attachment was confirmed to have zero other references before deletion. Screenshotted with the video actively playing at both breakpoints: the dark, low-contrast footage sits comfortably behind the white headline text and the always-on-top Live Detection corner-bracket markers with no legibility or wash-out issues, so no additional scrim/opacity adjustment was needed. Confirmed the existing per-slide fallback behavior still works correctly — the slideshow auto-advances every 5s, and slides 1-4 (which have no photo/video set) correctly pause the video, hide it, and fall back to the plain gradient, exactly as designed in the 2026-07-23 build.

**ACF field-level TEMPORARY notes added this session** (admin-only, shown when editing the field): `capability_cards.photo` (`acf-json/group_1e5502ad2d38.json`), `use_cases.image` (`acf-json/group_cb9a360b89a5.json`), `hero_slides.photo` and `hero_slides.video` (`acf-json/group_73b8c1766c9f.json`), and the new `hero_image_is_stock` field described above (`acf-json/group_1436e8de4e5f.json`). The industry `hero_image` field and Site Settings `why_choose_photos.photo` field already carried this note from the 2026-07-20 session and were left as-is.

**Upload mechanism:** the shell user cannot write to `wp-content/uploads/` directly (confirmed no group/other permission, no passwordless sudo) and REST API Application-Password Basic Auth was silently stripped before reaching PHP; both failed. Used WP REST API authenticated via cookie (`wp_generate_auth_cookie()`) + a freshly-fetched nonce (`admin-ajax.php?action=rest-nonce` on every call, since nonces went stale mid-session even with a valid 6h cookie) instead — this routes the actual file write through the PHP process running as `www-data`, sidestepping the shell user's permissions entirely. Reusable as `upload_stock.sh` (remote URL) / `upload_local.sh` (local file, used for the video-frame poster) in the scratchpad.
