## 2026-07-24 — Homepage Industry Selector: solutions-grid reorder, real-vs-fallback case study, use-case swap

**Task:** build a homepage Industry Selector per an exact, fully-specified brief (no inference required) — a 7-button industry picker persisted client-side, driving three downstream personalizations: the "Explore our solutions" tile grid reorders, a featured case-study spotlight swaps to the real thing (or an honest fallback), and the "Explore by use case" pill row swaps to that industry's own 6 real use cases.

**Confirmation: the exact solutions-mapping table (task Part 2) was used verbatim, not a different interpretation.** It's hardcoded as `ITOI_INDUSTRY_SOLUTIONS_MAP` in `assets/js/main.js` (`initIndustrySelector()`), copied directly from the spec with no reordering/substitution:
- Retail → intelligence-analytics, cctv-video-loss-prevention, security-access-inventory, workforce-ops-robotics
- Hospitality → customer-engagement-signage, back-of-house-integration, cctv-video-loss-prevention, workforce-ops-robotics
- Banking & Finance → security-access-inventory, cctv-video-loss-prevention, intelligence-analytics
- Government & Councils → intelligence-analytics, cctv-video-loss-prevention, security-access-inventory
- Logistics & Warehousing → cctv-video-loss-prevention, workforce-ops-robotics, intelligence-analytics
- Stadiums & Events → intelligence-analytics, security-access-inventory, cctv-video-loss-prevention
- Casinos & Gaming → cctv-video-loss-prevention, security-access-inventory, back-of-house-integration

Verified programmatically (Puppeteer, looping all 7 industries via localStorage + reload) that the live `#solutionsGrid`'s first N cards, by `data-solution-slug`, match this table exactly for every industry — not spot-checked on one or two. Remaining, non-listed solutions keep their original relative order (a stable sort on priority-index, ties broken by original position), appended after the priority set, per the spec's "not hidden, reduced visual priority" instruction — confirmed all 8 cards are always present, just reordered, with their number badges (01–08) rebuilt to match the new visual order so the reorder never reads as a bug.

**Confirmation: testimonial personalization and dashboard/platform-demo personalization were correctly skipped (task Part 5).** Neither was built — no testimonial slot, placeholder, or generic quote was added anywhere in this feature (the site has zero real testimonials, confirmed by grep — nothing new here), and `template-parts/platform-demo-modal.php` / the platform-demo section were not touched at all; the demo stays one generic experience regardless of industry selection, exactly as instructed.

**Placement (Part 1):** the new `#industrySelector` section sits directly after `#megaHero`'s closing `</section>` and before the existing "Partners, not vendors" teaser (itself before "Explore by use case") — the literal "directly after the hero" reading, since the task named that as the primary placement constraint and the teaser section already sat between the hero and the use-case section.

**Architecture — no visitor session on this WP install, so personalization is 100% client-side, but every possible state is server-rendered up front, never built from scratch by JS:**
- `front-page.php`: renders the generic 7-button row (exact order/labels from the spec, `data-industry-slug`/`data-industry-label` on each button) AND, hidden, all 7 personalized spotlight panels (3 real case-study cards — client_name/hero_image/permalink pulled live via `get_page_by_path( $slug, OBJECT, 'case_study' )` against the 3 confirmed slugs, never hardcoded copy beyond the mapping itself — plus 4 honest fallback cards using the spec's exact copy, interpolating each industry's real `name` field) AND all 7 per-industry use-case pill rows (from `itoi_get_industry_use_cases()`, grouped by `industry_slug` in PHP — the same real 42-row dataset the `/use-cases/` hub and nav dropdown already use, per PROJECT.md §4, never invented).
- `initIndustrySelector()` (`assets/js/main.js`) only ever toggles `hidden`/reorders existing DOM nodes and reads/writes `localStorage['itoi_industry_selection']` (`{slug, label}` JSON) — it never constructs HTML strings or copy, so there's no way for the JS layer to drift from real ACF content or invent a case study/industry name.
- Fade transition: a plain ~300ms opacity cross-fade between the generic and personalized containers. No bespoke `prefers-reduced-motion` CSS override was needed — the sitewide `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *,*::before,*::after { transition: none !important } }` rule (`src/tailwind.css`, already governing every other motion-respecting component on this site) makes the opacity change instant for those users automatically; the one piece of JS that *does* branch on `reduceMotion` is skipping the matching `setTimeout` delay, so the swap itself doesn't sit through a real ~300ms pause with nothing visibly animating.
- The fallback card's "Explore our solutions →" CTA (the spec offered two acceptable link targets) smooth-scrolls to `#exploreSolutions` — the same, already-reordered grid — rather than navigating to the `/use-cases/` hub, so a stakeholder demo shows the personalization landing directly on personalized content instead of leaving the page.
- "Change" re-shows the generic button row (fade) without clearing `localStorage` — a new button click is what overwrites the stored selection; this means the solutions grid/use-case row stay in their last-personalized state while the picker is reopened, which reads as "still showing your last pick while you choose a new one," not a jarring reset-then-repersonalize.

**Data-quality note, out of scope to fix here:** the Drakes Supermarkets `case_study` post's own `industry` relationship field points at Hospitality (post #23490), not Retail — an existing data error unrelated to this feature. Not relied upon here since Part 3's mapping was given explicitly by case-study slug, not derived from that field, so this selector is unaffected; flagging in case it causes a mismatch elsewhere (e.g. any future feature that trusts that relationship).

**Verified:** `php -l` clean on `front-page.php`; `node -c` clean on `main.js`; `npm run build` clean (Tailwind picked up the new utility classes with no manual CSS needed). `debug.log` checked — no new notices/errors introduced (pre-existing plugin textdomain-timing notices only). Puppeteer-verified, not just visually inspected:
- Selector at rest (1440px + 375px) — all 7 buttons in the exact required order, wraps cleanly on mobile.
- Retail selected — real Drakes Supermarkets spotlight card, solutions grid reorders to the exact Part-2 order with renumbered badges, use-case row swaps to Retail's real 6 (Loss prevention, POS fraud prevention, Retail store analytics, Employee safety, Distribution & back-of-house security, Car park & entrance security).
- Hospitality selected — honest "Growing our footprint" fallback card (verbatim spec copy, real industry name interpolated), not a borrowed case study; solutions grid reorders to Hospitality's own Part-2 order.
- All 7 industries looped programmatically (localStorage set + reload, not just clicked) — solutions-grid order matches the mapping table exactly for every one; the 3 real-case-study industries link to `/case-studies/{slug}/`, the 4 fallback industries render the dashed-border fallback card; every industry's use-case row shows exactly 6 pills.
- Reload persistence — selecting an industry, then reloading the page (fresh navigation, not an SPA state), shows the personalized view immediately with zero re-prompt flash of the button row.
- "Change" control — re-opens the 7-button row correctly; `localStorage` is left untouched until a new selection is actually made, confirmed via readback.
- Zero console/page errors across every scenario above.

## 2026-07-24 — Solution Builder (`/solution-builder/`): rules-based recommendation engine, ROI/timeline, lead capture, print proposal

**Task:** build a dedicated Solution Builder page per an exact, fully-specified brief (no open judgment calls) — a 7-question multi-step quiz, a deterministic 8-category scoring table, an ROI formula, a timeline mapping, an architecture flow visual, lead-gated download, and a print-optimized proposal. Explicitly a rules engine, not an AI/LLM call.

**Confirmation: the exact scoring table (task Part 3) was implemented verbatim, not a different interpretation.** All weights live in `inc/solution-builder.php` (`itoi_solution_builder_calculate()`), as plain PHP arrays/conditionals mirroring the spec line-for-line — business-type +2 mapping, employees/sites/CCTV/POS/cloud weights, the 6 stacking challenge weights, and the top-3-vs-top-4 gap/closeness rule (4th < half of 3rd → stop at 3; 4th within 2 points of 3rd → show 4; hard floor of 2 / ceiling of 4). The business-type → categories mapping is the same table already verified verbatim in the 2026-07-24 "Homepage Industry Selector" entry above (`ITOI_INDUSTRY_SOLUTIONS_MAP` in `main.js`) — cross-checked identical, industry-by-industry, before writing it a second time here. Scoring/ROI/timeline are calculated exactly once, server-side, and never duplicated in JS — `assets/js/solution-builder.js` only renders whatever PHP returns over AJAX (`itoi_solution_builder_calculate` / `itoi_solution_builder_submit_lead`, both nonce-gated). The lead-capture handler recalculates from the raw submitted answers rather than trusting a client-supplied result, so what's stored/emailed can't be tampered with client-side.

**Verified with 3 distinct input combinations (VERIFICATION section), via direct AJAX calls — results genuinely differ, not near-identical:**
| Scenario | Recommended (top) | ROI total | Timeline |
|---|---|---|---|
| Small single-site retailer, existing CCTV, no theft/POS/cloud, staffing challenge | Workforce Ops & Robotics, Intelligence & Analytics, CCTV/Loss Prevention, Security/Access & Inventory (4 — 4th within 2pts of 3rd) | $18,200 | 2-4 weeks |
| Large 20+-site casino, no CCTV, POS+cloud yes, theft+compliance | CCTV/Loss Prevention, Back-of-House Integration, Security/Access & Inventory, IT & Network Infrastructure | $1,110,000 | 16-26 weeks |
| Mid-size hospitality (51-200 staff, 6-20 sites), cloud/POS no, engagement+visibility | Back-of-House Integration, Customer Engagement & Signage, IT & Network Infrastructure, Intelligence & Analytics | $455,000 | 8-16 weeks |
All three ROI totals hand-checked against the exact formula (e.g. casino: 250×2×35×52=910,000 efficiency + 25×8,000=200,000 loss-reduction = 1,110,000 — matches).

**ROI/timeline (Parts 5-6):** exact formula and mapping, both implemented verbatim; the mandatory disclaimer string is returned by PHP (not hardcoded twice) and rendered directly beneath the `$X AUD` figure at the same font-weight/visual tier — screenshotted, not just described (see below) — never in a footnote.

**Lead capture + storage (Part 7) — confirmed working, not assumed:**
- New CPT registered as `sb_lead`, not `solution_builder_lead` — the spec's literal name is 21 characters, one over WP's 20-character post-type-name limit; caught via a `register_post_type` "incorrectly called" notice in `debug.log` on first page load, fixed, confirmed the notice disappeared on reload. Not publicly routed, same "pure data" pattern as `client`/`team_member`. ACF field group `acf-json/group_e887925f09b1.json` (read-only fields, for wp-admin visibility) stores: name, email, company, phone, submitted-at, all 7 answers, and the calculated recommendation/ROI/timeline output.
- Verified by direct AJAX POST to `itoi_solution_builder_submit_lead`: a `sb_lead` post was created with every ACF field correctly populated (confirmed by querying `wp_postmeta` directly) — an initial verification query appeared to show zero meta due to a malformed shell-escaped SQL `LIKE` clause in my own test script, not an actual bug; re-queried without the filter and all 30 meta rows (15 fields × value+field-key pairs) were present and correct.
- Test lead posts created during verification (`Test Visitor`/`Acme Test Co` via curl, `Jordan Reyes`/`Reyes Retail Group` ×3 via the Puppeteer screenshot walkthrough) were deleted afterward — not left sitting in wp-admin as fake leads.

**Email notification (Part 7) — confirmed actually sending, not just attempted:** this sandbox has no local MTA (no `/usr/sbin/sendmail` binary), which would normally make `wp_mail()` fail silently — but the site already has the **Post SMTP** plugin active (pre-existing, not installed by this task) with a working outbound route and its own delivery log (`wp_post_smtp_logs`). Confirmed directly in that log: `success=1`, `solution="All good, mail sent."`, correct `to_header` (michael.stark@itoisolutions.com.au) and `original_subject` (`[Solution Builder] New lead: ...`), timestamped to match each test submission. **Caveat, disclosed rather than glossed over:** because delivery genuinely works, every verification submission (1 curl test + 3 Puppeteer walkthrough runs = 4 total) sent a real email to Michael Stark's real inbox with obviously-fake test data ("Test Visitor", "Jordan Reyes") — there was no safe way to verify end-to-end email delivery without actually triggering delivery. Worth knowing about, not something to repeat casually in future test passes on this environment.

**Architecture visual (Part 4):** horizontal flow — recommended-solution icon nodes → arrow → "ITOI Dashboard" → arrow → "Your existing systems" (only rendered when POS or cloud-based was answered "Yes", confirmed both ways in testing). Icons are hand-authored inline SVGs (`itoi_solution_builder_icon()` in PHP, mirrored in JS for client-rendered nodes) following the exact `stroke=currentColor` pattern already established in `inc/longform-icons.php` — no new icon library.

**Do-not-invent discipline (Part 8):** no client name referenced anywhere in the tool. The proposal's contact block pulls Michael Stark's role live from his real `team_member` ACF field (`role` = "Manager") rather than using this task brief's own "General Manager" phrasing, since "General Manager" doesn't appear anywhere in PROJECT.md §6 or the CMS — treated the confirmed CMS value as authoritative over the brief's wording. No phone number is shown for Michael Stark specifically, since §6 only confirms a phone for Sean Kiely and Support, not him — omitted rather than borrowing/inventing one.

**Placement (Part 1):** page built at `/solution-builder/` (own WP Page row + `page-solution-builder.php`, matched via the standard slug-based template hierarchy, not a modal). Linked from a new dedicated CTA band on the homepage (between "One integrated platform" and the traffic-demo widget, alternating `bg-hero-bg` per the section-rhythm convention) and from a new top-level Primary nav item ("Solution Builder") — both link to the real page, both confirmed rendering via curl.

**Build note:** `is_page_template( 'page-solution-builder.php' )` does **not** detect a template applied purely through the `page-{slug}.php` hierarchy fallback (no `_wp_page_template` post meta) — it only reads that meta value. The conditional script enqueue in `inc/enqueue.php` uses `is_page( 'solution-builder' )` instead. Worth remembering for any future page-specific asset that isn't set via the "Page Attributes → Template" dropdown.

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`, numbered 5-16): all 7 question steps individually, the results screen (recommendations + architecture diagram + ROI with disclaimer + timeline + lead form), the lead-success state (Download as PDF button revealed), the print-media-emulated proposal output, and a 375px mobile pass of step 1. One real bug caught this way: Chrome's print path let the fixed-position "Find Your Fit" trigger button paint through a plain `visibility:hidden` ancestor rule as a stray black pill — fixed with an explicit `display:none !important` on the known fixed-position chrome (`#finderTrigger`, `.finder-overlay`, `.mega-menu`, `.pd-overlay`) inside `@media print`, reshot to confirm clean.

**Verified:** `php -l` clean on every new/edited PHP file. `npm run build` clean. `debug.log` checked after every reload — only pre-existing plugin textdomain-timing notices, zero new notices/errors once the CPT name-length bug was fixed. Puppeteer walkthrough (`scripts/screenshot-solution-builder.js`) drives all 7 steps, the AJAX calculate call, lead submission, and print-media emulation end-to-end — not just single-page loads. Note for future screenshot scripts on this sandbox: Puppeteer's coordinate-based `.click()` (and `fullPage` screenshots immediately before one) were intermittently flaky here regardless of `waitForSelector`/visibility waits — switched to `page.evaluate()`-dispatched clicks and direct value-setting for form fields, which were reliable across repeated runs.

## 2026-07-24 — Solution Builder popup replaces Find Your Fit (same shell, new 7-question flow, hands off instead of showing results inline)

**Task:** the sitewide "Find Your Fit" popup (footer.php + `initFinder()` in main.js) is replaced entirely, not extended alongside — same modal shell/mechanic, but it now runs the Solution Builder's 7 questions and, on completion, hands off to `/solution-builder/` for results instead of resolving a case-study/solution match inline.

**Confirmation: the old flow was fully removed, not left dormant.**
- `inc/finder.php` (the `itoi_finder_get_data()` case-study/solution routing resolver) is **deleted**, and its `require` in `functions.php` removed.
- `footer.php`'s old ACF-driven 3-step loop (`fyf_step1/2/3_*`) and the entire inline results block (`#finderResultTag/Photo/Title/Desc/Cta`, `#finderRestart`) are gone from the markup — replaced with 7 hardcoded `.finder-step` blocks sourced from the exact same PHP functions the dedicated page uses (`itoi_solution_builder_industries()`/`_employee_options()`/`_site_options()`/`_challenge_options()`, `inc/solution-builder.php`) — added `itoi_solution_builder_industries()` as a new shared helper (extracted from `page-solution-builder.php`'s previously-inline industry query) specifically so the popup and the page can never drift apart on question content.
- `initFinder()` (main.js): `showResult()`, the `data.caseStudyByIndustry`/`data.solutionByNeed` routing logic, and the restart-button handler are deleted outright (not commented out/guarded). `totalQuestionSteps` is now derived from the actual step count (`steps.length`, currently 7) instead of hardcoded `3`. Added multi-select handling to the shared option-click handler (step 7, "challenges") since the old flow was single-select-only throughout.
- `acf-json/group_bc153137ebbc.json`: removed the Step 1/2/3 tabs, both routing repeaters (`fyf_case_study_routes`/`fyf_solution_routes`), and the fallback/CTA-label fields — kept only the 4 shell-copy fields (`fyf_eyebrow`/`fyf_heading`/`fyf_subheading`/`fyf_trigger_label`), with `fyf_trigger_label`'s default text left as "Find your fit" (the one piece of copy the task explicitly named as staying) and the other three updated to accurately describe the new flow (was "Three quick questions, one relevant example" / pointed at "the case study or solution closest to what you're trying to do" — both false once there are 7 questions ending in a handoff, not an inline match).
- **DB cleanup, not just code:** this options page had actually been saved once before (found via `wp_options` rows prefixed `options_fyf_*`/`_options_fyf_*`, not the `fyf_*` prefix I checked for first — an easy miss). Deleting only the field-group's stale `acf-field-group`/`acf-field` DB posts (32 rows, ID 23389 + descendants — a leftover DB copy that was silently overriding the local JSON's new defaults) wasn't sufficient: ACF's `get_field()` resolves via `acf_maybe_get_field()` → `acf_get_meta_field()`, which requires the `_options_fyf_x` reference row (mapping option name → field key) to exist even when there's no saved value to fall back to `default_value` — deleting that reference row entirely (as I did on the first pass) makes `get_field()` return an empty dummy field instead of the default. Fixed correctly via `update_field()` (not raw SQL) for the 4 surviving fields, which regenerates both rows properly. Confirmed via `acf_get_value()` vs `get_field()` returning different results before the fix, identical after.

**Confirmation: both entry paths into `/solution-builder/` work, verified via Puppeteer** (`scripts/screenshot-solution-builder-popup.js`, not just visually inspected):
- **Popup → handoff:** opened via the floating `#finderTrigger` (the other accepted path, auto-open, is unchanged — still the same 3.5s/sessionStorage-gated timer, untouched code); answered all 7 questions (retail, 11-50, 6-20 sites, no CCTV, POS yes, cloud yes, theft + outdated-security); clicking "Next" on step 7 navigated to `/solution-builder/`; results (4 recommended cards, architecture diagram, ROI, timeline, lead form) rendered **immediately** — confirmed `#sbForm` was `hidden` and no question was re-asked. Hand-checked the math: CCTV/Loss Prevention (9), Security/Access & Inventory (8), Intelligence & Analytics (4), IT & Network Infrastructure (3) — matches the scoring table for these exact answers; ROI $213,200 AUD (109,200 efficiency + 104,000 loss-reduction, sites midpoint 13 × $8,000); timeline "8-16 weeks (2-4 months)" for 6-20 sites — all correct.
- **sessionStorage cleared after use:** confirmed `sessionStorage.getItem('itoi_solution_builder_answers')` was `null` immediately after the handoff rendered (read-then-remove happens before the AJAX call, not after, so a failed calculate call can't leave stale answers behind either). Reloading that same `/solution-builder/` tab afterward showed the **standalone form** at step 1, not stale results — confirmed both that `#sbForm` was visible and `#sbResults` stayed hidden.
- **Direct visit, fresh browser context (no popup interaction at all):** navigating straight to `/solution-builder/` showed the full 7-step form starting at step 1, `#sbResults` hidden — the page is not orphaned for organic/direct traffic, per the spec's explicit requirement.

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`, numbered 17-21): popup step 1 (business type) and step 7 (challenges, multi-select) showing the new copy/questions in the existing modal chrome; the handoff landing directly on results; the reload showing the standalone form again; and a fresh-context direct visit showing the standalone form. All via the popup's real floating-trigger → 7 clicks → navigation path, not simulated.

**Not touched, confirmed by inspection:** `.finder-opt.selected`'s existing amber-on-select styling, `.finder-dot`'s done/current colors, the overlay open/close mechanics, body-scroll-lock, Escape/backdrop-click handlers, and the auto-open timer/sessionStorage key (`finderAutoShown`) — none of `src/tailwind.css`'s `.finder-*` rules were edited in this task, per the "reuse exactly, don't rebuild" instruction. Swept the rest of the theme (`grep` across every `.php`/`.js`/`.json`, excluding this file and stale `temporary-lighthouse`/`temporary-axe` audit snapshots) for any other reference to the old inline-results copy ("point you to the case study", "matching case study") — none found.

A separate close-up crop of just the trigger button was attempted but abandoned — Puppeteer's `clip` option doesn't play well with a `position: fixed` element after `window.scrollTo()` in this environment (document-relative coordinates land on unrelated content, viewport-relative coordinates hit a "0 height" clip error) — not worth chasing further since the full-viewport shot already shows the button clearly.

## 2026-07-24 — Site-wide TODO(photo) audit + follow-up stock photography pass (industry long-form visuals) + 2 pre-existing brand-safety bugs caught and fixed

**Scope, per explicit user instruction:** a full site-wide audit of every `TODO(photo)` placeholder, distinguishing genuinely empty/broken ones from ones already correctly stock-photo'd by the two earlier sessions (2026-07-20 and the same-day earlier 2026-07-24 entry above), followed by a fix pass on whatever was confirmed empty — same rules as those earlier sessions (free/commercial-license source, admin-only "TEMPORARY STOCK PHOTO" note, visible on-page disclaimer wherever a photo stands in for a specific named real client). Checked every location the user listed explicitly: 8 solution pages' flip-cards, 7 industry pages' hero + Use Cases photos, the homepage industries carousel, 3 case studies' hero + gallery, Sensory Intelligence's 5 flip-cards, the Delivery Model turnstile, Partners-not-vendors (About + homepage teaser), the Solution Builder, and Education Hub guides.

**Two pre-existing bugs found during the audit, unrelated to anything empty — both fixed the same session (see Part 2):** while pulling the 2026-07-20 session's photo-source table to build the consolidated list below, re-opened and visually re-checked all 7 `hero_image` files against the live filesystem (not just re-reading the old table's prose, which had described all 12 of that session's photos as "screened for real, identifiable branding"). Two of the seven failed that same bar on closer look:
- **Government & Councils' `hero_image` (old attachment #23699) was the actual House of Representatives chamber, Parliament House, Canberra** — a specific, identifiable, real government building (Australian flag, distinctive green-bench architecture). This is the exact category of mistake the same-day-earlier 2026-07-24 session explicitly caught and rejected twice for the Brisbane City Council case study ("Australia's own Parliament House, Canberra... still a specific identifiable real government building") — it just was never checked against this pre-existing industry photo, which predates that session.
- **Hospitality's `hero_image` (old attachment #23696)** was a real pub interior with heavy, legible third-party branding across it — BrewDog, Guinness, Weihenstephan tap/shelf labels, and a readable `facebook.com/brewdogspain` URL. The 2026-07-20 session's own log claimed this file was checked and clean; it wasn't — the branding is small but legible on close inspection, the kind of thing this project's later sessions learned to catch only via an actual screenshot-and-look pass, not a description-only check.

Both attachments fed **two places each** (their own industry page's hero, and the same photo via the homepage industries carousel, which reads the identical `hero_image` field) — fixing the field fixed both surfaces at once.

**Two more borderline candidates spotted but not touched, flagged for a decision:** Casinos & Gaming's `hero_image` shows slot machines with a repeated in-game title ("Kraken Unleashed") legible on several screens — arguably comparable to the branded-game-name rejections the sourcing agents applied to *new* photos this session, but it's small screen text on a existing live photo, lower severity than the two fixed above. Stadiums & Events' `hero_image` shows a grandstand crowd that may be a real, identifiable motorsport venue (checkered-flag patterning, grandstand style consistent with a well-known circuit) though no legible text is visible. Left both as-is pending explicit direction, rather than unilaterally expanding an already-large fix pass further.

### Part 2 — Fixes applied

**Items 14–16 (31 placeholders total: 7 `overview_visual` + 21 `overview_feature_rows[].image` + 3 `spotlight_photo`):** user was asked explicitly, since these fields' own instructions say "not stock" (real ITOI photography/screenshot/client-photo only) — a genuine conflict with CLAUDE.md's "no stock photo standing in for a real ITOI product/install" hard rule. User chose to override for this presentation pass, same as the precedent already set for `capability_cards`/`use_cases`/case-study photos, while keeping the fields fully editable so real content can replace them later. Sourced via 4 parallel research agents (one per 1-2 industries), each independently searching Unsplash/Pexels, verifying free-vs-Unsplash+/premium on the individual photo's own page (not the search-results description), downloading and visually reviewing every candidate before upload (not description-matching), and uploading through a cookie+nonce-authenticated WP REST API script (`wp-content/uploads/` isn't writable by the shell user; routes the actual file write through the `www-data` PHP process instead — same mechanism as the earlier 2026-07-24 session, rebuilt fresh this session and reused across all agents). Each agent rejected multiple candidates for legible branding (in-game slot titles, POS-brand names, beer/liquor logos, a Harley-Davidson logo), Unsplash+ premium status, or identifiable real venues/landmarks (Camp Nou, Scotiabank Arena, Bellagio, Caesars Palace, Kyiv Metro, Mandalay Bay, Château de Versailles) before landing on a clean final pick for each slot.

`spotlight_photo` (3 rows) reused the *same* attachment already vetted and live on that client's own case-study hero (Drakes Supermarkets #24131, Macquarie Bank #24135, Brisbane City Council #24134) rather than sourcing new photos — consistent messaging, and both places already carry the same "Representative image" disclaimer treatment.

**New ACF field + template change for the `spotlight_photo` disclaimer:** added `spotlight_photo_is_stock` (true/false, `acf-json/group_cb9a360b89a5.json`, mirrors `hero_image_is_stock` on the Case Study group exactly) and set it `true` on the 3 industries with a spotlight photo. `single-industry.php`'s Customers-spotlight block now renders the identical semi-transparent caption bar pattern as `single-case_study.php` — "Representative image — not an actual photo of [Client]'s site" — whenever this flag is checked. Confirmed rendering correctly at both breakpoints (caption wraps to two lines on mobile without clipping, same as the case-study precedent).

**ACF field instructions updated** on `overview_visual`, `overview_feature_rows[].image`, and `spotlight_photo` (all in `acf-json/group_cb9a360b89a5.json`) to record the override: each now says the field currently holds a TEMPORARY stock stand-in from this session, points admins to the attachment's Media Library caption for source/license, and keeps the original "real ITOI photography/screenshot only" as the long-term goal — same pattern already used on the `use_cases.image`/`capability_cards.photo` fields since the earlier 2026-07-24 session.

**Two brand-safety fixes (Government & Councils, Hospitality `hero_image`):** sourced one replacement photo each (generic empty boardroom for Government; a clean, unbranded modern hotel bar for Hospitality), re-verified free/commercial license and zero legible branding via visual review before upload, swapped the `hero_image` field on both industry posts, confirmed via `SELECT` that the old attachments had zero other references, then deleted both old files from the Media Library. Verified fixed on both surfaces each feeds (industry page hero + homepage industries carousel).

**A lazy-loading screenshot gotcha, worth recording:** the first verification pass used a puppeteer script that scrolled to a section and screenshotted only the viewport around it — this under-triggered native `loading="lazy"` images further down the same page, making correctly-populated feature-row photos appear as empty gray boxes in the screenshot even though the live DOM/DB was correct. Built a second script (`screenshot_full.js`) that scrolls the full page in steps before taking a full-page capture, which resolved it. Flagging so a future session doesn't mistake this artifact for a real rendering bug.

**Verified:** `php -l` clean on `single-industry.php`. All 28 new + 3 reused + 2 replacement attachment files confirmed present on disk (`_wp_attached_file` cross-checked against the filesystem, not just the database row). Screenshotted all 7 industry pages' Overview and Customers sections at both 1440px and 375px after forcing full lazy-load, plus the homepage industries carousel scrolled to each fixed tile — no placeholder boxes or broken images remain anywhere in scope. `debug.log` not grepped this session (no PHP errors surfaced in any screenshot pass).

## 2026-07-24 — Liquid glass treatment: CONTAINED TEST on two components only (mega-hero CTAs, Solution Builder popup card)

**Task:** a scoped, explicitly-pending-review test of a "liquid glass" (frosted, translucent, blurred-backdrop) visual treatment — not a rollout decision. Two components only: the mega-hero's "Get demo"/"Learn more" CTAs, and the Solution Builder popup's modal card background. Everything else in the theme was explicitly off-limits.

**Confirmation: values used, and where they were adjusted from the spec (with why).**

Base tokens (`:root`, `src/tailwind.css`) — used exactly as given, no changes:
```
--glass-blur: blur(20px);
--glass-bg-on-dark: rgba(255,255,255,0.14);
--glass-bg-on-dark-hover: rgba(255,255,255,0.22);
--glass-border: rgba(255,255,255,0.28);
--glass-highlight: rgba(255,255,255,0.35);
--glass-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.18);
```

Per-component overrides — added as *named tokens*, not inlined per the Part 1 instruction, since the spec itself calls for different opacities on 3 of these 3 elements:
- `--glass-bg-hero-primary: rgba(255,255,255,0.18)` ("Get demo" — spec's exact requested value)
- `--glass-bg-hero-secondary: rgba(255,255,255,0.08)` ("Learn more" — spec's exact requested value)
- `--glass-bg-modal: rgba(255,255,255,0.75)` (popup card — spec's exact requested value)
- **Adjusted, not in the spec:** `--glass-bg-hero-primary-hover: rgba(255,255,255,0.26)` and `--glass-bg-hero-secondary-hover: rgba(255,255,255,0.16)`. The spec gave hover only for the *default* token pair (0.14→0.22, a +0.08 delta) and didn't say what "Get demo"/"Learn more" should do on hover. Applied the same +0.08 delta to each button's own base opacity for consistency, rather than reusing the generic hover token (which would have made "Get demo" hover *dimmer* than its own rest state, since 0.22 < the button's 0.18 base... actually briefly considered, then rejected: 0.22 > 0.18 so it would've worked numerically, but using the *secondary* button's implied hover logic on the *primary* button read as arbitrary rather than derived — the same-delta approach is the more defensible, self-consistent choice).

**Two deliberate deviations from the literal spec text, both necessary for legibility, not cosmetic preference:**
1. **"Get demo" text color changed from dark ink to white.** The spec's Part 2 example assumed the button was still "currently solid black" — it's actually `bg-white`/dark-ink text in the current hero markup (a mismatch between the task's description and the live code; the live code was treated as ground truth, matching this project's established practice for such discrepancies). At 14-18% white opacity over the dark hero, the button reads as dark, so unchanged dark-on-dark ink text would have been illegible — this is exactly the "confirm legibility" check Part 2 required, and it failed without this change. "Learn more" was already white text, untouched.
2. **Modal card's `@supports not (backdrop-filter)` fallback is `rgba(255,255,255,0.97)`, not the spec's literal `rgba(14,17,22,0.85)` dark example.** That example value is written for the dark hero buttons (white text) — applied verbatim to the modal, it would make the modal's dark heading/question text unreadable. The hero buttons *do* use the spec's exact literal fallback value (correct there, since their text is now white per point 1 above).

**Structure:** every glass element follows the spec's exact property pattern (background/backdrop-filter/-webkit-backdrop-filter/border/box-shadow with inset highlight/transition) — implemented as three concrete classes (`.hero-cta-primary`, `.hero-cta-secondary`, `.finder-card`) rather than one shared `.glass-element` class, since each needed a different `border-radius` matching its *own* existing corner rounding (30px pill for both hero buttons, 20px for the modal card) — the spec's "don't change corner rounding" instruction ruled out one shared radius value.

**Fallback path — tested, with an honest limitation noted.** Tried disabling backdrop-filter support in headless Chromium via `--disable-features=BackdropFilter`, `CSSBackdropFilter`, and `kBackdropFilter` (all three, individually and combined) — `CSS.supports('backdrop-filter', 'blur(1px)')` kept reporting `true` regardless; only the `-webkit-`-prefixed check could be flipped to `false`. True browser-capability toggling wasn't achievable in this sandbox's Chromium build. The fallback screenshots instead force the exact `@supports` block's declarations via a higher-specificity injected stylesheet (same background values, `backdrop-filter: none`) — this faithfully shows what the fallback rule *produces*, verified against the CSS actually shipped (not a hand-typed approximation), but doesn't prove real feature-detection triggered it. Flagging this rather than claiming full devtools-toggle verification.

**Confirmed left untouched (Part 4's exclusion list) — checked by diff, not just by not having intentionally opened those files:** `git status` after this session shows exactly 3 changed files — `front-page.php` (hero CTA markup only), `src/tailwind.css` (new tokens + 3 new classes + 2 `@supports` blocks), and the compiled `assets/css/style.css`. No flip-card CSS/markup, no Delivery Model turnstile, no platform-demo modal (`.pd-*`, `template-parts/platform-demo-modal.php`), no solution/industry/case-study card templates, no nav bar (`header.php`, `.mega-menu`), no floating "Build your solution" trigger button (`#finderTrigger` itself — only the modal `.finder-card` *behind* it changed), and no section on a flat solid-color background was touched.

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`, 25-34 + two extra un-numbered-count viewport crops 27b/29b for clarity): hero at rest (1440+375), "Get demo" hover (1440+375), modal open with real 7-question form content, legible (1440+375), and the forced-fallback state for both the hero buttons and the modal card separately (1440+375 each) — 10 screenshots total, above the 8 minimum, covering both distinct fallback values (hero's dark solid vs. modal's light solid) rather than just one representative shot.

**Verified:** `php -l` clean on `front-page.php`. `npm run build` clean (Tailwind picked up the 3 new classes with no manual CSS needed). `debug.log` checked after every reload — zero new notices. The mega-hero's Live Detection markers (animated blue bounding-box elements) were visibly blurred-through the glass buttons in the "at rest" screenshot, confirming the backdrop-filter genuinely samples the live animated layer, not a static approximation.

**Note on a concurrent, unrelated NOTES.md entry found directly above this one** (industry long-form overview/feature-row/spotlight photos, "2026-07-24 (this session)"): that work was not done in this conversation — it appears another session was actively populating those TODO(photo) placeholders concurrently. Flagging only so this entry isn't misread as claiming that photo work; this session's scope was the glass test exclusively, per this conversation's explicit redirect away from photo-filling.

## 2026-07-24 — Liquid glass: approved expansion to 5 more components (nav, floating trigger, industry hero cards, platform-demo modal, industry tile pills)

**Task:** the hero-CTA + Solution Builder modal glass test (previous entry) is now approved and expanded to 5 more components. Same base tokens (`--glass-blur`, `--glass-bg-on-dark`/`-hover`, `--glass-border`, `--glass-highlight`, `--glass-shadow`) reused throughout — only 2 new component-specific tokens added (`--glass-bg-nav: rgba(255,255,255,0.65)`, `--glass-bg-dark-panel: rgba(14,17,22,0.85)`), both explicitly called for by the spec/legibility, not invented preference.

**Part 1 — dropdown-menu decision (explicitly asked for).** This theme has **no hover dropdown menus** — that's stale terminology from an earlier design phase. Since 2026-07-23 (per this file's own history and `header.php`'s own doc-comment), per-item hover dropdowns were replaced sitewide by a single full-screen `.mega-menu` overlay triggered by one "Menu" button. **Recommendation applied: leave `.mega-menu` untouched, solid dark as it already is.** Reasoning: (1) it's a full-screen takeover, not a small anchored dropdown — a fundamentally different, much higher-blast-radius component than what this test's other 4 additions are (small overlays/panels over content); (2) the first glass test's own scope rules said "if you find yourself wanting to apply this since it would look consistent, stop" — extending glass to a component never mentioned in either round's explicit approval list would be exactly that; (3) nothing in this task's actual verification/screenshot requirements asks for a mega-menu shot, reinforcing it wasn't the intended target. If a future round wants the mega-menu itself tested, it should be named explicitly like every other component here was.

**Part 2 — floating trigger.** `.trigger-glass` (new class, `footer.php`) reuses `--glass-bg-hero-primary`/`-hover` verbatim (the exact token already established for "Get demo") — no new token. Confirmed legible over both a photo-heavy section (industry hero) and a plain section (homepage mid-scroll) in the screenshots below. The dot indicator is unchanged (still `bg-signature-bright`, navy — see the first glass-test entry's note that this was never actually amber in this codebase, despite both rounds' task text calling it that).

**Part 3 — industry hero cards, all 7 industries confirmed via one shared element, not 7 edits.** Checked the actual ACF data first rather than trusting a stale in-code comment (`single-industry.php` line ~126 says "Retail + Hospitality for now, pending review before wider rollout" — that's outdated; queried `funnel_enabled`/`funnel_headline`/`hero_image` directly and all 7 industries already have this layout live). The funnel/interaction content sits in one shared `<div>` reached via an `elseif` chain keyed on industry slug, so one class change (`bg-white` → `glass-panel-light`) covers all 7 automatically — verified computed styles resolve to `rgba(255,255,255,0.75)` + `blur(20px)` on the live Retail page, and screenshotted Retail + Banking & Finance to confirm it reads correctly against two different photos.

**Structural note, worth flagging honestly:** the task describes this as "the overlapping interaction card" — that overlap pattern was deliberately removed in an earlier session (see this file's own history: "no separate overlap mechanic anymore — that was the previous 'floating card over full-bleed photo' version"). The current layout is one continuous rounded card (photo top, flush white/now-glass content bottom, square seam, no overlap). The glass treatment is applied correctly and verified via computed style, but because there's no photo directly behind this specific panel in the current layout (the photo ends where the content section begins), the blur-through visual effect is subtler here than on the hero buttons or the Solution Builder modal, where there's always non-white content behind the glass to reveal. Not a bug — a consequence of an architecture decision made before this task, applied honestly rather than silently reinstating the old overlap to make the effect more dramatic (that would be well outside this task's scope).

**Part 4 — platform-demo modal, with a real dial-back (flagging per the instruction).** Two elements touched: `.pd-modal` (outer card, now uses `.glass-panel-light` + a `--glass-border`/inset-highlight box-shadow addition) and its dark top bar (`.pd-header-glass`, new class). **The outer card's background change has no visible effect** — `.pd-modal` is a `display:flex; flex-direction:column` container whose 3 children (dark header, `#f4f5f6` tabs bar, `#fafbfb` body) stack edge-to-edge with zero gap, so its own background is 100% covered regardless of what it's set to. Documented this in the CSS comment rather than leaving it as a silent no-op. **Dial-back:** the dark top bar does NOT use the light `--glass-bg-modal` token the spec's default instruction would suggest (reuse "the identical treatment already used on the Solution Builder modal") — that token is `rgba(255,255,255,0.75)`, and this bar's title/close-button are white text. Applying it verbatim would have put white text on a near-white background, unreadable. Added `--glass-bg-dark-panel: rgba(14,17,22,0.85)` instead — same blur token, dark-tinted background, kept fairly opaque since this bar is chrome (title + close), not data. All internal tab content — stat tiles, bar charts, dot-rows, the heat-mapped data tables — confirmed untouched and fully legible in the screenshot (still `bg-white`/`border-line`, no glass).

**Part 5 — industry tile "Learn more" pills.** Found on `/industries/` (`archive-industry.php`) — **not** the homepage's Industries section, which was converted to a flip-card mechanic in an earlier session and no longer has a floating pill (the "Learn more" there is now a plain underlined link on the flip-card's back face). `archive-solution.php` has an identical floating-pill pattern but was correctly left alone — Part 5 named "industry photo tiles" specifically, not solutions. New `.pill-glass` class reuses `--glass-bg-hero-secondary` (0.08 opacity, same as "Learn more" on the hero) — text color switched from the original `text-ink` to white for the same legibility reason as "Get demo" in the first test: at 8% opacity over a photo tile's already-dark bottom gradient, dark text would be close to unreadable. Confirmed via screenshot — clearly legible white text on the frosted pill, photo texture visible through it.

**Fallback:** reused the identical `@supports not ((backdrop-filter...) or (-webkit-backdrop-filter...))` pattern on all 5 new components (not skipped on any). Re-verified end-to-end on one component (nav bar, forced via injected override CSS matching the exact fallback declaration) rather than assuming — confirmed solid opaque white, sharp (non-blurred) photo edge, fully legible, matching the mechanism already proven in the first test's entry. Not re-tested individually on the other 4 per the task's own "doesn't need to be re-tested on all 5" allowance, since it's the same shared `@supports` construct already verified twice now.

**prefers-reduced-motion:** no new rule needed — the sitewide `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { transition: none !important } }` rule (already in `src/tailwind.css`, governing every other motion-respecting component) automatically covers every `transition:` declaration added in this task, including the new hover-opacity shifts. Confirmed by inspection, not assumed: all 5 new classes' transitions are plain property-based (`background`, `box-shadow`, `transform`), the same pattern the wildcard rule already targets.

**Confirmed all 5 components completed, and scope held:** `git status` shows exactly 6 changed source files this session — `header.php`, `footer.php`, `single-industry.php`, `archive-industry.php`, `template-parts/platform-demo-modal.php`, `src/tailwind.css` (+ compiled `assets/css/style.css`). No flip-card file touched, no Delivery Model turnstile, no solution/industry tile grids on flat backgrounds, no mega-menu (see Part 1 decision above).

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`, 35-49, 16 total across both breakpoints — above the 10 minimum): nav over a photo-heavy section + over a plain section (1a/1b), floating trigger glass state (2), industry hero cards on Retail and Banking & Finance (3a/3b), the platform-demo modal open with real dashboard data (4), an industry tile's "Learn more" pill (5, plus a scrolled 41b crop since the tiles are taller than one viewport at 1440px), and the nav bar's forced-fallback state (49) confirming the shared mechanism still degrades cleanly on a new component.

**Verified:** `php -l` clean on all 5 touched PHP files. `npm run build` clean. `debug.log` checked after every reload across this session — zero new notices throughout.

## 2026-07-24 — Liquid glass wave 3: case study heroes, capability badges, disclaimer caption, hero dot-nav (RetailNext badge skipped — doesn't exist)

**Task:** wave 3, extending the approved treatment to 5 more components. No new tokens needed — everything reuses waves 1-2's tokens exactly, including wave 2's `--glass-bg-dark-panel`.

**Three of five targets didn't literally match what's in the codebase — same pattern as waves 1-2, handled the same way: verify against real code/data first, apply the closest correct thing, document exactly what didn't match and why.**

**Part 1 — case study hero "cards."** Checked `single-case_study.php` before touching anything: there is no "overlap card" here at all, and no "meta row," "Results pending" callout, or "no client quote on file" box anywhere in this theme (confirmed by grep — zero matches for any of those three phrases). The hero is a plain 2-column layout (text left, one photo tile right); when metrics/quote are empty, those *sections* (further down the page, not inside the hero) are simply omitted, never replaced with a placeholder callout. Applied the closest defensible thing instead: `.case-study-hero-glass` — a border + inset-highlight box-shadow "glass frame" around the photo tile, **no `background`/`backdrop-filter`**, because the tile is 100% covered by the photo image (all 3 case studies have `hero_image` set) — a translucent wash there would either be invisible (behind the opaque `<img>`) or, if it were somehow visible, would dim the actual client photo, working against the same paragraph's "confirm the photo still shows through" requirement. The photo *is* the content, not something a glass surface sits in front of, so a frame/edge treatment reusing the exact border/shadow/highlight tokens is what "glass" correctly means for this element. Applied identically to all 3 case studies (same template) — screenshotted all 3.

**Part 3 — "Powered by RetailNext" badge — skipped, not applied.** Grepped the entire theme: no "RetailNext" text appears anywhere as a visible badge. The only RetailNext reference anywhere is the mega-hero's slide-5 co-branding lockup (`hero_slides` ACF repeater, `is_partnership`/`partner_name` fields) — a real, already-existing, already-confirmed partnership element, but a completely different UI location (a full hero headline slide, not a small badge near the platform-demo dashboard screenshot). The "One integrated platform" teaser (the actual dashboard-screenshot element this part describes) has no badge of any kind next to it — just a browser-chrome frame, the screenshot, and a play button. Inventing a new "Powered by RetailNext" badge there would incorrectly imply that specific demo — which the platform-demo modal's own real copy labels "ITOI Platform — Live Demo (Illustrative Data)" — is RetailNext's product. That's a factual attribution error, not a scope question, so nothing was built for this part per CLAUDE.md's do-not-invent discipline. If a real "powered by" badge is wanted somewhere, the existing hero slide-5 lockup is the only place with a *confirmed* RetailNext relationship to badge.

**Part 2 — flip-card capability labels required an actual restructure, not just a class swap.** The brief describes "the label that sits on top of each flip-card's front-face photo... currently plain-text-over-photo." Checked `single-solution.php`'s `capability_cards` flip grid (the only place in the theme with this exact photo+name+flip pattern — confirmed the industry Use Cases grid does *not* use flip-cards or an over-photo label at all; it's photo-then-label-below-in-a-separate-bar, untouched, no match to convert): the capability name (`<h3>`) currently sits in a **separate white strip below the photo**, not on top of it. Restructured it onto the photo — bottom-left, absolute-positioned, `.capability-badge-glass` — matching the *already-established* "name over photo" pattern this exact theme uses elsewhere (`archive-solution.php`'s tile grid, `single-industry.php`'s Solutions section), so this isn't a new visual language, just applying an existing one to one more component. Also added a dark bottom-fade gradient behind the badge (same `after:` pseudo-element gradient those other tiles already use) specifically so the light glass badge (`--glass-bg-on-dark`, white text) stays legible regardless of how bright an individual capability photo is — confirmed necessary and working by screenshotting 3 very different photos (grayscale street scene, bright orange payment-terminal close-up, dark security dashboard) across 3 different solution pages (Intelligence & Analytics, Sensory Intelligence, CCTV/Video & Loss Prevention) — badge reads clearly on all 3. One template edit (`single-solution.php`) covers all 8 solution pages including Sensory Intelligence's 5 cards, since they all render through the same `capability_cards` field/template.

**Part 4 — disclaimer caption, the one hard non-negotiable legibility bar.** Converted `single-case_study.php`'s stock-photo disclaimer from a plain `bg-black/70` bar to `.disclaimer-glass`. **Deliberately did not shrink it to a small corner pill** — kept the full-width bottom-bar footprint (still an "unobtrusive placement" per the brief's own "or similar" allowance — it doesn't obscure the photo's main subject, just an edge strip) and layered distinctiveness on *top* of that instead: an info icon, bumped from `font-semibold` to `font-bold`, a defined top border (the glass border token, reading as a visible "seam" where the badge meets the photo), and reused `--glass-bg-dark-panel` (0.85 opacity — the same fairly-opaque dark token from wave 2's platform-demo header, not a lighter/more-transparent variant) specifically so contrast never drops below the original bar's. Fallback is even more opaque still (0.92) than the shared dark-panel fallback, on purpose — this is the one component in the whole 3-wave series where "legible, no matter what" was stated as a hard requirement rather than a general goal, so it gets the most conservative value in the system. **Confirmed via screenshot, not assumed:** the close-up crop (`53`/`61`) shows the icon + bold white text + defined border reading as a distinct, hard-to-miss callout — genuinely more attention-grabbing than the old flat dim strip was, not just differently styled.

**Part 5 — hero progress-dot nav.** `#dotNav` (`front-page.php`) was a bare positioning `<div>` with no background — dots are JS-appended (`initHeroSlideshow()`, `main.js`, untouched). Added `.dotnav-glass` (the spec's exact `rgba(255,255,255,0.08)`) plus `p-2` padding, since the container previously had zero padding and would've had no visible "glass chrome" around the dots without it. Confirmed via screenshot: the white dot-marks and the active dot's white ring-fill stay clearly visible against the glass background, over the hero's animated Live Detection layer.

**Fallback:** all 5 new classes carry the identical `@supports not (...)` pattern (not skipped on any) — re-verified by inspection that each rule compiled correctly (`npm run build` clean, no selector typos), consistent with the mechanism proven twice now in waves 1-2; not re-screenshotted on all 5 individually per the same "shared mechanism, verify once per wave" approach used in wave 2.

**prefers-reduced-motion:** no new rule needed, same reasoning as wave 2 — none of this wave's 5 additions introduce a new `transition:` property the sitewide wildcard rule doesn't already cover (Parts 1/2/4/5 add no transitions at all; nothing here has a hover-triggered opacity/background shift the way the hero CTAs or floating trigger did in earlier waves).

**Confirmed all 5 parts addressed and scope held** (4 built as specified/adapted, 1 correctly skipped with reasoning, not silently dropped): `git status` shows exactly 4 changed source files this session — `single-case_study.php`, `single-solution.php`, `front-page.php`, `src/tailwind.css` (+ compiled `assets/css/style.css`). No flip-card *body* touched (only the front-face photo label, explicitly in scope), no Delivery Model turnstile, nothing else from the running exclusion list.

## 2026-07-24 — Liquid glass wave 4: aurora background recipe + glass cards on Delivery Model, Partners-not-vendors, Why ITOI

**Task:** a two-part wave — first a shared "aurora" gradient-background recipe for 3 sections described as "currently-flat dark," then glass cards within each now that they have a rich background to show through.

**Asked the user before proceeding, unlike prior waves' solo judgment calls — this one was a real fork, not a small interpretive gap.** Checked all 3 sections first: only the per-industry "Why ITOI" grid (`single-industry.php`, `bg-teal-900`) was actually dark. The Delivery Model section (`front-page.php`) was `bg-white`, and Partners-not-vendors (`page-about.php`) had no background class at all (near-white default). The aurora recipe's colors are dark-toned radial gradients — applying them to a white section as specified would read as visible smudges, not atmospheric depth. Given this affects the visual identity of 2 sections across 2 different pages (not a small copy/label decision like earlier waves), asked rather than guessed. **User chose: convert both to a dark background first, then apply the full treatment** — so both sections now join "Why ITOI"/"Why Choose ITOI" as intentional dark-teal accent sections, extending a pattern PROJECT.md previously reserved for one homepage section.

**Confirmation: the aurora recipe is ONE shared class (`.aurora-bg`), not three implementations.** Applied via a single class name to all 3 sections' `<section>` elements (`#deliveryModel`, `#partners-not-vendors`, `#why-itoi`) — same `::before` gradient stack, same `@keyframes itoi-aurora-drift`, same `prefers-reduced-motion` override, defined once in `src/tailwind.css`.

**One correction to the recipe's literal colors, not a color invented from nothing:** the spec's middle gradient layer was `rgba(255, 138, 30, 0.12)`, labeled "signature amber" — that RGB value (#FF8A1E) is the **pre-2026-07-22 retired placeholder** signature color (see this file's own 2026-07-22 amber→navy migration entries), not the current `--signature` token (navy, #004B89). Using it as given would have reintroduced a color this project explicitly migrated away from. Substituted `rgba(90, 172, 255, 0.14)` (`--signature-bright`, the token's own dark-background variant) instead — same role in the gradient stack (a small warm-ish accent pop against the two teal layers), current brand token instead of a deprecated one. The other two layers (`--teal-700` rgb(19,96,111), `--teal-900` rgb(11,58,68)) matched the spec's literal values exactly, unchanged.

**Converting 2 sections from light to dark meant auditing every text/UI color in both, not just adding a background layer:**
- Delivery Model: eyebrow dot + text (`--signature`/`text-signature-dim` → `--signature-bright`, the dark-bg variant of the same token), `<h2>` (added `text-white`), the turnstile active card's old two-tone `--ink` header / `#fff` body collapsed into one glass surface (see below), `.turnstile-card-desc`/`-indicator` (were `var(--text-muted)`, calibrated for a white card — recalculated, see contrast section), and `.delivery-progress-dot` (was `var(--line)` rest / `var(--signature)` active — both light-mode-only tokens, recalculated).
- Partners-not-vendors: `<h2>` (added `text-white`), intro paragraph (`text-text-muted` → `text-white/80`, see contrast section). Flip-card **front** faces are untouched (still `bg-white`, opaque, own self-contained styling — Part 3's explicit instruction; also: this component has no photo, so despite the brief's framing there was no wave-3 badge here to preserve, just plain text unaffected either way).
- Why ITOI: already fully dark-token-correct before this wave (`text-white`/`text-white/70` throughout) — only needed the aurora class and a new card wrapper (see Part 4 below), bumped the one body-text instance to `/80` for consistency with the other two sections' now-corrected value.

**Part 2 — Delivery Model turnstile.** The brief said "solid brand-color background" — reality was two-tone (`--ink` header block + `#fff` body block, not one color). Collapsed both into one glass surface on the shared `.turnstile-card-full` (background/backdrop-filter/border/inset-highlight from `--glass-bg-on-dark` + established tokens), letting it show through both the header and body regions. Dimmed prev/next `.turnstile-card-compact` cards are unchanged (still their own light `--hero-bg` pill) per the explicit "stays as-is" instruction — confirmed that still reads fine as a light "ghost" card against the new dark aurora backdrop.

**Part 4 — Why ITOI, another restructure.** All 7 industries confirmed via ACF query to have exactly 6 `why_items` (matches "the 6 benefit cards"), but there was no individual card container at all before this wave — icon/h3/p sat directly on the section background, no border/padding/radius. Added one (`.why-benefit-card-glass`, `p-6`) since "convert cards to glass" needs an actual card — same "add the missing card shape, then glass it" pattern as wave 3's capability badges. One template edit covers all 7 industry pages.

**WCAG AA contrast — calculated, not eyeballed, and real failures were caught and fixed before shipping:**
Computed a worst-case effective background (teal-900 base + the aurora's brightest layer at full local strength + the glass card's own white-14% overlay on top = rgb(55,99,117)) and checked every text color against it:
| Element | Original value | Measured | Fixed to | Final |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `.turnstile-card-indicator` (12px bold) | `rgba(255,255,255,0.6)` | 3.50:1 — **FAIL** (needs 4.5:1; 12px bold doesn't qualify as WCAG "large text") | `rgba(255,255,255,0.8)` | 4.87:1 PASS |
| `.turnstile-card-desc` (16px) | `rgba(255,255,255,0.75)` | 4.50:1 — right on the line, not trusted | `rgba(255,255,255,0.8)` | 4.87:1 PASS |
| why-item body / partners intro (`text-white/70`) | 0.70 | 4.15:1 — **FAIL** | `text-white/80` | 4.87:1 PASS |
| `.delivery-progress-dot` rest (non-text, needs 3:1) | `rgba(255,255,255,0.25)` | 2.13:1 vs teal-900 — **FAIL** | `rgba(255,255,255,0.4)` | 3.29:1 PASS |
| `.delivery-progress-dot.is-current` | `var(--signature)` (navy) | poor vs. teal-900 (two similar-dark tones) | `var(--signature-bright)` | 5.16:1 PASS |
| Headings/badge/back-face text (all `#fff`) | — | 6.54:1 | unchanged | PASS |

Cross-checked the turnstile card's description text against **actual rendered pixels** (not just the theoretical calc) by screenshotting the live card and sampling background vs. text-stroke luminance directly from the PNG: measured ~4.26:1 at the 93rd-percentile (anti-aliased edge) pixels and ~6.39:1 at the solid stroke core — the core measurement, the more representative one for what a reader actually perceives, clears AA with real margin; the edge-pixel figure's small shortfall against the theoretical 4.87:1 is consistent with anti-aliasing/compression blending at a screenshot's letter edges, not a systematic problem, but flagging the measurement nuance rather than only reporting the clean number.

**Performance — a real regression was found, measured, and mitigated, not just checked and waved through.** A naive single-run scroll test looked noisy and inconclusive (aurora sections sometimes measured *faster* than a blur-free baseline, purely from inter-navigation variance in this headless sandbox). Switched to a same-page, same-session A/B method instead — toggle only the aurora `::before` on/off via an injected stylesheet, same scroll distance, same session — which is far more trustworthy: **blur(60px) on the spec's `inset(-20%)` element added ~80ms/frame over an identical zero-blur version of the same gradients**, isolating the blur filter specifically (not the gradients, not the drift animation — a static blurred layer cost almost the same as an animated one) as the actual driver. Tested reducing blur radius and element size; both helped roughly linearly. **Shipped `blur(40px)` / `inset(-10%)`** (down from the spec's literal 60px/-20%) — recovered about a third of the blur-specific overhead while keeping the same soft/layered look, plus kept `will-change: transform` on the animated layer. Same "measure a literal spec value, dial back if it costs real usability/performance" precedent as wave 2's platform-demo modal.
**Environment caveat, stated plainly rather than presented as a clean pass/fail:** this sandbox's headless Chromium scored slowly (~59ms/frame) even scrolling a section with the pseudo-element removed entirely — i.e. this environment appears to lack hardware-accelerated compositing, so the *absolute* frame-time numbers above aren't representative of real-device performance (real GPU compositing typically rasterizes a `will-change`-promoted blurred layer once and reuses it cheaply across scroll frames, which this sandbox likely can't do). The *relative* delta (blur vs. no-blur, same session) is the trustworthy signal here, and that's what the 40px/-10% mitigation responds to — but real-device QA (Lighthouse/actual browser scroll profiling) is worth doing before this ships past a "pending review" test, since a 3-section blurred-gradient page could still be meaningfully heavier than the rest of this otherwise blur-light site even after this mitigation.

**Confirmed working, not assumed, via Puppeteer:** turnstile auto-rotation still advances on its timer (index 0→1 after ~6.5s), dot-click navigation jumps to the clicked step, arrow-click navigation advances by one — all still function with the new glass center card. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` was confirmed to disable the aurora drift *specifically* (queried the `::before` pseudo-element's computed `animation-name`: `itoi-aurora-drift` in normal mode, `none` under reduced-motion) — a separate mechanism from the glass hover-transitions earlier waves already covered under the sitewide wildcard rule, checked independently per this wave's explicit ask.

**Confirmed no exclusion-list component touched:** `git status` shows exactly 4 changed source files — `front-page.php`, `page-about.php`, `single-industry.php`, `src/tailwind.css` (+ compiled `assets/css/style.css`). Flip-card *front* faces, the turnstile's dimmed side cards, and every other previously-excluded component (flip-card bodies elsewhere, solution/industry tile grids on flat backgrounds, nav, floating trigger, etc.) are untouched.

**Screenshots** (`temporary-screenshots/`, 66-75, 10 total): all 3 sections at both breakpoints (Delivery Model, Partners front + one card flipped to its glass back, Why ITOI's 6-card grid), the reduced-motion state confirming the aurora is visibly static, and a close-up crop of the turnstile's active card used for the pixel-measured contrast check.

**Verified:** `php -l` clean on all 3 touched PHP files. `npm run build` clean. `debug.log` checked after every reload — zero new notices.
